# Changelog

All notable changes to RemEx will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **The localization check gained a fourth axis, for the failure that slipped past the first three.** A value can exist in all 9 files, be genuinely translated, be current, and be referenced correctly — and still be broken from the inside, if it does not take the same arguments as its English source. That is how a literal `{0}` reached Spanish users (RemEx-xn7l): parity was intact because the key existed everywhere, staleness ignored it because it was properly translated and too short to judge, and the undefined-key axis never fired because the key was defined. The defect lived *inside* a value all three axes considered healthy. Axis 4 compares placeholders per key against English: a locale carrying an argument English does not supply is an **error** (it renders literally, or throws `FormatException` in that language alone), a locale dropping one English has is an **error** (the number or name is silently lost in that language alone), and a malformed brace is an error. **The two platforms get separate extractors, because sharing one would be wrong in both directions:** `.resx` uses .NET composite formatting where literal braces are doubled — a regex that does not consume `{{`/`}}` in pairs reads `{{0}}` as a placeholder — so that side is hand-scanned rather than regexed; Android uses printf, where the unit of comparison is the *(index, conversion)* pair, since a locale turning `%1$d` into `%1$s` changes number formatting without crashing. That last case is deliberately a **warning**, not an error, because it degrades rather than breaks. Repeating a placeholder stays legal in both syntaxes — sets are compared, not counts — so `"Delete {0}? {0} is gone"` is not flagged against a single `{0}`. One deliberate leniency is documented in the code: the printf space-flag is not accepted, because it would make the `% d` inside ordinary prose like `"100% done"` parse as a format specifier. **A detector that finds nothing is indistinguishable from a broken one, so it was proved by positive control rather than by its clean run:** seven values were injected into the real files and reverted via git — extra `{0}`, missing `{0}`, unclosed brace and extra `%1$s` were all caught, while `{{Sayfa}}`, `100% done` and `50%%` were all correctly ignored, and a `%1$d`→`%1$s` swap produced a warning rather than an error. The current tree is clean on this axis, so it was switched on with **zero** new baseline suppressions. (`scripts/check-localization.ps1`; RemEx-ozep.)
- **A localization check that catches the three separate ways a translation can go wrong, on both platforms, and a CI job that runs it.** RemEx ships 1080 PC keys and 754 Android keys across 9 languages each, and nothing verified any of it. `scripts/check-localization.ps1` checks three independent axes, because passing one says nothing about the others. **(1) Parity** — every key exists in all 9 files. **(2) Staleness** — parity only proves a key *exists*; two detectors ask whether it is still *correct*. An English-similarity pass flags values that are still mostly English words, comparing against both the current English and every historical English value recovered from git, so that a value stranded by the repo's own Host→PC find-replace — English text matching no version of the English file — is still caught. A freshness pass compares the commit that last touched each key's line in English against the same key's line in each translation; that is the only way to catch a value which is perfect prose in the target language but describes behaviour the app no longer has. **(3) Referenced-but-undefined** — a key used in code that *no* file defines. Axes 1 and 2 are structurally blind to this because parity is never broken: the key is equally absent from all nine files. This axis found 16 live defects on the PC side (`Status_PairingFailed`, `Status_InvalidHostAddress`, six `RemoteDesktop_Window*` keys and more) where `LocalizationService`'s `?? key` fallback means the user is shown the raw key name — the literal text "Status_PairingFailed" — in every language; they are filed as RemEx-qug7 rather than fixed here. Three false-positive traps are handled deliberately rather than tuned away: Polish and Ukrainian carry four plural quantity buckets where English carries two, so only the plurals *name* is required across locales; `translatable="false"` inverts the parity rule for that key; and the technical vocabulary that inflates similarity scores (`ipconfig`, `Linux`, product names) is *derived* — a token every one of the 8 translators left verbatim is by definition non-translatable — rather than kept in a hand-maintained list that would rot. Values under 40 characters are not judged for similarity at all, because cognates and loanwords ("Client Android", "Mode Palette") are indistinguishable from untranslated text at that length. The 397 findings that already existed are recorded in `scripts/localization-baseline.json` and reported as known, so the check could be switched on today and no *new* defect can be introduced; the suppressed count is always printed, the script names which baseline entries have since been fixed so the file shrinks, and `-NoBaseline` shows everything. `git blame --line-porcelain` once per file replaces the `git log -L`-per-key approach the design called for — the same answer in 18 subprocesses instead of roughly 16,000 — and the whole run takes 19 seconds. Cross-platform pwsh per the parity rule; the GitHub job checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, without which blame reports one commit per line and the freshness detector silently finds nothing. (`scripts/check-localization.ps1`, `scripts/localization-baseline.json`, `.github/workflows/localization-check.yml`; RemEx-km0i.20.)
- **Both FAQs now tell you how to stop RemEx launching itself at sign-in.** Both already said it "starts automatically at sign-in" without saying how to change that, so a user who wanted RemEx off their startup had no in-app answer and no way to check what it had installed. The new entries (PC `Faq_Q14`, Android `faq_q15`) lead with the switch rather than the plumbing: Settings → "Launch RemEx when you sign in", quoted in each locale exactly as that file ships the label, because `SettingsViewModel.IsLaunchAtLoginEnabled` → `StartupRegistrationService.SetEnabled` is what actually creates and deletes the autostart registration — pointing a non-technical user at Task Scheduler when a toggle exists would be poor guidance. Both mechanisms are still named for anyone verifying or repairing by hand, as the bead required: on Windows a Task Scheduler task literally named `RemEx` (created via `schtasks /Create /TN "RemEx"`, visible under Task Scheduler Library), on Linux an autostart entry at `~/.config/autostart/remex-agent.desktop`. Both entries give the repair step — toggle it off and back on to recreate the registration — and the Android one then closes with the consequence a phone user needs to know: the phone can only reach the PC while RemEx is running, so with autostart off it has to be started on the PC first. Each locale also names its own Settings screen as that file labels it (`Configuración`, `Налаштування`, `Ayarlar`…), matching the sibling pairing entry rather than inventing a synonym. Translated in all 9 files per platform (key parity 1044 PC / 767 Android). (`AboutViewModel.cs`, `FaqScreen.kt`, 9 resx files, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-36n4.)
- **Both FAQs now explain why RemEx needs elevated permission, and what to do when that is why the phone will not connect.** Elevation is load-bearing on Windows — a non-elevated `remex.agent` cannot read the machine-wide `cert.pfx`, so every paired phone is refused while still showing as paired — yet the words "administrator", "elevated" and "UAC" appeared nowhere in either FAQ. New entries (PC `Faq_Q13`, Android `faq_q14`) explain that the certificate is stored where nothing else can read it, that this is what stops another program impersonating the PC, and how to recover. The guidance is split per host OS, because the two are opposites: on Windows the fix is to restart RemEx elevated, while on Linux RemEx deliberately runs unprivileged with a `0600` cert in `~/.local/share/Remex`, `sudo` is the wrong move, and the cause is almost always a root-owned `/var/lib/remex/cert.pfx` left by the retired `remex-host` service — so the answer points at re-running the installer, which repairs that automatically, rather than at a raw `chown` on a path that does not exist yet in that state. Translated in all 9 languages on both platforms (key parity 765 Android / 1042 PC). (`FaqScreen.kt`, `AboutViewModel.cs`, 9 `strings.xml`, 9 resx files; RemEx-csfz.)
- **The PC FAQ now explains the "phone suddenly refuses to connect" certificate-pinning failure.** A new entry (`Faq_Q12`, mirroring Android's `faq_a13` from the PC's perspective) covers why the phone refuses after a RemEx reinstall, backup restore, or hardware move — the phone pins this PC's certificate fingerprint at pairing — and walks through the unpair-then-re-pair fix using the QR code or 6-digit PIN. Certificate pinning is the most likely hard failure a user will ever hit, and the PC previously had no user-facing explanation at all. Translated in all 9 languages (key parity 1040). Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (`AboutViewModel.cs`, 9 resx files; RemEx-a6to.)

### Security

- **The Indonesian FAQ told users to pipe a script from the wrong domain into their shell.** `Faq_Q11_Answer` in `Strings.id.resx` gave the Tailscale install step as `curl -fsSL https://scale.com/install.sh | sh` — `scale.com`, not `tailscale.com`. That is a live, unrelated third party's domain, and the instruction pipes whatever it returns straight into `sh`, aimed at a non-technical user following a help page. Corrected to `tailscale.com`, matching what the other eight locales already said; `scale.com` now appears nowhere in the repo. Found while converting that value's line-break escapes (RemEx-mqwx) and fixed inline rather than deferred, since the fix is one token inside bytes already being rewritten and leaving it meant knowingly shipping it. (`Strings.id.resx`; RemEx-mqwx.)
- **The `/ws/desktop` and `/ws/files` channels now require cryptographic proof-of-possession, not bare clientId presence (VULN-2).** These secondary channels — live screen capture + input injection into the always-elevated desktop, and bulk filesystem read/write — gated only on `IsClientPaired(clientId)`, a presence check, not authentication: a `clientId` is an unguessable-but-not-secret identifier. They now perform the same PAIR-1 HMAC-over-nonce handshake the `/ws` control channel already enforces (new shared `ChannelReconnectAuth`): the host issues a fresh 32-byte nonce and services the socket only when the client returns `HMAC-SHA256(reconnectSecret, nonce)` for the presence-checked clientId. Loopback still bypasses; legacy secret-less paired clients must re-pair. Client side: the native `RemexDesktopClient` answers the challenge synchronously on connect (before any stream-control frame, reusing the reconnect secret the `/ws` client already holds — no new JNI plumbing), and the Kotlin `/ws/files` client answers before any binary frame. **This is a coordinated change — the host, `libRemexCore.so`, and the Android APK must be deployed together, and an on-device round-trip (remote desktop + a file transfer succeed; an un-updated client is rejected) is the release gate.** Covered by 6 new host crypto tests. The 8338 external-script command channel is tracked separately (RemEx-ng5z) because its clients are external automation scripts, not the app. (`ChannelReconnectAuth.cs`, `HostBootstrapper.cs`, `remex.core/Native/RemexDesktopClient.cs`, `remex.core/Native/RemexNativeClient.cs`, `FileTransferChannelClient.kt`; RemEx-s032.2.)
- **The unauthenticated `GET /debug/logs` endpoint has been removed (VULN-1, closes the primary auth-bypass chain).** It served up to 3000 buffered in-memory log lines to any network-reachable caller — Kestrel binds `0.0.0.0:5005` with no auth or loopback gate — and those lines retained the live 6-digit pairing PIN (logged at Information) plus full paired `clientId`s in cleartext. An unpaired LAN/Tailnet peer could read the PIN during the pairing window to complete pairing, or harvest `clientId`s to feed the secondary channels. The endpoint is deleted (diagnostics read the buffer in-process via `InMemoryLogSink.GetEntries()`; there was no remote consumer). Independently: the PIN is no longer written to any log sink at all (removed from `PairingHandler` and `PairingService` — it is shown on the host screen only), and `clientId`s are now redacted to an 8-char prefix everywhere they are logged via a new shared `LogRedaction.RedactClientId` helper. (`HostBootstrapper.cs`, `PairingHandler.cs`, `PairingService.cs`, `PairedClientRegistry.cs`, `TransferSessionManager.cs`, `FileTrustService.cs`, `WindowsInteractiveSessionGuard.cs`, new `LogRedaction.cs`; RemEx-s032.1.)
- **`LaunchApp` now enforces an allowlist and rejects network paths (VULN-3).** The `LAUNCHAPP` command passed the client-supplied `TargetPath` straight to `ProcessStartInfo` after only a `Path.GetFullPath` + existence check, so a paired client could launch *any* file — including a remote `\\attacker\share\evil.exe` — as the always-elevated host (admin RCE). `AppLauncherService` now normalizes the path, rejects UNC / `\\` / `//` / mapped-network-drive targets outright (before any other check), and requires the normalized path to match a persisted launcher entry (`ILauncherStorageService.LoadEntriesAsync`) the PC's owner added themselves. Covered by 8 new tests. (`AppLauncherService.cs`; RemEx-s032.3.)
- **A shared root derived from a read-only root now inherits the parent's permissions instead of escalating to full write/delete (VULN-4).** `AddRootFromPathAsync` pinned every new root with `IsWritable/CanRename/CanMove/CanDelete = true` unconditionally, so a paired client could browse a read-only default root (Documents/Desktop/Pictures/Downloads), pick a subfolder, and re-pin it as a fully writable/deletable root — defeating the read-only designation. The derived root now inherits the parent root's capability flags (a read-only parent yields a read-only child); `CanRemoveRoot` stays true as it is a UI-management flag, not a filesystem-write grant. Covered by 2 new tests. (`FileTransferService.cs`; RemEx-s032.4.)
- **The native TLS client now fails closed when no certificate SPKI pin is supplied (VULN-5).** The SPKI-pinning `RemoteCertificateValidationCallback` was installed only `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(spkiHash))`; with an empty pin, `SslStream` fell through to the OS trust manager, which the JNI trust-manager overrides force to accept *any* certificate (full MITM). This was latent (shipping callers gate the empty-pin path) but had no fail-closed backstop. Both `RemexNativeClient` and `RemexDesktopClient` now throw before connecting when the pin is null/empty/whitespace, and the validation callback is *always* installed and rejects when no pin is configured — so an empty pin can never reach an accept-any state. NativeAOT-safe (no reflection). Covered by 10 new tests. (`remex.core/Native/RemexNativeClient.cs`, `remex.core/Native/RemexDesktopClient.cs`; RemEx-s032.5.)
- **Residual unauthenticated info-disclosure endpoints trimmed (VULN-6).** `GET /` no longer returns the full remote-desktop diagnostic report (OS, capture backend, capability/failure detail) to anonymous callers — it is now a minimal `{service, status}` handshake, and capabilities reach paired clients over `/ws` as before. The dead `GET /download/apk` endpoint (which served a hardcoded `Z:\` debug-APK path) is deleted. `GET /pairing-qr` was reviewed and exposes only public-by-design data (host id + certificate SPKI hash); no machine name is disclosed. Covered by new endpoint tests. (`HostBootstrapper.cs`; RemEx-s032.6.)

### Changed

- **The PC drag-and-drop handlers moved off Avalonia's deprecated `IDataObject` API, and the search box no longer clears itself by writing a private field.** The solution built with nine warnings, in two unrelated families. Eight were `CS0618`: Avalonia 11.3 deprecated `DragEventArgs.Data`/`DataFormats.Files` in favour of `DragEventArgs.DataTransfer` and the generic `DataFormat.File` token, so the five drop sites in the launcher and file-transfer views now read files through `TryGetFiles()` — a typed accessor rather than the old cast-in-disguise — with identical accept/reject behaviour. The ninth, `MVVMTK0034`, was not cosmetic: `FileTransferViewModel.ClearSearch()` assigned the `[ObservableProperty]` backing field `_searchQuery` directly and then hand-raised the change notification, because `ClearSearch()` is itself called *from* `OnSearchQueryChanged` and going through the property would have re-entered it and run the search teardown twice. That workaround also skipped `PropertyChanging` entirely. It is now an explicit `_isClearingSearch` re-entrancy guard: the property setter is used normally, the callback short-circuits while the clear is in flight, and the intent is documented where the next reader will look for it instead of being implied by a field write. No user-visible behaviour change on any path — the search TextBox still receives the cleared value, and whitespace-only or unsupported-root queries still reset to the folder view. Full rebuild is now warning-free in production code; the four remaining `xUnit1031` warnings are in a test file and are filed separately rather than folded into this diff. 811 tests green. (`AppLauncherView.axaml.cs`, `FileTransferView.axaml.cs`, `FileTransferViewModel.cs`; RemEx-5c37.)
- **The file-manager protocol parsing existed twice; now it exists once.** `ShareToPcViewModel` re-implemented `FileTransferViewModel`'s response parsing — two copies of the same field reads for shared roots and directory entries — now extracted into `FileManagerLogic.parseSharedRoots` / `parseFileEntries`, with each screen still mapping onto its own model. **The risk in a dedupe like this is the error handling, not the parsing:** the two callers were *not* symmetric (`FileTransferViewModel` checks `errorMessage` before parsing, `ShareToPcViewModel` never did), and that asymmetry is preserved deliberately rather than unified, because "make both do the stricter thing" is a behaviour change wearing a refactor's clothing. Whether the share screen *should* check `errorMessage` is a real question and a different bead. New tests pin the two cases most likely to drift later: a null JSON array yielding an empty list rather than throwing, and a missing boolean defaulting to false. (`ShareToPcViewModel.kt`, `FileTransferViewModel.kt`, `FileManagerLogic.kt`, `FileManagerLogicTest.kt`; RemEx-4xhj.)
- **The remote-desktop MJPEG fallback repaints frames in place instead of rebuilding the image node every frame.** The fallback path wrapped its `Image` in `key(currentFrameTimestamp)`, so every incoming frame tore down and rebuilt the entire node — layout, `graphicsLayer` transform and modifier chain — just to force a redraw. A `Canvas` at a stable call site is reused across frames and only the draw phase reads the new bitmap, using the screen's own `contentRect()` letterbox math (already documented as producing the same fitted rect `ContentScale.Fit` did) so input mapping and the cursor overlay stay pixel-aligned; the frame's content description is preserved via `semantics`. H.264 (the primary path) is untouched. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-ki0t.)
- **Launcher app icons decode off the main thread, through one helper instead of two copies.** `AppGridItem` and `RecentAppCarousel` each carried a near-identical `remember { Base64.decode + BitmapFactory.decodeByteArray }` block that ran on the main thread during first composition of every grid item. Both now call a single `rememberAppIconBitmap(iconBase64)` that decodes via `produceState` on `Dispatchers.Default`, returning null (and thus the fallback launch icon) for missing or corrupt data instead of crashing or leaving a blank tile. (`AppLauncherScreen.kt`; RemEx-f076.)
- **Cancelling a file transfer no longer does ContentResolver I/O on the caller's thread.** `FileTransferEngine.cancel()` ran its empty-download-target cleanup (a `query` + `deleteDocument`) synchronously on whatever thread invoked it — often the main thread, from a UI cancel button. The cleanup now hops onto the engine's own long-lived scope, which also survives the caller's coroutine being mid-cancellation; a `withContext` there would have silently skipped the cleanup in exactly that case. (`FileTransferEngine.kt`; RemEx-cuiq.)
- **Twelve host constructors now fail fast with a named `ArgumentNullException` instead of storing a null dependency.** Required non-nullable dependencies were assigned bare (`_logger = logger;`), so a missing DI registration surfaced later as an opaque `NullReferenceException` from wherever the field was first dereferenced — often on a background thread far from the real cause. They now go through `Guard.NotNull` per the CLAUDE.md null-safety convention, which names the offending parameter via `[CallerArgumentExpression]`. Two constructors gain a real robustness win beyond the diagnostics: `RemoteDesktopHandler` and `WindowsDisplayPowerMonitor` now validate *before* starting their long-running input-queue and message-pump threads, so a bad dependency no longer leaks a live thread. Optional nullable parameters are deliberately left unguarded — guarding them would turn an optional dependency into a hard failure, and in one case would have been fatal: `WindowsScreenCaptureService`'s `IWgcCaptureSource? wgc` is passed as literal `null` by the delegating constructor and resolved with `GetService` (not `GetRequiredService`), so guarding it would have crashed host startup on every build without the WGC project. `RemexNetworkListener`'s `certificateService`/`authenticator` are likewise left bare. The two pairing-flow sites (`PairingService`, `PairingHandler`) were done under explicit operator sign-off and are strictly constructor-assignment edits — the handshake, PIN generation and verification, HMAC computation, session lifetime, and brute-force throttle are byte-identical, verified line-by-line against HEAD by an adversarial review specifically tasked with finding a way to brick pairing. No behavior change on any valid startup path; 790 tests green including 51 pairing tests. Reviewed by Opus (PASS, both rounds). (`RemexNetworkListener.cs`, `ExternalNetworkListenerService.cs`, `MdnsAdvertisingService.cs`, `LinuxCaptureSessionLifetime.cs`, `FFmpegH264Encoder.cs`, `WindowsDisplayPowerMonitor.cs`, `DxgiDesktopCapture.cs`, `HostCapabilitiesProvider.cs`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.cs`, `PairingService.cs`, `PairingHandler.cs`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService.cs`; RemEx-2gl8, RemEx-wpfo.)
- **Bold text is now driven by named emphasized typography roles instead of 60+ ad-hoc weight overrides.** The same visual rank used to get a different weight on different screens (`titleMedium` was SemiBold in 6 places, Bold in 3, Black in 1). `typographyForFontFamily` now builds all 15 M3 `*Emphasized` roles with the app's canonical emphasis weight per role (majority-wins from the pre-migration inventory, ties resolved to SemiBold) — built alongside the base roles so they carry the user's font family and in-app scale, which `Typography.copy()` would otherwise silently drop. 63 call sites migrated to the emphasized roles; base roles are untouched, so plain text renders identically. 18 minority-weight sites shift slightly to their role's canonical weight (e.g. the Personalization section headers Black→SemiBold, WAKE PC Bold→SemiBold). Four deliberate literals remain (AC budget ≤5): two telemetry hero values and the connection orb label at display-weight Black, and one link `SpanStyle` — plus three dynamic-weight conditionals (lock-state chip, directory-vs-file rows) that can't be a single static role. Reviewed by Opus (round-2 PASS; round 1 caught three deeply-line-wrapped sites the migration script missed). (`Type.kt` + 21 screen/component files; RemEx-0855.)

### Changed (docs/tooling)
- **Build hygiene: an orphaned package pin removed and `ImplicitUsings` aligned.** `Directory.Packages.props` still pinned `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.WindowsServices`, a leftover of the removed Windows Service architecture, referenced by zero projects. And `remex.desktop` was the only one of nine projects without `ImplicitUsings` — an odd split given it compiles into `remex.agent`, so the two halves of one process disagreed. Enabling it was the item with actual risk, since implicit usings can surface `CS0104` ambiguities in an Avalonia project; none materialised. Existing explicit usings in that project are now redundant rather than wrong, which is the orphaned-using sweep's job. The `xunit.runner.visualstudio` bump the same bead asked for is split out: only the current version is in the local NuGet cache, so choosing a target needs a restore this machine cannot confirm. (`Directory.Packages.props`, `remex.desktop.csproj`; RemEx-woc0.)

- **Code comments no longer describe a Windows service, a Session-0 host, or a separate desktop client as the current design.** Seventeen sites across `remex.agent`, `remex.core` and `remex.desktop` still presented the pre-2.0 process model as live — a Session-0 command agent paired with a separate GUI host, a LocalSystem service, "the desktop client". That model has been gone since RemEx-aep; there is one elevated process in the signed-in user's session that is both host and UI, and Android is the only client. This matters beyond tidiness: CLAUDE.md exists partly because these comments have repeatedly misled contributors into designing around components that do not exist. Rationale comments kept their history rather than losing it — the reason `cert.pfx` and pairing state live in machine-wide `ProgramData` really *was* the LocalSystem service, and machine-wide storage is still deliberate, so those now read as "originally X, still correct because Y". Three of the seventeen were not comments at all but text shown to the operator, including one that told a non-technical user to *"configure the Windows service to log on as that user"*; those now say plainly that RemEx must be running in a signed-in session and normally starts on its own. The two independent copies of that message — the primary diagnostic and its fallback — were made identical so they can no longer contradict each other. Comments-only otherwise: no code, signatures or behaviour changed. Reviewed by Opus (round-2 PASS; round 1 correctly failed an incomplete sweep, catching a fixed comment whose verbatim twin was left stale and an edit that contradicted a line eight rows above it). (16 files; RemEx-k521.)

- **`AGENTS.md` rewritten lean (647 → 357 lines) with all 2.0-era history archived to `docs/OLD DOCS/AGENTS-2.0-archive.md`.** The frozen 2.0 Release Gate tables, closed-work narratives, and a duplicate machine-managed Beads block are gone from the live file; the durable do-not-regress rules embedded in those narratives (WinRT `FromAbi`/IID marshaling, `PrecisionPacer`/`DuplicationReinitThrottle` routing, `h264_nvenc_bgra`-only GPU encode, sender-scoped `OpenPipeWireRemote`, warm portal session, SurfaceView `Modifier.layout` invariant, and more) are distilled into a new "Carry-Forward Rules" section. (RemEx-79j3.)
- **`CLAUDE.md` no longer instructs agents to read per-project `AGENTS.md` files that don't exist**, points global instructions at the real `~/.claude/AGENTS.md` (there is no global `CLAUDE.md`), and marks `agy`/`agy-splitter` as external tools to verify before use. (RemEx-79j3.)

### Removed
- **A dead Android string that was filed as a translation bug turned out to need deleting, not translating.** `connection_required_context` ("Connection required to access this feature.") sat as raw English in `values-es`, `-fr`, `-hi`, `-in` and `-pt-rBR` while `pl`, `tr` and `uk` carried real translations — so the new English-similarity detector (RemEx-km0i.20) flagged it, correctly. The inference drawn from that flag was wrong: nobody has ever seen the string in any language, because it is referenced nowhere. It is the un-parameterised ancestor of `disconnected_requires_connection` ("%1$s requires a connected PC.", live in `CommonComponents.kt`), which is strictly better because it *names* the feature instead of saying "this feature" — a supersession, not a gap. Removed from all 9 files (753 → 752 strings per locale, parity exact), and the 5 similarity entries dropped out of `scripts/localization-baseline.json` (381 → 376). **This is the second time this repo has misread a dead key as an untranslated one** — RemEx-x5dx was filed as an accessibility bug for the same reason — and RemEx-a7n4 had already classified this exact key as "probably genuinely dead, safe to delete after a spot check"; this was that spot check. Two verification lessons were recorded on that bead for its remaining 31 keys: a bare-token sweep is only exhaustive if nothing resolves resources dynamically, so `getIdentifier` must be confirmed absent (it is) — `lintVitalRelease` cannot close that hole, since it proves only that nothing referenced the key *statically* — and the sweep must cover `.pro`, `.gradle`, `AndroidManifest.xml`, navigation and theme XML, not just Kotlin and layouts. The qualified `R.string.KEY` grep remains banned: ktfmt line-wrapping makes it report live keys as dead, which already caused one wrong deletion. `assembleRelease` passes including the fatal `lintVitalRelease` gate. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (9 `strings.xml`, `scripts/localization-baseline.json`; RemEx-9lsr.)
- **Four `Strings.Designer.cs` accessors outlived their resource keys.** `Settings_AccessKey`, `Settings_AccessKeyTooltip`, `Settings_AccessKeyWatermark` and `Shell_AccessKeyWatermark` were deleted from the `.resx` files earlier but their generated C# properties remained. They compiled fine and returned `null` at runtime, which surfaces as an empty label rather than an error — a trap for the next reader, who would see what looks like live API. Verified by diffing the full accessor set against the resx keys: 309 → 305 properties, zero orphans remaining, and zero references to any of the four. Doing this *before* the large orphaned-key purges (RemEx-b5kx, ~284 keys) matters, since deleting keys without regenerating this file would multiply the same trap. (RemEx-t9u8.)
- **The Connection help sheet no longer gives macOS IP-lookup instructions.** `remex.agent` ships for Windows and Linux only, so the sheet's macOS row pointed users at a platform that cannot run the PC side. This finishes what RemEx-oq6l (FAQ) and RemEx-hqcl (tutorial) started, on the last surface that still had a macOS block. The now-orphaned `connection_platform_macos` / `connection_ip_macos` keys are deliberately left in all 9 locales for the RemEx-a7n4/RemEx-3jne orphan triage, so key-set parity is untouched here. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-j1sp.)
- **Eight dead localization keys deleted, and two beads closed as stale rather than "fixed".** A parallel sweep of ten low-risk beads, grouped so no two agents touched the same file. PC: `Tooltip_GlassOpacity` / `Tooltip_GlassOpacitySlider` (RemEx-yho8) and the four pre-2.0 Access Key strings `Settings_AccessKey` / `_AccessKeyTooltip` / `_AccessKeyWatermark` / `Shell_AccessKeyWatermark` (RemEx-dk3p), each proven unreferenced by a whitespace-collapsed search across every `.cs` and `.axaml` — never by grepping the qualified `LocalizationService.Instance["Key"]` form, which line-wrapping defeats. Android: two dead pairing error strings (RemEx-fegh), where `pairing_error_generic` and `pairing_error_start_failed` survive only inside a code *comment*, and `pairing_error_bad_pin` was correctly left alone as live. Parity 1085 → **1079** on PC and 748 → **746** on Android, identical in all nine files per platform. Two beads were **skipped as stale, not forced**: `dashboard_connecting` and `cd_exit_fullscreen` are already translated in `tr` and `uk` (RemEx-omfw), and RemEx-y0dq described an Indonesian `Mulai Ulang` / `Restart` split against keys that do not exist — `values-in` uses the `Restart` loanword consistently across all three real keys. (9 resx, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-yho8, RemEx-dk3p, RemEx-fegh.)
- **Twenty unreachable Android string keys are gone, and the bead that asked for them to be translated was wrong about why.** RemEx-x5dx was filed as an accessibility bug — ten keys English in all nine locales, seven of them `cd_*` content descriptions, therefore screen-reader-only English for every non-English TalkBack user. That premise did not survive checking: **none of the ten was reachable by any user**, so there was no English for a screen reader to read out and nothing to translate. Nineteen `cd_*` keys had zero references anywhere, each superseded by a live replacement during an earlier refactor (`cd_expand_icon` by `cd_expand`, `cd_settings_fab` by `cd_settings`, `cd_kill_process` by `button_kill`, `cd_mouse_left` by `remote_mouse_left_click`), and `cd_display_picker_icon` described a control that no longer exists at all. The twentieth, `screen_qr_scanner_title`, was a third untranslated copy of "Scan QR Code"; `Screen.QrScanner` now points at `qr_scanner_title`, which is already translated in all nine, and that redirect is what lets the key be deleted at all — no user-visible text changes, because `QrScannerScreen.kt:315` already hardcoded `qr_scanner_title` in its own `TopAppBar` rather than reading the sealed-class field, so the redirect only makes an unread constructor argument agree with what the screen actually shows. Key parity 768 → 748 in every locale. Two keys referenced only by the `Screen` sealed-class constructor were translated instead of deleted, because removing them needs code surgery: `screen_splash_title` in all eight locales (each taken from that file's own `file_transfer_loading`) and `screen_tutorial_title` in five (from its own `settings_replay_tutorial`) — `es`/`in`/`pt-BR` keep "Tutorial" because that is the correct word in those languages, not an untranslated leftover, which is a third way the byte-identity heuristic misleads. The audit that proved deadness also has to be recorded, because the first version of it was wrong: **grepping raw source for `R.string.KEY` reports live keys as dead**, since ktfmt wraps long references as `R.string` + newline + `.key`. That false positive deleted `connection_scan_qr_code`, which is live at `ConnectionScreen.kt:575`; it was caught by a post-write sweep, all nine files were reverted, and the audit was redone against whitespace-normalised source. The remaining 30 unreferenced keys are deliberately **not** touched here and are triaged in RemEx-a7n4 — three are pairing-adjacent (`pairing_error_cert_mismatch`, `qr_error_invalid`, `qr_error_old_format`) where an unreferenced error string may mean that failure reaches the user unexplained, and nine more may be gaps rather than dead weight. (`NavRoutes.kt`, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-x5dx.)
- **The Android tutorial explained how to find your PC's IP address on macOS.** There is no macOS PC side — `remex.agent` ships for Windows and Linux only — so `tutorial_page4_body` was giving steps for a platform that cannot host RemEx. RemEx-oq6l had removed the same block from the FAQ; this finishes it in the tutorial, in all 9 locales, leaving the Windows and Linux blocks intact. Related but deliberately not folded in: the whole `connection_platform_*`/`connection_ip_*` picker set is orphaned (all six keys, zero references), so it belongs to the Android resources purge rather than a partial two-key deletion here. (9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-hqcl.)
- **A settings hint shipped the developer's own PC name and account.** `Settings_UsernameHint` read "Use .\Username for local accounts. GLADOS-V3\Connor will become .\Connor automatically." in all nine resx files — the developer's machine and login, in every language, as the worked example. The bead proposed a neutral placeholder; checking reachability first changed the answer, because the key has zero references anywhere and is a leftover of the removed Windows-service credentials UI. So it was deleted rather than reworded — rewording would have translated dead text into nine languages while leaving the machine name in shipped resources. A scan confirmed this key was the only carrier: one hit per PC resx before, zero after, and none in any Android `strings.xml`. Parity 1044 → 1043 with identical key sets, plus the matching accessor in the hand-maintained `Strings.Designer.cs`. (9 resx files, `Strings.Designer.cs`; RemEx-qmlq.)

- **The PC shell no longer compiles in an Android bottom nav bar, a duplicate FAB and an add-panel that could never appear.** `remex.desktop` targets `net10.0`, not `net10.0-android`, so `OperatingSystem.IsAndroid()` can never return true in that assembly — the Android client is Kotlin. Everything gated on it was unreachable: a 65-line five-tab M3 bottom navigation bar, a second gear FAB stacked above the live one, and the AppLauncher's in-place "Android Add Panel" with its three backing properties and `SubmitAndroidNewAppAsync`. The two dead behaviour branches went with them — the shell's Android-only CrossFade page transition in both `ShellViewModel` and `ShellView.axaml.cs`. About 150 lines net. The inverse gate needed care rather than deletion: `IsDesktop` is `!IsAndroid()`, i.e. *always true*, so the Border it guarded is the entire sidebar and the FAB it guarded is the live one — those kept their content and lost only the redundant condition. Verified no live entry point was lost: all nine `NavigateTo*` commands are still bound in the sidebar, and `Nav_Remote` orphaning is only because the sidebar labels that destination `Nav_Commands`. Seven resx keys are now orphaned (`AppLauncher_AddNewApp`, `AppLauncher_DisplayNameWatermark`, `AppLauncher_TargetPathWatermark`, `Btn_Submit`, `Nav_Remote`, `Nav_Launch`, `Nav_Tasks`) and are recorded on RemEx-b5kx rather than deleted here, since removing them means a parity-aware pass across all 9 files. The last three are duplicate *labels* for destinations the sidebar names differently (`Nav_Commands`, `Nav_Launcher`, `Nav_Processes`), not lost destinations. (`ShellView.axaml`, `ShellView.axaml.cs`, `ShellViewModel.cs`, `AppLauncherView.axaml`, `AppLauncherViewModel.cs`; RemEx-f167.)
- **Five dead PC strings that pointed users at a `Remex.PC.exe` the architecture no longer has.** `Service_InstallFailed`, `Service_HostNotFound` ("Remex.PC.exe not found."), `Shell_HostPathHint`, `Shell_HostPathTooltip`, and `Settings_SelectHostTitle` ("Select Remex.PC.exe") described a Windows Service install path and a *"set the path to the separate host executable"* setting — neither of which exists; there is one RemEx app on the PC that is both host and UI. The setting's backing UI is long gone (no host-path row, no file picker, no `SelectHost` command anywhere), so all five were unreachable, but any of them surfacing would have sent a non-technical user hunting for a nonexistent file. The bead named two keys; the other three were forced by its own second criterion — that `Remex.PC.exe` appear nowhere in the repo — which the two alone could not satisfy, since the string occurred in four values per file. Removed from all 9 resx files with key parity preserved (1040 → 1035, identical key sets), plus the three now-dangling accessors in the hand-maintained, non-generated `Strings.Designer.cs`. Reachability was proven, not assumed: all five runtime-constructed key families (`Splash_Style_`+id, `Activity_{Kind}`, `Faq_Q{n}_*`, `EnumLocalizerConverter`'s `{EnumTypeName}_{Value}`, and `ConfirmAsync`'s caller-supplied keys) were enumerated and ruled out — the enum-name vector needed a full enumeration of all 49 enum types in the repo, since a literal grep cannot see it. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (9 resx files, `Strings.Designer.cs`; RemEx-o8n1.)

### Fixed

- **Spanish users saw a literal "{0}" printed on the canvas selection bar.** `Canvas_SelectedLabel` was `"Seleccionado: {0}"` in `Strings.es.resx` where English is just `"selected"` with no placeholder — and the key is bound plainly (`CanvasView.axaml:437`, `Text="{local:Localize …}"`) with no `string.Format` anywhere, so nothing ever substituted it and the two characters `{0}` rendered on screen. The value was wrong in shape as well as content: lines 436–437 put `{Binding SelectionCount}` and this label in a horizontal `StackPanel`, so English composes "3 selected" and the label is a **suffix**, not the standalone sentence with its own colon that the Spanish had been written as. Now `seleccionadas`, matching the single invariant suffix word all eight other locales use. The gender is the part worth recording: review caught that `seleccionados` — the obvious masculine default — was wrong, because the things being counted are `SelectedCards` and Spanish calls them *tarjetas*, feminine. The file was already agreeing correctly elsewhere on this exact adjective (`la carpeta remota **seleccionada**` vs `el archivo remoto **seleccionado**`) and even in dependent clitics (`Arrastra tarjetas para reorganizar**las**`), so an invariant masculine would have been the one place in the file that ignored its referent. **Why this shipped at all is the more useful finding:** all three axes of the localization check are structurally blind to it — parity is intact (the key exists everywhere), the value is genuinely translated and too short for the similarity detector, and the key is defined, so the undefined-key axis never fires. The defect lives *inside* a value every axis considers healthy; confirmed by the baseline being unchanged at 369 entries before and after. A placeholder-parity axis is filed as RemEx-ozep. The two sibling defects on this same key are filed rather than folded in: pt-BR reads "3 selecionado" (singular — RemEx-z2t6) and fr reads "3 sélectionnés" where *carte* is feminine (RemEx-zupl). Reviewed by Opus (FAIL on the gender, then PASS). (`Strings.es.resx`; RemEx-xn7l.)
- **Eight Ukrainian pairing strings were still raw English, and the French pairing screen used three different words for "pairing".** On the PC pairing dialog and both Settings surfaces — the first thing a new user meets — `Btn_Pair`, `PairingDialogTitle`, `PairingDialogInstruction`, `Settings_ShowPairingPin`, `Settings_PairingPinHint`, `Settings_PairingPinTitle`, `Settings_ShowPairingPinTooltip` and `Settings_PairingPinExpiresIn` all rendered in English for Ukrainian users; they are now translated. `Status_Pairing` also said *хостом*, the pre-rename word, and now says *ПК*. On the French side `Status_Pairing` was the lone outlier at "Association avec l'hôte…" against twelve keys using *appairage*, and `PairingDialogTitle`/`PairingDialogInstruction` still said *hôte* where English says "PC" — all three now match. The Ukrainian dialog title deliberately takes the imperative (*Сполучити з ПК*) against the verbal-noun status (*Сполучення з ПК…*), mirroring English's own "Pair with PC" vs "Pairing with PC…" contrast rather than merely disambiguating them. **The bead this came from had two wrong premises, and finding that out was most of the work.** It was named for a *Conn_Pairing*-vs-*Status_Pairing* clash "on adjacent screens" — but `Conn_Pairing` has zero references in any XAML or C#, so that conflict never rendered anywhere; the key is now flagged for the resx purge (RemEx-b5kx) instead of being translated. And scoping the family by keys with "Pair" in the *name* was itself the error: the true semantic family is 39 keys — every key whose English mentions pairing — spanning three French stems (*appairage*/*associé*/*couplé*) and **four** Ukrainian ones (*сполучення*/*спарювання*/*створення пари*/*з'єднання*), reaching `Home_WelcomeIntro`, `Settings_TrustDesc`, the consent dialogs, the tutorial and the FAQ. Rewriting ~31 prose values across two languages on an agent's judgement is not appropriate, so RemEx-5xys stays open and deferred for native review, carrying the full evidence: the leading candidate is *створення пари*, which is already live in this app's own Ukrainian FAQ, is unambiguous, and avoids the concrete collision where `FileTransfer_BrowseErrorFormat` currently reads "чи **підключений** пристрій досі **сполучено**" — "check the connected device is still connected". Committing this partial fix was measured rather than assumed to be safe: 7 of the 9 touched keys were already inside those 39 and the other 2 carry no pairing stem, so the deferred sweep grows by **zero keys**. Reviewed by Opus (FAIL, then PASS after fixes). (`Strings.fr.resx`, `Strings.uk.resx`, `scripts/localization-baseline.json`; RemEx-5xys, partial.)
- **Sixteen PC status messages showed the user the raw key name — "Status_PairingFailed" — in every language.** `LocalizationService`'s indexer ends in `?? key`, so a key that exists in no `.resx` file renders as its own name. Sixteen were in that state, found by the new three-axis check (RemEx-km0i.20) on its first run. The worst are the bare ones: fail to pair and `ConnectionViewModel` set the status bar to the literal text `Status_PairingFailed`, in English, Polish and every other locale alike. `Status_InvalidHostAddress` was a milder variant — its `?? "Invalid host address format"` fallback degraded to untranslated English rather than a key name — and `RemoteDesktop_WindowControlUnavailable` was the default value of the window-control status line, so it was the *first* thing that panel ever displayed. All 16 are now defined in all 9 `.resx` files (1080 → 1096 keys per file, parity intact). **No C# changed**: every call site already asked for the right key, the keys simply did not exist. Each site was checked for reachability before writing 144 entries rather than assuming — `ConnectCommand` and `RevealPairingPinCommand` are bound in five live XAML files, and every RemoteDesktop site sits in a `[RelayCommand]` or an `[ObservableProperty]` initializer. Four of the keys are consumed by `string.Format`, where a missing `{0}` in one locale silently drops the argument for that language only and a stray `{1}` throws `FormatException` for that language only; all 36 placeholder instances were verified against English. Review caught a terminology defect worth recording: the five new French pairing strings initially followed `Status_Pairing` ("Association…"), which is itself the lone outlier in that file against seven uses of *appairage* — shipping them would have taken the wrong term from 1 occurrence to 6 and left a French user pressing **Appairer**, reading **Échec de l'association**, and seeing **PIN d'appairage** on three adjacent screens. They now use *appairage*; the pre-existing outliers in `fr` and `uk` are filed as RemEx-5xys. Indonesian standalone *Pairing* likewise became *Penyandingan*, matching `Conn_Pairing`. The 16 corresponding entries were removed from `scripts/localization-baseline.json` (397 → 381), so the ratchet tightened rather than hiding the fix. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). 811 tests green. (9 resx files, `scripts/localization-baseline.json`; RemEx-qug7.)
- **The command palette now opens on Ctrl+Shift+P, the shortcut the app has been telling users about all along.** `MainWindow.axaml` bound the palette to `Ctrl+K`, while two localized strings named `Ctrl+Shift+P` — the tutorial body ("Press Ctrl+Shift+P to jump to screens, trigger quick actions, and search the command center instantly.") and `Shell_OpenCommandPaletteTooltip` ("Open Command Palette (Ctrl+Shift+P)") — in all 9 locales. The documented shortcut did nothing and the working one was documented nowhere. Fixed by changing the binding rather than the strings: rebinding is one line, whereas rewording would have meant 2 keys × 9 files and 8 fresh translations, and would have left the app disagreeing with the more conventional shortcut. `Ctrl+Shift+P` collides with nothing else in `Window.KeyBindings` (`Ctrl+D1`–`D7`, `Ctrl+OemComma`, `Escape`, `F5`, `Ctrl+Z`, `Ctrl+Y`). `Ctrl+K` is deliberately kept as an undocumented alias so anyone who learned the old binding is not stranded by the fix. No localization files were touched, so every existing translation stays correct and key parity is unchanged. 811 tests green. (`MainWindow.axaml`; RemEx-n8nl.)
- **The Remote Desktop status text was painted over the Resolution readout in Ukrainian and Polish.** The status bar's Grid was `*,Auto,Auto,Auto,Auto,Auto`: the five `Auto` metric columns claim their full desired width — plus 32px of margin each — and column 0 absorbs only the remainder. While the metrics were hardcoded English they were always narrow; RemEx-suc9.1 localized them, and `uk` "Роздільна здатність: {0}" and `pl` "Rozdzielczość: {0}" are roughly twice that, so on a narrow window column 0 could reach **zero**. It did not merely clip: `TextBlock` does not clip itself and nothing in that Border sets `ClipToBounds`, so the status text was **drawn on top of the Resolution metric**. Column 0 now carries `MinWidth="140"` and `TextTrimming="CharacterEllipsis"`, and the four metric columns trim too, so a squeeze produces an ellipsis rather than an overlap. The bead's alternative — a `MaxWidth` on the metrics — was rejected because a column `MaxWidth` caps *unconditionally*, so the Ukrainian resolution would ellipsize even on a 3000px window; `MinWidth` binds only when space is genuinely tight (below roughly a 630px view width). Verified against Avalonia's own `Grid` source rather than by analogy with WPF: `MinWidth` **does** apply to a star-sized column — `UpdateMinSize` sits outside the `GridUnitType` switch, and the phase-5 star resolution clamps *up* to `MinSize` when nothing is left to distribute. Theme-invariant by inspection: no `DynamicResource` reference changed (85 → 85), none of the four PC themes defines a `TextBlock` or `Grid` selector, and that Border's padding is a literal rather than a themed resource, so all four reach any given regime at the same window width. What pays for the floor is recorded in the markup — beyond it the trailing metric and the streaming dot arrange off the right edge un-ellipsized, a pre-existing band whose onset this moves by 140px, filed as RemEx-em4x. (`RemoteDesktopView.axaml`; RemEx-9a46.)
- **The FAQ described port forwarding backwards, pointed at the wrong question, and the tutorial named a screen the app never labels.** Three content defects of the kind that survives review because the sentence reads fine and only the *fact* is wrong. (1) `faq_a6` said "forward a port on your router **to** your public IP" — the direction is inverted, you forward *from* the public side *to* a host on the LAN — and this is the paragraph steering users *away* from port forwarding toward Tailscale, so a garbled description undercuts the advice it exists to give. Now "open a port on your router and connect through your public IP", matching what six locales already said. (2) `faq_a6` claimed the Tailscale instructions were in "the **next** question", but `FaqScreen` builds `faqItems` in strict q1..q15 order, so it is card 6 pointing at card 11 — five cards later. The quoted question *title* is kept, since that's what actually lets a reader find it; only the false adjacency is dropped, and deliberately not replaced with "question 11" — a number breaks the moment anyone reorders the list, which is the fragility that produced the bug. (3) `tutorial_page6_body` said "From the dashboard" while the screen is titled "RemEx Home Base". **Two of the bead's premises were stale in opposite directions:** the port reversal was in *three* locales, not nine (RemEx-jc7f had already corrected six, so English was an outlier rather than the source — fixing all nine would have rewritten six correct strings), and `screen_dashboard_title` is no longer untranslated, so that defect is now per-locale and the five locales naming something their own UI never shows are filed as RemEx-9vcw. My first sweep of defect 2 also found only 6 of 9, because I matched nominative phrases and `hi`/`pl`/`uk` inflect (`w następnym **pytaniu**`, `в наступному **запитанні**`); shortest-invariant-stem matching found all nine, and a positive control confirms it — the same matcher reports **9/9 and 3 at `git HEAD`, 0 and 0 in the worktree**. Values-only; parity unchanged at 753. (9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-7gwa.)
- **The dashboard's connection menu item was labelled with a noun, so it read as somewhere to go rather than something to do.** The FAB item that calls `onToggleConnection()` borrowed `dashboard_pc_status` — "PC Status" — a key the card title at the top of the same file legitimately needs, so one string was serving two grammatical roles and only one of them was right. It now shows a verb, and the **honest** verb for the current state: `button_reconnect` when connected, `button_connect` when not. Not "Disconnect", though that was the first attempt — `RemexClientManager.toggleConnection` is **connect-only** and this client has no user-initiated disconnect at all (`_isConnected` is written by two inbound native callbacks), so a Disconnect label would have named an action the code cannot perform and then sat there unchanged tap after tap. Reconnect describes what actually happens: `connect()` runs again and re-handshakes. The missing-disconnect defect is filed as RemEx-q72d. The bead proposed a single toggle-phrased key, but any such key must be vague enough to cover both directions in nine languages — "PC connection", "Connection" — which is the same noun-ish register the bead objected to; state-dependent labelling gives two precise verbs and, because `button_connect` already existed and was translated everywhere, needs only **one** new key rather than nine vague translations. `isConnected` was already a parameter of the composable, so the state cost nothing. Each value mirrors its sibling `button_connect`'s grammatical form — the two labels alternate in the same UI slot as the state flips, so a mismatch in part of speech would read as a bug: `pl` *Połącz ponownie* against *Połącz*, `uk` *Підключитися знову* against *Підключитися*, `tr` *Yeniden bağlan* against *Bağlan*, and `in` *Hubungkan ulang* rather than a bare word because an Indonesian verb root needs an object or modifier to disambiguate. Parity 752 → 753; `dashboard_pc_status` is untouched and now has exactly one usage, the card title it was written for. (`DashboardScreen.kt`, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-j8uo.)
- **Voice control could not press the Remote Desktop keys it could see (WCAG 2.5.3).** The key pill draws **hardcoded Latin legends, identical in every locale** — `Ctrl`, `Alt`, `Shift`, `AltGr`, `Esc`, `Tab`, `Del`, `Ins`, `Home`, `End`, `PgUp`, `PgDn` — while their accessible names were localized. Wherever the translation moved away from the legend, the two disagreed and the key became unreachable by speech: a Spanish user reading "End" off the screen had nothing named End to say, because `cd_key_end` was "Fin". **The bead listed about five cases; there are 32**, and the reason for the undercount is in its own words — "all three locales checked". It sampled `en`/`tr`/`uk`, which had mostly kept English key names, so the sample looked clean while `es`, `fr` and `in` had between them translated `Shift`, `End`, `Home`, `Del` and `Esc` away from the legend — not all three for all five keys; `in` kept `Shift`, and `es` kept `Esc`. Legends were re-derived mechanically from the `Triple(...)` lists rather than trusted. The fix is **legend first, previous localized name as a parenthetical gloss** — `"PgUp (Page Up)"`, `"Shift (Mayús)"`, `"Esc (Échap)"` — which sidesteps the matching-rule question the bead demanded be settled first: a leading legend passes word-boundary matching, and word-boundary implies substring, so it conforms under either reading without betting on voice control's internals. That's why all 56 values are fixed rather than only the 32 strict failures; the other 24 pass as substrings but fail as words ("Delete" against a drawn `Del`), and a user reading "Del" will say "Del". Bare legends were rejected because dropping the gloss restores conformance by making the control *less* comprehensible to the screen-reader users this exists for; localizing the drawn legend was rejected because Turkish, Ukrainian, Hindi and Indonesian keyboards genuinely print English keycaps, so a translated legend could contradict the user's own hardware. The 7 glyph-only keys (`⌫ ↵ ⊞` and four arrows) are untouched by construction — no visible text means 2.5.3 does not apply, and `cd_key_enter` being localized is *correct*. Post-condition asserted rather than assumed: all 12 text-legend keys in all 9 locales now **start with** their legend — 108 pairs, 0 violations — a stronger and more mechanically checkable invariant than "contains", and a candidate for the localization CI work in RemEx-km0i.20. Values-only; parity unchanged at 752. (9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-qykh.)
- **TalkBack read the card-opacity slider as a bare "0.55".** No unit and no meaning — just the underlying float — while the human-readable "Card Opacity: 55%" sat in a separate, unrelated semantics node beside it — so a screen-reader user heard a number with no idea what it controlled. The slider now carries `contentDescription` (naming the control) **and** `stateDescription` (the percentage the label shows). Both are needed, and the reason is worth stating precisely because it will be copied to six more sliders: the `"0.55"` is **not** derived from `ProgressBarRangeInfo` — Material3's own `sliderSemantics` explicitly sets `stateDescription` to the formatted raw value, and this override wins only because `SliderImpl` applies its semantics *inner* to the caller's modifier, and semantics collapse walks tail→head with a plain overwrite, so the outermost wins. `progressBarRangeInfo` is deliberately left alone: it feeds the `SeekBar` role and TalkBack's adjust-gesture maths, and must keep reporting the true 0.1–1.0 range or the slider stops being adjustable. The visible label is now `clearAndSetSemantics {}` — required rather than optional, since the slider carries the same information and leaving the label announceable makes TalkBack say the percentage twice (the double-announcement problem RemEx-xqli tracks elsewhere); the text stays fully visible to sighted users. Two new keys in all 9 locales, parity 750 → 752. **The names are derived from each locale's own existing label rather than translated afresh** — a hand-written first draft disagreed with the shipped labels in three languages (`es` "Opacidad de **la** tarjeta" vs the file's "Opacidad de Tarjeta", `pt-BR` "cartão" vs "Card", `hi` an extra की), which for a screen-reader name whose whole job is to match what a sighted user reads is a silent failure that passes every automated check. `cd_percent_value` is a localized format rather than `"$pct%"` built in Kotlin, so percent typography stays a translator's decision rather than a hardcoded assumption — though it turned out every locale here writes it tight, including French, whose narrow-no-break-space rule (RemEx-19et) applies before `:` `!` `?`, **not** before `%`; a first draft put one there and was the only string in the file to do so. One of seven sliders on this screen; the other six, including corner radius fifteen lines above, are filed as RemEx-qiz5 with the site list and the pattern to copy. (`PersonalizationScreen.kt`, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-porq.)
- **The phone signed the PC out without asking, and the flag that should have stopped it was measuring the wrong thing.** `remoteCommandCards` set `requiresConfirmation` from the command's **category** — everything in `POWER` was `true`, everything in `SESSION` was `false` — and Sign Out sits in `SESSION` next to Wake and Lock, both genuinely harmless, so it inherited `false` by association. Signing out closes every open program on the PC and discards unsaved work, exactly like Shutdown. The category stays `SESSION`, because that grouping is correct; only the confirmation changed. The identical mistake existed independently on the PC (RemEx-4b8m) for the identical reason — a flag tracking a *proxy* for the property you care about eventually disagrees with it. The confirmation also carries the consequence a phone user cannot see, via a new optional per-command warning slot: the PC stays **on** but becomes unreachable, since `remex.agent` lives inside the signed-in session and is started by a per-user logon task. That matters more here than on the PC, because whoever taps this is by definition not sitting at the machine. Two short sentences rather than the PC's fuller paragraph, consequence first and the non-obvious fact second — the ordering matters because the confirm face **replaces** the card's title with a generic "Confirm choice", so the warning is the only place the action's effect is stated, and these cards sit in a two-column grid about 160dp wide on a 360dp phone where every extra line pushes Confirm and Cancel further down. **Confirming also no longer offers a timer it cannot honour:** the confirm face includes a delay field, and enabling confirmation for Sign Out exposed it for the first time — but `PingPongHandler`'s `SIGNOUT` branch never reads `CommandParameters` and `ISystemCommandService.SignOut()` takes no delay, so the PC would sign out immediately and still report success. A new `supportsDelay` flag hides the field for this command rather than silently discarding the value. Translated in all 9 locales (parity 749 → 750), each reusing that file's own term for the PC and its own second-person sign-in phrasing; `pl` says *komputer* per its convention. Styled with `bodySmall` and `onSurfaceVariant` rather than any colour literal, so it holds under monochrome and contrast 1.0. Whether Shutdown and the three restart variants deserve the same line is tracked separately (RemEx-mkq1) rather than smuggled in here. (`RemoteControlScreen.kt`, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-awks.)
- **A file transfer whose send threw left a waiter nobody would ever complete.** `UploadAsync` and `DownloadAsync` registered a `TaskCompletionSource` plus progress reporter (and, for download, a channel and hasher) before a sequence of awaited sends, with no `try/finally`. Any send that threw — including mid-way through the upload chunk loop — stranded all of them: nothing would complete the TCS, so a later awaiter of that transfer id waited forever and the dictionaries grew for the process lifetime. These two were outside the twelve sites RemEx-jxmf converted, because they aren't simple request/response, but the blast radius is larger: these are long-running and user-visible, so a stranded waiter means transfer UI that never resolves. Both now reap every dictionary in a `finally` on every exit path while still letting the exception propagate — swallowing it would turn a visible error into a silent hang, which is the bug itself. (`FileTransferClient.cs`; RemEx-w9lj.)
- **Removing a shared folder could report "Saved" while removing nothing.** `SharedRoots.Remove(item)` matched by object *identity*, so if `ReplaceSharedRoots` rebuilt the collection while the confirmation dialog sat open, it removed nothing and still told the user the folder was unshared. That's an access-**revocation** path: the phone kept access to a whole folder tree the user believed they had just revoked. The handler now re-finds by the stable `RootId`, which survives a full collection rebuild, and claims success only when something was actually removed. (`SettingsViewModel.cs`; RemEx-xqcx.)
- **Pairing's Submit button stayed enabled after a failure that had already killed the session**, so the on-screen advice to try again dead-ended — the user pressed a button that could no longer do anything. A `sessionDead` flag now folds into the button's enabled state. Deliberately scoped to UI state only: the pairing protocol, PIN verification, ECDH exchange, certificate pinning and error-code mapping are untouched, since a change there can silently brick every paired device. `Cancel` stays unconditionally enabled, so the new state can't become a dead end of its own. (`PairingScreen.kt`; RemEx-aor9.)
- **Three connect failures showed English to every user, and one showed developer text.** `RemexClientManager` emitted hardcoded strings straight into the user-facing error card, never passing through `strings.xml`: a blank init result, a blank-reason fallback, and an `UnsatisfiedLinkError` handler that displayed "Native library not linked: …" — a linker diagnostic, shown to a non-technical user in every language. All three are localized in all 9 locales now (parity 746 → 749), with the exception detail kept to `Log.e`. The one remaining raw pass-through is deliberate: `ConnectionViewModel.isCertMismatch` sniffs that message's content, so localizing it would break certificate-mismatch detection. (`RemexClientManager.kt`, 9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-hn05.)
- **The retired "host" vocabulary is gone from the Android UI, in one sweep rather than four.** There is no host/client pair in this product — one PC app, one phone client — yet fourteen keys still called the PC a "host" across `fr`/`hi`/`pl`/`tr`/`uk`, including the connection screen, the pairing and QR errors, and four Remote Desktop failure messages. `pl` uses *komputer* per that file's own convention rather than "PC". RemEx-l03l turned out to be a strict subset of this sweep and was folded in instead of done twice. Left alone on purpose: mDNS "detect running hosts", the FAQ's "background host service" and the historical `remex-host` installer name, where the current English still says host too. The sweep also caught a plainly broken translation in an adjacent key — `about_pc_host_label` read **"PC de PC"** in `es` and **"PC do PC"** in `pt-BR`. Alongside it: `hi` now uses the same *पेयर* transliteration for "paired" that the rest of that file uses 22 times (RemEx-er1x), and `about_github_star` / `tutorial_page6_body` are re-translated from the *current* English in the six locales that still carried the older wording (RemEx-eaas — `tr` and `uk` were already fixed by RemEx-c7hu.5). (9 `strings.xml`; RemEx-1cal, RemEx-l03l, RemEx-er1x, RemEx-eaas.)
- **The PC FAQ described a port fallback the host doesn't perform.** `Faq_Q5_Answer` skipped the step that actually happens first: the host tries to reclaim a port held by its own stale instance before falling back through 5006–5009. Corrected against `StalePortReclaimer`, then propagated as *meaning* rather than literal re-translation to all 8 locales. (9 resx; RemEx-as3t.)
- **The command palette can now Force Restart the PC, and the splash timer's teardown is no longer dead code.** Two loose ends from the parallel sweep, both surfaced by review rather than by the beads. `ForceRestart` existed only on `RemoteViewModel`, bound straight from `RemoteView.axaml`, so the palette — which wires exclusively through `shell.Connection.*` — had no way to reach it. `ConnectionViewModel` now exposes `ForceRestartCommand`, and the palette entry carries all three `Confirm_ForceRestart_*` keys, since `CommandPaletteEntry` throws at construction on a partial set. The bead asserted "everything needed already exists"; it did not, and the agent that hit it stopped rather than write code against a member that cannot compile. Separately `SkiaSplashControl.Dispose()` had been added but **nothing ever called it**, so the `DispatcherTimer`'s strong reference back into the control survived exactly as before. It is now hooked to the owning window's `Closed` event — deliberately *not* to unload, because `OnDetachedFromVisualTree` intentionally calls only `Stop()` so a reattached splash resumes ticking through that same subscription, and disposing on unload would leave a reattached splash frozen. The `SequenceCompleted` hookup beside it is guarded now too: `OnLoaded` runs again on every reattach and was adding a duplicate handler each time, which is the same subscribe-without-unsubscribe shape the lifecycle bead existed to fix. (`ConnectionViewModel.cs`, `CommandPaletteViewModel.cs`, `ShellView.axaml.cs`, 9 resx; RemEx-6cda, RemEx-wcte.)
- **Three PC event-handler lifecycle defects: a resize drag that double-applied after a theme switch, a sensor subscription leak, and a splash timer with no detach path.** `DraggableCard.OnApplyTemplate` re-wired the resize thumb without detaching the previously-found one — template reapplication (e.g. switching between the four themes) left the old thumb firing alongside the new, so one resize drag applied its delta twice. `CanvasDashboardViewModel.CleanupSensorSubscriptions` removed `AlertTriggered` but never the `PropertyChanged` handler added at sensor creation, so every sensor kept the view model alive and could fire customization handling more than once after a reconnect. And `SkiaSplashControl` subscribed `_timer.Tick` with no `-=` anywhere; it is now `IDisposable` with a `Dispose` that stops the timer and unhooks `Tick` (detach-from-tree deliberately still only pauses, since the control can be re-attached). (`DraggableCard.cs`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `SkiaSplashControl.cs`; RemEx-wcte.)
- **The RemEx-jxmf waiter-leak fix actually landed this time.** That bead (FileTransferClient leaks its pending-reply waiter when the send itself throws, 11 sites) was closed with its change sitting uncommitted in the working tree — recovered and committed now. Every request/response helper goes through one `SendAndAwaitReplyAsync` that reaps the waiter in a `finally` covering a throw from `SendAsync` itself, keeps each call site's bounded-wait semantics, and leaves copy/move unbounded on purpose because their duration scales with file size. (`FileTransferClient.cs`; RemEx-jxmf.)
- **"Your PC" was untranslated in seven PC locales, not the six the bead listed.** `About_PCHost` shipped raw English in `hi`, `id`, `pl`, `pt-BR`, `tr`, `uk` — and in `es`, which the bead did not mention and which the sweep caught anyway. Each value now uses the phrasing that file already uses for the same referent rather than a dictionary rendering: `PC Anda`, `Twój komputer`, `Seu PC`, `PC'niz` (nominative possessive, matching the file's existing locative `PC'nizde`), `Ваш ПК`, `Tu PC`. `fr` was already correct (`Hôte PC`) and untouched. Android's counterpart `about_pc_host_label` had the same gap in `values-in` alone (RemEx-m6ap). (9 resx, `values-in/strings.xml`; RemEx-gwdp, RemEx-m6ap.)
- **French put a breaking space before `?`, `!`, `:` and `;` — in 109 places, not the 23 the bead counted.** French typography requires a narrow no-break space before those marks, or the line can wrap leaving the punctuation stranded at the start of the next line. Every plain ASCII space in that position in `values-fr/strings.xml` is now U+202F, punctuation otherwise untouched, file still well-formed and key count unchanged. (RemEx-19et.)
- **An unknown saved theme preset no longer vanishes silently.** `ThemeService.ApplyCustomization` let a failed `Enum.TryParse` fall through and applied nothing, so a stale or hand-edited `ThemeId` left the app with no theme and no clue why. It now logs a warning naming the unrecognised preset and falls back explicitly to `AppTheme.Dynamic`, the same known-good default the file already uses. It deliberately does not throw — a bad saved preset must not stop startup. (RemEx-0mbh.)
- **`accessKey` locals in `AndroidNativeExports` did not hold an access key.** They hold an SPKI certificate hash; the name invited exactly the wrong assumption in the file that defines the JNI boundary. Renamed locals and comments only — every export name, `UnmanagedCallersOnly` signature and Kotlin-bound symbol is untouched, because a renamed export breaks `libRemexCore.so` at NativeAOT link time with no obvious connection to the change. (RemEx-t4hn.)
- **Signing the remote PC out asked nothing first, on either surface that offers it.** Sign Out closes every open program and discards unsaved work — precisely why Shutdown, Force Shutdown, Restart, Force Restart and Reboot to Firmware all confirm — but `RemoteViewModel.SignOutPcAsync` ran immediately, and the Ctrl+K palette entry inherited that. Both surfaces are fixed together, deliberately: they invoke *different* commands (the Remote page button binds the `RemoteViewModel` wrapper, the palette invokes `Connection.SignOutCommand` directly), so confirming one would have recreated exactly the surface divergence RemEx-eifi existed to remove. New `Confirm_SignOut_Title` / `_Message` / `_Btn` in all 9 locales, parity 1082 → 1085. Lock, Sleep and Hibernate remain unconfirmed on purpose — none of them closes a program or loses work — which leaves the confirmed set matching destructiveness rather than category. **The message carries a warning the other power actions don't need:** the PC stays *on*, but `remex.agent` lives inside the signed-in user's session (started by a logon task on Windows, an XDG autostart entry on Linux), so signing out ends the agent too and the phone cannot reach the PC again until **you** sign in *at* the machine — a lockout worth knowing about before confirming, not after, since the person tapping it is by definition not sitting at the PC. The message says "you", not "someone", because the logon task is registered `-User $account`: it fires only for that account's next interactive sign-in, so a *different* user signing in would not bring the agent back. Each locale's verb comes from its own `Remote_SignOut`, and each title follows the pattern that file already uses for `Confirm_Shutdown_Title`, which is not the same shape everywhere — `pl` and `uk` take a verbal noun ("Wylogowanie", "Вихід із системи") where `es`/`fr`/`tr` take an infinitive or imperative. Also adds the first tests for `CommandPaletteEntry`'s confirmation contract (9 of them): `ValidateConfirmationKeys` rejects a half-marked entry at construction, and its failure direction is fail-**open** — two of three keys and `RequiresConfirmation` is quietly false, so a destructive entry would run straight from Ctrl+K with nothing reporting it — yet nothing had ever tested it. (`RemoteViewModel.cs`, `CommandPaletteViewModel.cs`, 9 resx files, new `CommandPaletteEntryTests.cs`; RemEx-4b8m.)
- **Confirming a kill could end a different program than the dialog named, and the failure message was a raw resource key.** Two defects in the same method. First, `KillProcessAsync` captured a `ProcessInfo`, awaited the confirmation dialog, then sent only `process.Id` — but process polling keeps replacing the list underneath and the dialog can sit open indefinitely, so if the target exited while the user was deciding and the OS reused its PID, RemEx ended whatever now held that number. The confirmation added by RemEx-6p1f is what opened this window; the previous no-prompt behaviour acted immediately and could not drift. A new `ConfirmedTargetStillPresent` guard now re-verifies, after the dialog and before sending anything, that the current list still holds an entry with the same `Id` **and** `Name` **and** — only when both sides are readable — the same `FilePath`. It fails closed: anything short of an exact match leaves the process running and tells the user why. Comparisons are deliberately case-**sensitive**, since both values come from the same host enumeration (so casing is stable and a case-only difference means something really changed), because ignoring case could only ever turn a mismatch into a match, and because on Linux two paths differing only in case are two different files. `FilePath` is skipped when either side is blank, as the host reports blank when it could not read the path — comparing against that would refuse every kill of a protected process. The guard is `internal static` rather than inline specifically so it could be unit-tested; 11 new tests cover PID reuse by a different program, PID reuse by the same name at a different path, unreadable paths on either side, case-only differences, and same-name processes at other PIDs. Second, the fallback error used `TaskManager_KillFailed`, which **existed in no `.resx` file at all** — and `LocalizationService`'s indexer ends `?? key`, so a kill that failed without a host message displayed the literal developer string `TaskManager_KillFailed` to the user in every language, English included; the call also passed the PID into a format string that had no placeholder, so it was dropped. Both new keys (`TaskManager_KillFailed`, `TaskManager_KillTargetChanged`) are now translated in all 9 locales, quoting per each file's own `Confirm_KillProcess_Format` convention rather than one house style; key parity 1080 → 1082. This closes the dangerous case — ending an *unrelated* program — but not every case: `ProcessInfo` has no start time, so a relaunch of the same program handed the same PID still matches, and the guard is only as fresh as the last poll. Closing that needs the host to verify identity and kill atomically, which also covers Android's kill path (currently sending a bare PID with no guard at all) and is filed as RemEx-druh. (`TaskManagerViewModel.cs`, 9 resx files, new `TaskManagerKillTargetTests.cs`; RemEx-2s91.)
- **The Task Manager's "Sort by:" label was English in every language — and translating it exposed a layout bug.** Its siblings were localized (`Name` → `Nombre`, `Nazwa`, `Назва`) while the label itself never had been; it only *looked* deliberate because all nine locales matched. Each translation is built from that locale's own existing sort verb rather than a dictionary, so `Ordenar`, `Sortuj`, `Сортувати` and the rest carry through. Turkish uses the nominalised `Sıralama ölçütü:` because its imperative `Sırala` cannot grammatically govern a criterion, and Hindi drops a backward-pointing demonstrative that gestured away from the sort options it introduces. Making the label longer then revealed that the sort row measured it before the four sort chips and gave it everything it asked for, so the chips absorbed the entire shortfall and cut their labels mid-glyph — English was already close to that edge at large font scales. The label is now capped at a maximum width and ellipsizes, and the chips ellipsize rather than slicing characters, so English renders exactly as before while only the long translations are trimmed. (`values-*/strings.xml` × 8, `TaskManagerScreen.kt`; RemEx-0pxq.)
- **On the PC, Ukrainian used the download word for uploads in fourteen places — including two buttons sitting side by side.** `Upload File` and `Download Selected` both read `Завантажити…`, so the two controls were indistinguishable, and five status messages were byte-identical to their download twins. The bead was filed for those five; the real count was fourteen, because byte-identity is only the symptom. The worst cases aren't collisions at all: `Settings_FileTransferDesc` and the file-transfer tutorial each used the download word **twice in one sentence for opposite directions**. All fourteen now use `вивантаж-`, which this file had already committed to in the two places that state direction explicitly (`ВИВАНТАЖЕННЯ` in the queue label, `Вивантажено` in the activity feed) — so the fix aligns the outliers with the file's own convention, and with Android, which standardised on the same stem. `FileTransfer_QueuedForUpload` is deliberately untouched: it is identical to its download twin in all nine locales *including English*, making it direction-neutral by design rather than a translation defect.
