- **Android onboarding and connection help no longer advertise PC products that don't exist.** The tutorial's setup page offered a choice between a "Desktop Client (Recommended)" and a "Standalone PC background-only service" — neither exists; the PC side is one app. That page, the auto-discovery hint, and connection-help step 1 (`tutorial_page2_body`, `connection_auto_discover_hint`, `connection_help_step1_body`) now describe the single RemEx app that starts automatically at sign-in, exactly where a confused user lands when discovery fails. The IP-lookup FAQ (`faq_a2`) drops its macOS instructions (macOS is not a supported PC platform), and the replay-tutorial setting no longer version-pins "the 2.0 walkthrough". All in 9 languages; the Turkish and Ukrainian variants of these keys were untranslated English placeholders and are now properly translated. Remaining macOS content elsewhere (tutorial page 4, platform picker) is tracked as RemEx-hqcl. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (9 strings.xml files; RemEx-oq6l.)
- **The PC About page's version card no longer calls the app a "Desktop Client".** The label implied a separate client component that doesn't exist — RemEx on the PC is one app that is both the host and the UI. The row is now labeled "RemEx on this PC" (translated in all 9 languages), and the XAML comments describe the two rows truthfully (this app's own version vs. the embedded host's connection status). (`AboutView.axaml`, 9 resx files; RemEx-qegi.)
- **Remote-desktop failure messages no longer read "…on the Your PC."** A device-label token was pasted into sentences that already carried an article, breaking the exact errors users see when the stream fails — in English, Spanish ("en el Your PC"), Indonesian ("di Your PC"), and Brazilian Portuguese ("no Your PC"); the other five locales had translated the label properly. The three `rd_err_*` strings now read grammatically in all 9 languages. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (4 strings.xml files; RemEx-24df.)
- **A pinned shared folder now accepts writes no matter how you navigated to it.** The same physical folder behaved differently by route: reached via its pinned shared root, uploads and file management worked; reached via full-device drive browsing ("Browse this PC"), every write op — upload, delete, rename, copy, move, new folder — failed with "Unknown shared root", even when that exact folder was pinned. The host now resolves the volume-mode path first (collapsing `..`, denylist enforced — raw input is never prefix-compared) and, when it lands inside a pinned root, re-expresses the request against that root — the identical reference the app could have used directly, so nothing is widened and the root's own permission flags still apply; the deepest containing root wins so the most specific permissions govern, and copy/move requires both endpoints in the same pinned root. Genuinely un-pinned targets now refuse with a plain-English pointer to the shared-folders list instead of the cryptic "Unknown shared root". Covered by 6 new security tests + 2 handler tests; full suite green (191/149/450). Adversarial Opus review PASS (junction/cross-volume/separator/drive-root/info-leak probes all resolved). (`IFileTransferService.cs`, `FileTransferService.cs`, `FileTransferHandler.cs`; RemEx-hb1t.3.)
- **The Wake-on-LAN quick-settings tile no longer leaks coroutines or crashes when the panel closes quickly.** Both `onClick` and `onStartListening` launched coroutines on throwaway unmanaged `CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO)` instances — never cancelled, and the listener path dereferenced `qsTile` after a suspending settings read, so collapsing the QS panel at the wrong moment could NPE (and tile state was mutated off the main thread). The tile now uses one service-scoped supervisor scope on `Main.immediate` (cancelled in `onDestroy`), cancels the tile-update job in `onStopListening`, hops to IO only for the settings read and native send, and null-checks `qsTile` before updating. A tapped wake still goes out if the panel closes — the send job survives until the service dies. (`RemexWakeOnLanTileService.kt`; RemEx-8efl.)
- **Android downloads no longer sit on a frozen 100% bar while the file is verified and saved.** After the last data frame lands, the engine still fsyncs the staging file, waits for the host's hash, computes SHA-256, and copies the entire staging file into the SAF destination — for big files that second full write dominates, and the UI showed a stuck 100% Active bar the whole time. `runDownload` now enters the same `Verifying` state uploads already use the moment streaming completes, so the row shows the localized "Verifying" label until the file is committed. State flow is now Active → Verifying → Done, matching uploads; the job service already treats Verifying as active. (`FileTransferEngine.kt`; RemEx-hb1t.2.)
- **Android's About page no longer advertises "What's New in 2.0" four releases later.** The title is now bound to `BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME` (it can never freeze on an old version again), and the five highlight entries were rewritten with current content — Dashboard 2.0, smarter sensor cards, resumable verified file sharing, Expressive motion with Reduce Motion support, and the 2.4.0 security hardening — replacing the 2.0.0-era H.264/pairing/Linux-launch copy. Translated in all 9 languages; the Opus review confirmed no unreleased feature (like the pending channel proof-of-possession work) is claimed. (`AboutScreen.kt`, 9 strings.xml files; RemEx-7204.)
- **Android's "Clear completed" now also removes Failed transfers.** The queue panel's clear button lit up for Done, Cancelled, *and* Failed rows, but the store's prune predicate only removed Done and Cancelled — so failed transfers survived every clear, and the only way to dismiss one was the absurd retry-then-race-to-cancel dance. `pruneFinished` now treats Failed as terminal, matching every other finished-state predicate in the queue UI. Clearing a failed row intentionally removes its Retry button; the transfer can be re-initiated from the browser. (`TransferQueue.kt`; RemEx-hb1t.5.)
- **The PC About page's What's New panel now shows the newest released version's notes instead of stale 2.0-era highlights.** The changelog parser scanned only the first `## [` section — `[Unreleased]` — so an empty Unreleased section yielded 0 items and silently triggered the hardcoded fallback (live since 2.1; the code looked correct, which is why it survived four releases). It would also have shown *unshipped* entries once Unreleased gained bullets. The parser now skips `[Unreleased]` and any bullet-less section and returns the newest non-empty released section, covered by 5 new unit tests including one that parses the real `docs/CHANGELOG.md`. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (`AboutViewModel.cs`, new `AboutViewModelChangelogTests.cs`; RemEx-xtc2.)
- **The PC FAQ pairing entry no longer sends users hunting for a "desktop client" that doesn't exist.** `Faq_Q2` asked "How do I pair my phone or second PC?" and answered "Use Settings → Connection on the desktop client" — but Android is the only client and there is no second-PC pairing. The entry now describes reality: open Settings → Connection *in this window* for the QR code and 6-digit PIN, scan or type it from the RemEx phone app, pairing always runs phone-to-PC. Bonus: 8 locale files carried untranslated English placeholders for these two keys — all now have real translations using each locale's actual Settings/Connection UI labels. Reviewed by Opus (PASS, incl. translation-quality check). (9 resx files; RemEx-c0dd.)
- **Deleting remote files from the PC's file panel now asks for confirmation first.** The Delete button looped permanent remote deletes over the entire multi-selection immediately on click — one mis-click on a large selection was unrecoverable. It now shows the same confirmation dialog the power actions use, naming the single file (or the count plus the first three names) before anything is deleted, and deletes nothing on cancel. Localized in all 9 languages (4 new `Confirm_DeleteRemote_*` keys, key parity 1038 everywhere). Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (`FileTransferViewModel.cs`, `FileTransferView.axaml.cs`, 9 resx files; RemEx-07jx.)
- **Failed, declined, timed-out, and cancelled Android downloads no longer leave a 0-byte ghost file behind for every attempt.** The SAF destination document is created by the system file picker before the transfer even starts, but every terminal failure path only cleaned up the internal `.part` staging file — so each retry minted another empty "name (1).ext" in the chosen folder. All terminal download endings (host declined, negotiate timeout, stream/hash/commit failure, channel-dead exception, not-connected, and queue cancel) now delete the pre-created destination document via `DocumentsContract.deleteDocument`. Safety guard: the document is deleted **only** when it verifiably reports zero length — a picker-returned *existing* file chosen for overwrite still holds the user's original bytes until commit and is never touched. Reviewed by Opus (PASS). (`FileTransferEngine.kt`; RemEx-hb1t.1.)
- **Downloading, hashing, searching, viewing metadata for, or thumbnailing a file found via full-device drive browsing no longer fails with "Unknown shared root".** Browsing a bare mounted drive (not a pinned shared root) already worked — `HandleFileBrowseRequestAsync` had a fallback from the small pinned-root registry to `VolumeEnumerator` — but every other read operation resolved `rootId` only through the pinned-root list, so touching any file found that way threw immediately (reported as failed downloads showing "Unknown shared root 'C:\'" for each drive letter in the Android Transfers panel). `IFileTransferService` gained read-only volume-mode counterparts (`OpenVolumeForReadAsync`, `ComputeVolumeSha256Async`, `SearchVolumeAsync`, `GetVolumeMetadataAsync`, `GetVolumeThumbnailBase64Async`), and the five corresponding handlers now re-verify the full-browse consent grant and fall back to the real volume, mirroring the existing browse path exactly. Upload/delete/rename/copy/move/mkdir against a bare drive are deliberately unchanged and still require pinning the folder as a shared root first — full browse never exposes write/delete (RemEx-hb1t). Covered by 5 new tests including an end-to-end volume download. (`IFileTransferService.cs`, `FileTransferService.cs`, `FileTransferHandler.cs`, `PingPongHandler.cs`; RemEx-39jw.)
- **The tutorial screen's welcome emoji and body text no longer ignore the in-app font-scale setting.** `TutorialPageContent` overrode two Material typography roles with hardcoded literals (`displayLarge.copy(fontSize = 64.sp)` on the emoji, `lineHeight = 24.sp` on the body), discarding the per-role scaling `typographyForFontFamily(fontFamilyKey, fontScale)` already applies. Both `Text`s now use their unmodified typography roles, so raising the font-scale preference to maximum visibly enlarges both. (`TutorialScreen.kt`; RemEx-ii9c.)
- **The telemetry view-picker cell now announces its selected/unselected state to TalkBack.** `ViewPickerCell` (the mode picker in the sensor-card "change view" sheet) used a plain `clickable`, carrying no semantic role or selected state — screen readers announced it as a generic button regardless of which view was active. It now uses `Modifier.selectable(selected, role = Role.RadioButton, onClick)`, exposing `SemanticsProperties.Selected`, covered by a new Compose UI test (`ViewPickerCellTest`). (`TelemetryViews.kt`; RemEx-xo8b.)
- **The dashboard's first-run coach-mark overlay and its scrim no longer snap on and off at full opacity.** The overlay was mounted with a raw `if`, so its 62%-black scrim flashed in and out instantly on a screen where everything else is spring-animated. It's now wrapped in `AnimatedVisibility` with `fadeIn`/`fadeOut` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`, so the scrim (a plain `.background()` draw inside the animated subtree) ramps smoothly instead. (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-o1dw.)
- **Dashboard cards now spring into their lifted scale on drag instead of snapping to 1.03x in a single frame.** `DraggableDashboardCard` applied the lift via a conditional `Modifier.scale`, so the one gesture the app's own tutorial demos with spring physics didn't actually have any in the real interaction. The scale now comes from `animateFloatAsState` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`, applied via `graphicsLayer`; drag/pan/pick-up gesture logic is untouched. (`DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-r39j.)
- **The dashboard card pin toggle now has a real 48x48dp touch target.** The clickable `Surface` was explicitly sized to 24dp — half the Material minimum — while sitting on a drag surface where mis-taps also trigger card gestures. It now wraps `.minimumInteractiveComponentSize()` around the existing `.size(24.dp)`, expanding the touch/hit region to 48dp while the visual chip stays 24dp, centered. Covered by a new Compose UI test (`DraggableDashboardCardTest`), run and passing on-device. (`DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-pdj0.)

### Fixed

- **Curvy dashboard card shapes (ellipse, hexagon, blob) no longer clip the card title.** The uniform adaptive inset is sized for the card's widest band, but those shapes intrude much further at the title row's height near the top — "RAM" rendered as "M", "CPU Temp" as "PU Temp" at every font size. The title row now gets an extra start/end inset measured against the actual clip path at the title's own vertical band (worst case of the band's top and bottom edges), so it is exact for every shape, morph progress, and card size with no hand-tuned per-shape constants. Rectangular presets measure zero intrusion and keep a byte-identical layout, and the discrete rounded-rectangle preset skips the measurement entirely. Verified on-emulator with Oval telemetry cards at system font scale 1.0 and 1.3 — all titles render fully. (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-0ukf.)
- **Dashboard cards no longer clip their labels at large system font sizes.** At system font Largest, "TAP TO CONNECT" lost glyphs and "WAKE PC" wrapped letter-by-letter inside fixed-size cards. The canvas is now scale-aware: rendered card bounds *and* positions grow with the system font scale (capped at 1.3×), so the user's arrangement scales uniformly with no new overlap — persisted sizes/positions are untouched and layouts round-trip losslessly. Drag, group-move, and resize stay finger-exact because the factor folds into the same `canvasScale` divisor the pinch-zoom math already uses (verified against all three gesture paths in review; `DashboardInteraction.kt` untouched). The shape-safety padding also scales with font size so titles sit deeper inside unsafe shapes. Verified on-emulator at system Largest; at 1.0 the math is byte-identical to before. Residual blob-shape title clipping pre-exists at all scales and is tracked as RemEx-0ukf. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`DashboardScreen.kt`, `Theme.kt`; RemEx-0i3x.)
- **The remote desktop's start-streaming buttons are announced to TalkBack and show tooltips.** Both PlayArrow buttons (windowed top bar and fullscreen control bar) had `contentDescription = null` — invisible to screen readers and the only two control icons that couldn't receive tooltips in the RemEx-31wq pass. Both now reuse the existing localized `button_start_streaming` string (present in all 9 locales — no new key) for their content description and `RemexTooltip` label. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-ji98.)
- **TalkBack announces the grid overflow chip and dashboard pin toggle as buttons again.** Both were converted from `IconButton` (which sets `Role.Button` implicitly) to clickable `Surface` chips during their touch-target fixes, silently losing the role — screen readers announced a generic clickable. Each now carries `Modifier.semantics { role = Role.Button }` on its single merged node. Existing touch-target tests still pass. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileManagerFileItem.kt`, `DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-qluo.)
- **The in-app font scale no longer compounds unbounded with the system font-size setting.** The in-app scale (85–140%) multiplies sizes that Android's system scale multiplies again, so system Largest plus in-app max rendered ~1.82x and clipped single-line headers. A documented clamp (`MAX_COMBINED_FONT_SCALE = 1.6`) now bounds the combined scale by reducing only the app's own contribution — the system accessibility setting is always fully honored (on API 34+ the system alone can reach 2.0 and wins). Verified on a release build at system Largest + in-app max: Settings and Personalization render unclipped; the dashboard's remaining card clipping reproduces at system scale alone (compounding-neutral control run) and is filed separately as RemEx-0i3x. Unit-tested (5 cases). Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`Type.kt`, `Theme.kt`; RemEx-95ls.)
- **TalkBack now announces the disconnected state on the Dashboard navigation item.** The red dot `Badge` shown on the nav item when the PC is disconnected was purely visual, so screen-reader users got no indication of the state it conveys. Both the NavigationBar and NavigationRail badge sites now carry `Modifier.semantics { stateDescription }` sourced from the existing localized `status_disconnected` string (present in all 9 locales) — the state propagates into the nav item's merged semantics and TalkBack reads "Dashboard … Disconnected". Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS), including verification of the semantics merge path through `NavigationBarItem`'s selectable node. (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-8d9k.)
- **Removed dead space below the bottom navigation bar.** `NavigationBar` was wrapped in a manual `Modifier.navigationBarsPadding()` on top of the inset M3's `NavigationBar` already consumes through its default `windowInsets`, so the system-bar inset was applied twice on both gesture-nav and 3-button devices. The manual padding is gone; verified with a screenshot on a 3-button-navigation emulator showing the nav items flush above the system bar. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS), which also confirmed no ancestor consumes the inset (ruling out the single-inset alternative). (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-57og.)

### Fixed (file manager)

- **The file manager grid's unselected selection circle is no longer invisible.** In grid selection mode the `RadioButtonUnchecked` indicator was tinted `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.surface` — a container role nearly identical to the tile behind it — so unselected items showed no visible indicator at all. It now uses the content role `onSurfaceVariant`, matching the list view's indicator, which holds contrast across light/dark, all three scheme sources, monochrome, and contrast 1.0. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileManagerFileItem.kt`; RemEx-n88y.)
- **The file manager grid's selection indicators are now readable over photo thumbnails.** The CheckCircle/RadioButtonUnchecked indicators drew bare over the tile, so their role-token tints could lose contrast against arbitrary image content. They now sit on the same translucent circular `surface`-chip backing the grid's overflow button already uses. (`FileManagerFileItem.kt`; RemEx-hrxl.)
- **The file manager grid's overflow button now has a real 48x48dp touch target.** The per-item actions button (download/rename/delete/pin — the only way to reach them in grid view) was an `IconButton` forced to 28dp, well under the Material minimum. The decorative chip and button are now a single clickable `Surface` carrying `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()`, so the visual chip stays 28dp while the touch bounds meet 48dp — the same pattern as the dashboard pin toggle. Covered by a new Compose UI test (`FileManagerGridItemTest`) asserting both touch dimensions, run and passing on an API 37 emulator. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileManagerFileItem.kt`; RemEx-7f31.)

### Added

- **The file-properties sheet's thumbnail now grows into place instead of just fading in.** The original ask — a shared-element transform from the grid tile into the sheet — is impossible with the current architecture (`ModalBottomSheet` renders in its own window, and Compose shared elements cannot cross window boundaries), so the bead was re-scoped: the thumbnail enters with an expand-from-small scale (0.6 → 1.0, `defaultSpatialSpec`) plus fade (`defaultEffectsSpec`), visually suggesting continuity from the tile it came from. (`FileManagerSheets.kt`; RemEx-mgk1.)
- **Dynamic color is now a user-facing toggle in Personalization → Color Studio.** `RemExTheme` has always taken a `dynamicColor` parameter, but nothing ever passed it — Material You wallpaper theming was permanently on and the RemEx brand fallback palette (amber, RemEx-2xsy) was unreachable on API 31+ devices. A new Switch (shown only on API 31+, disabled while the Custom palette is active since that branch overrides dynamic color anyway) persists to a dedicated DataStore key via an immediate, non-debounced write and re-themes live. Two new strings added across all 9 locales. Verified on the API-34 emulator: live re-theme both directions, persistence across restart, and the disabled state under Custom. (`SettingsManager.kt`, `PersonalizationViewModel.kt`, `PersonalizationScreen.kt`, `MainActivity.kt`, 9× `strings.xml`; RemEx-9429.)
- **Icon-only toolbar buttons now show a label on long-press.** A new `RemexTooltip` wrapper (M3 `PlainTooltip`) wraps 25 icon-only controls — the file-manager toolbar and selection bar (11), the remote-desktop control bars in both windowed and fullscreen modes (14) — always using the same string as the icon's `contentDescription`, so sighted long-press and TalkBack announce identical labels. Verified by a new instrumented test (`FileManagerToolbarTooltipTest`, passing on the emulator) asserting long-press surfaces the label. The two start-streaming buttons have `contentDescription = null` and were skipped — filed as RemEx-ji98. (`RemexFlexibleTopBar.kt`, `FileManagerToolbar.kt`, `RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-31wq.)
- **The splash screens respect "Remove animations".** All three splash variants (RemexCommand's scanline/sweep/zoom, Pong's rally, CosmicZoom's starfield) gate on the new `LocalReducedMotion`: when set, they render a single static brand-mark frame and finish immediately instead of holding the user through several seconds of unskippable choreography on every cold start. Verified on an emulator — with animator scale 0, a cold start lands on the dashboard in under 3 seconds. (`SplashScreen.kt`, `SplashRemexCommand.kt`, `SplashPong.kt`, `SplashCosmicZoom.kt`; RemEx-n39x.)
- **The app now honors the system "Remove animations" accessibility setting.** `RemExTheme` reads `Settings.Global.ANIMATOR_DURATION_SCALE` (kept live via a ContentObserver, so toggling the setting re-themes without a restart): at scale 0 the app-wide motion scheme switches from Expressive to the calmer non-bouncy Standard scheme, and a new `LocalReducedMotion` CompositionLocal is provided for screens with self-driven choreography (splash zooms, coach-mark loops) to suppress their motion — the splash bead RemEx-n39x consumes it next. Covered by a unit test asserting the scheme differs between scale 0 and 1. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`Theme.kt`; RemEx-tej8.)

### Changed

- **The success greens now follow the system contrast setting when dynamic color is active.** With dynamic color, the main scheme responds to the OS-level contrast slider but the success palette only knew the in-app contrast — raising system contrast adjusted every role except success. A new `rememberSystemContrast()` (API 34+, kept live via `UiModeManager.ContrastChangeListener`) feeds the success palette whenever the dynamic scheme is active; custom and fallback paths keep using the app's themeContrast. Bonus: the system domain reaches −1, so *reduced* contrast now lowers success too, which the 0–1 app slider never could. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS, zero findings). (`Theme.kt`; RemEx-84dj.)
- **`surfaceBright`/`surfaceDim` are now real theme roles, and the app's translucent chip backings became opaque tiers.** `colorSchemeFromSeed` never populated the two roles, so the custom-seed and static-fallback paths silently used baseline grays that ignored seed, style and contrast; both branches now derive them from the `DynamicScheme` (the dynamic-color path already did natively). The file-manager chip backings (`surface` @ 0.7 alpha over photo thumbnails) and the floating connection chip (`surfaceContainerHighest` @ 0.92) now use opaque `surfaceBright`, guaranteeing designed icon contrast over arbitrary content and tracking monochrome/contrast axes correctly. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`Theme.kt`, `FileManagerFileItem.kt`, `CommonComponents.kt`; RemEx-2i8s.)
- **Dashboard cards now scale-and-fade in when added and out when removed instead of materializing and vanishing instantly.** A per-card presence tracker (`MutableTransitionState` + a snapshot of the card's last state) drives `AnimatedVisibility` around each canvas card. Exiting cards render their snapshot with **all pointer input consumed** at the initial pass — a vanishing card can never be grabbed — and always draw beneath the live layout; live cards render in exact `visibleCards` order, preserving the original z-stacking byte-for-byte (a first draft iterated the unordered tracking map, which the reviewer caught as nondeterministic stacking on the drag surface — fixed in round 1). The drag/reorder logic in `DashboardInteraction.kt` is untouched, and the dashboard instrumented tests still pass. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS after 1 fix round). (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-1ymv.)
- **The More sheet now slides out fully before navigating instead of vanishing mid-animation.** Selecting a More destination navigated immediately while `hide()` was still animating, and a route-change effect destroyed the sheet's composition in the same frame. Navigation now happens in the hide job's `invokeOnCompletion` (gated on non-cancelled completion, so a double-tap race can't fire a stale navigation), and the route-change effect awaits `hide()` before unmounting. The reviewer verified main-thread safety of navigating from the completion handler and that no double-hide/deadlock path exists. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-rzzo.)
- **The Remote Mouse trackpad now responds visually to touch.** Tapping the trackpad gave haptics but zero visual response — the only interactive surface in the app without one. A pressed flag from `detectTapGestures`' `onPress` slot (which neither consumes events nor alters tap/drag semantics) now drives an animated tonal shift of the pad (`surfaceVariant` → `surfaceContainerHighest` on the `motionScheme` fast effects spec) while the finger is down, covering taps and drags alike. (`RemoteMouseScreen.kt`; RemEx-uba6.)
- **The disconnected badge on the Dashboard nav icon now pops in and out instead of appearing in one frame.** Every nav item renders a single stable `BadgedBox` (the icon no longer changes composable identity with connection state); the badge itself scales+fades via `AnimatedVisibility` on `motionScheme` fast specs. The TalkBack `stateDescription` from the earlier a11y fix now applies conditionally and vanishes on reconnect — the reviewer verified semantics configs rebuild from scratch per recomposition, badge-less `BadgedBox`es measure identically to bare icons, and settled invisible badges compose nothing. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-pgzk.)
- **Cancelling a predictive-back gesture now springs the app shell home instead of snapping.** `backProgress` became an `Animatable`: mid-gesture it snaps to the finger, and on cancel or completion it animates to identity with a `motionScheme` spatial spring, per the M3 predictive-back spec. The cancel-path animation runs on the composition scope — the reviewer verified against androidx source that `onBackCancelled()` cancels the handler's own coroutine, so an inline `animateTo` there would never execute and would have frozen the shell mid-gesture (the first draft's bug, caught and fixed in review). The `isBackGestureActive` flag is gone; the transform gates follow `backProgress.value > 0f` so the return spring stays visible. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS after 1 fix round). (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-gblj.)
- **Entering selection mode in the file manager now animates instead of jumping every visible row at once.** The list row's leading slot (type icon/thumbnail ↔ selection indicator) cross-fades via `AnimatedContent`; the grid tile's indicator chip fades in/out; a new shared `SelectionIndicator` cross-fades the check/uncheck icon and animates its tint; and selected rows/tiles now get an animated `secondaryContainer` highlight via `animateColorAsState` (the list previously had no selected highlight at all). All specs from `motionScheme`; existing touch-target and tooltip tests still pass on the emulator. (`FileManagerFileItem.kt`; RemEx-40y8.)
- **The file manager's selection bar, breadcrumbs row and connected/disconnected swap all animate.** The contextual selection bar and the breadcrumbs (hidden during search) now enter/exit through `AnimatedVisibility` expand/shrink+fade, and the whole-screen connected/disconnected switch cross-fades through `AnimatedContent` — its `return@Column` early exit is gone. All specs from `motionScheme`. (`FileTransferScreen.kt`; RemEx-xgc7.)
- **The breadcrumb trail animates as folders change and auto-scrolls to the current crumb.** The trail rebuilt with no motion on every navigation, and descending into a deep path silently pushed the current folder off-screen. The trail's width now animates via `animateContentSize` (on the unbounded inner row, where the scrollable actually measures it), and a `LaunchedEffect` on the crumb count animates the scroll to the trail's end — both on `motionScheme` spatial specs. (`FileManagerBreadcrumbs.kt`; RemEx-dkr6.)
- **The file-manager header's transfer bar, status text and search-truncation notice now grow and shrink into place.** All three stacked notices popped in via raw conditionals, shoving the file list below in a single frame. Each is now an `AnimatedVisibility` with `motionScheme` expand/shrink + fade; the status text remembers its last non-blank value so its shrink-out exit still has text to show. (`FileTransferScreen.kt`; RemEx-z01v.)
- **The App Launcher's disconnected/empty/apps states now cross-fade instead of hard-swapping.** The three-branch conditional (disconnected full-screen, empty column with fetch button, recent-carousel + grid) renders through `AnimatedContent` keyed on a body-state enum with a `motionScheme` fade, so the grid dissolves in when a refresh returns instead of appearing fully formed. Tile `animateItem` placement and press-spring behavior are untouched; the disconnected pane's `weight(1f)` became `fillMaxSize()` since `ColumnScope` doesn't reach through the transition (same available space, sole child). (`AppLauncherScreen.kt`; RemEx-svue.)
- **Transfer-queue rows animate placement, their progress bars shrink out, and the pause/resume/cancel cluster cross-fades.** Queue rows now carry `Modifier.animateItem` (fast spatial spec) so inserts, completions and clears move rows instead of teleporting them; the finished-transfer progress bar shrinks+fades via `AnimatedVisibility` instead of collapsing row height in one frame; and the action buttons swap through `AnimatedContent` keyed on the transfer state, with branches reading the transition's own state so exiting copies render correctly. All specs from `motionScheme`. (`FileManagerQueuePanel.kt`; RemEx-c6xp.)
- **The connection chip's status-dot pulse is tokenized, honors reduce-motion, and its label now cross-fades.** The permanent 1.0–1.4x dot pulse gets a named `CONNECTION_CHIP_PULSE_MS` token and is no longer composed at all when disconnected (it previously ran forever at target 1.0) or when "Remove animations" is on; the pulse value stays a Compose `State` read inside `graphicsLayer`, so pulse frames never recompose the chip. The connected/disconnected label, which popped via a raw ternary while the dot color animated, now swaps through `AnimatedContent` with a `motionScheme` fade. (`CommonComponents.kt`; RemEx-3gkr.)
- **The connection orb's glow pulse is tokenized and honors reduce-motion.** The infinite 0.3–0.8 alpha breathe kept its M3-sanctioned linear curve for continuous indicators, but the bare `tween(1500)` literal became a named `CONNECTION_GLOW_PULSE_MS` token, and under the system "Remove animations" setting the pulse is skipped entirely — the glow holds at its midpoint instead of looping forever. (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-bspj.)
- **File-manager rows and grid tiles now animate placement, appearance and removal.** Both `items()` sites carry `Modifier.animateItem` with all three specs sourced from `motionScheme` (fast spatial placement, default-effects fades), so sorting, searching, renaming, deleting — and folder navigation itself — animate per row instead of teleporting. This reconciles with the earlier body-state cross-fade (RemEx-h9rd): the entry list left the `AnimatedContent` key (with it there, any list change re-keyed the whole region and item animations could never fire); LIST/GRID toggles and loading/empty/no-roots changes still cross-fade, and the old stale-exit-content hazard is gone by construction since kind changes away from Content occur only with an empty list. Also retires the per-keystroke whole-region search fade flagged in RemEx-jy4k. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileTransferScreen.kt`; RemEx-598q.)
- **The hand-rolled screen header is gone — Dashboard, File Transfer, Remote Mouse, and all four Settings panes now use the M3 top app bar.** `RemexScreenHeader` (a bespoke Column with manual status-bar padding and no scroll awareness) is deleted; all seven call sites use the existing `RemexFlexibleTopBar` (MediumTopAppBar) with the codebase-standard `rememberRemexTopBarScrollBehavior()` pinned behavior, so screens get the standard M3 container-color change when content scrolls under the bar. Subtitles and action buttons (including the dashboard's coach-mark anchor measurement) carry over unchanged; insets are now handled by the bar itself. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS after one fix round that standardized all sites on the shared scroll-behavior helper). (`RemexScreenHeader.kt` removed, 4 screens; RemEx-qa2s.)
- **All corner radii in the UI layer now come from the theme's shape scale.** Roughly two dozen hardcoded `RoundedCornerShape` literals across nine files (coach overlay demo cards, telemetry chips, file-manager thumbnails/chips, dashboard action bar, remote-desktop sheet, tutorial/About/Settings icon squircles) are replaced by `MaterialTheme.shapes` roles mapped to the nearest step of the 4/8/12/16/28dp scale, plus a new named `remexIconSquircle` token for the percent-based app-icon crops (no M3 role expresses percent corners). Coach-overlay `graphicsLayer` clips capture the shape in composition scope (those lambdas can't read `MaterialTheme`), sharing one val with the matching background/border so clip and paint always agree. A grep now finds `RoundedCornerShape(` in `ui/` only inside `Theme.kt`. Small deliberate visual drift on off-scale radii (e.g. 22→16, 10→12, percent 35→30) is the consolidation the bead intends. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS), including confirmation the gated `DashboardInteraction.kt` change is numerically identical (28dp = `extraLarge`). (9 files + `Theme.kt`; RemEx-flro.)
- **The QR scanner's permission rationale now cross-fades into the camera preview.** Granting camera permission swapped the entire screen body in a single frame via a bare `if`. The two states (rationale with grant button, camera preview with its overlays) are now extracted composables rendered through `AnimatedContent` with a `motionScheme` fade — the file's scan-result overlay already animated correctly; this was its one bare swap. (`QrScannerScreen.kt`; RemEx-uq7r.)
- **The success color roles (connected-state greens) now adapt to dark mode and the contrast setting.** `success`/`onSuccess`/`successContainer`/`onSuccessContainer` were frozen hex pairs in a side CompositionLocal, so at contrast 1.0 every other role adjusted and success did not. They're now generated through the same `Hct`/`SchemeTonalSpot`/`MaterialDynamicColors` pipeline as the rest of the theme — seeded from the former hardcoded green so the hue family is unchanged (success is semantic and stays green regardless of the user's seed). Unit-tested: dark≠light, contrast now shifts the roles, hue stays in the green band across the mode×contrast matrix. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS); follow-up RemEx-84dj filed for system-contrast parity in the dynamic-color path. (`Theme.kt`; RemEx-ou53.)
- **Pre-Android-12 and dynamic-color-off users now get a RemEx-branded color scheme instead of Compose-template purple.** The static fallback `DarkColorScheme`/`LightColorScheme` were the stock wizard colors (`Purple80`/`Purple40` etc.); they now derive from the amber brand seed (`SplashBrand.Amber` #FFB63D, tonal-spot) via `colorSchemeFromSeed`, and the template `Color.kt` is deleted. The vals are lazily initialized — eager top-level init crashed the app at class-load (Kotlin initializes top-level properties in file order, and the scheme builder reads `MaterialDynamicColorsInstance`, declared later in the file); caught by on-device launch verification, since the crash would have hit *every* user, not just the fallback path. A unit test asserts both fallback primaries carry the warm brand hue (<120°) rather than purple (~260°), standing in for the AC's API<31 visual check that modern emulators can't run. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`Theme.kt`, `Color.kt` removed; RemEx-2xsy.)
- **All navigation transitions now source their motion from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` instead of hand-rolled curves.** `AppNavigation.kt` defined its own `EmphasizedDecelerate`/`EmphasizedAccelerate` cubic-bezier curves and hardcoded ~24 `tween()` literals across the NavHost container transform, the QR-scanner/Pairing modal slides, the Splash/Tutorial/Remote-Desktop crossfades, and the NavigationBar/NavigationRail show/hide. All of it now uses motionScheme springs — enters on the default (emphasized) tier, exits on the fast tier, preserving the old slow-in/quick-out feel — with specs captured in composable scope and closed over by NavHost's non-composable transition lambdas. Navigation logic, routes, and scale/slide geometry are byte-identical; a grep for `tween(`/`CubicBezierEasing` in the file returns zero hits. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`AppNavigation.kt`; RemEx-b1il.)
- **The file-manager toolbar now animates into the expanded search field instead of being replaced in one frame.** Tapping search swapped the entire icon row for a full-width `OutlinedTextField` via a bare `if`/early-return. The swap now runs through `AnimatedContent` with a direction-aware transition — the field fades and grows horizontally out of the search icon's leading edge while the row fades, and shrinks back on close — with enter/exit *and* the container size change all sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` (`defaultSpatialSpec`/`defaultEffectsSpec`, explicit `SizeTransform`). Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileManagerToolbar.kt`; RemEx-ggh0.)
- **The file manager's loading/no-roots/empty/grid/list states now cross-fade instead of hard-swapping.** The app's most-navigated surface swapped five entirely different subtrees through a bare `when`, so folder navigation and the LIST/GRID toggle replaced the whole region in one frame. The region now renders through `AnimatedContent` with a `motionScheme` fade, keyed on a descriptor that snapshots the entry list and view mode — so a new folder listing cross-fades in (the exiting copy keeps rendering *its* folder from the key, not live state), the LIST/GRID toggle animates, and selection-mode toggles and thumbnail arrivals deliberately do not re-trigger transitions. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileTransferScreen.kt`; RemEx-h9rd.)
- **The copy/move folder picker's rows now animate placement as the user navigates folders.** The destination sheet's entries carry `Modifier.animateItem` with the `motionScheme` fast spatial spec, completing the sheet's motion story (its loading/empty/populated cross-fade landed with RemEx-cy76). (`FileManagerSheets.kt`; RemEx-6m0c.)
- **The file-manager sheets' loading spinners are now the expressive RemexLoadingIndicator, and their loading-to-content swaps cross-fade.** The properties sheet and copy/move destination picker were the last two raw `CircularProgressIndicator`s in the app, each swapped for full content in a single frame by a bare null-check/conditional. Both now render `RemexLoadingIndicator` inside `AnimatedContent` with a `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` fade (the picker keyed on a Loading/Empty/Populated descriptor), so metadata arrival dissolves into content and the sheet height animates instead of snapping. The two remaining plain spinners in `ShareToPcActivity` (send button, centered progress) were also swapped per the bead's repo-wide-grep criterion — zero raw `CircularProgressIndicator` call sites remain. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS). (`FileManagerSheets.kt`, `ShareToPcActivity.kt`; RemEx-cy76.)
- **The dashboard coach overlay's five demo animations now source their spring physics from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` instead of each hardcoding its own `MotionScheme.expressive()`.** No visible behavior change today (the app-wide theme already resolves to Expressive in `Theme.kt`), but the five sites previously bypassed the theme entirely, so a future reduce-motion setting applied centrally would have silently had no effect on this overlay. (`DashboardCoachOverlay.kt`; RemEx-4dq0.)
- **The remote-desktop FPS overlay's fade in/out no longer hardcodes a 200ms tween.** Both `PlainAnimatedVisibility`'s `enter`/`exit` specs now source from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastEffectsSpec()`, matching the rest of the app; `RemoteDesktopScreen.kt` now has zero animation-spec `tween(` literals. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-ql9u.)
- **The telemetry view-picker cell's selection border now transitions instead of popping.** Border width (1dp→2dp) and color (`outlineVariant`→`primary`) both changed in a single frame on tap. Both now animate via `animateDpAsState`/`animateColorAsState` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`. (`TelemetryViews.kt`; RemEx-awp6.)
- **The telemetry view-picker's secondary-sensor pane now slides and fades in instead of hard-swapping.** Picking Dual Metric replaced the entire sheet body (view-mode grid → sensor list) in a single frame via a bare `if`. It's now an `AnimatedContent` keyed on `awaitingSecondary` with a directional slide+fade `transitionSpec` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` (captured into a local val, since `transitionSpec` runs outside composition). (`TelemetryViews.kt`; RemEx-l69y.)
- **The two color animations in the Personalization screen's live theme previews (tonal palette row, mini card preview) no longer hardcode a 300ms tween.** They were the only two color animations in the file ignoring `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`, on the exact surface meant to demonstrate the app's own motion tokens. Both now source `animationSpec` from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`; `PersonalizationScreen.kt` has zero `tween(` literals remaining. (`PersonalizationScreen.kt`; RemEx-4woy.)
- **`CommandCard`'s confirm-mode content now fades through instead of popping in fully opaque.** The card already animated its container height via `animateContentSize`, but the title text and the timer-field+Confirm/Cancel-vs-Run block swapped via a raw `if(isAwaitingConfirmation)`, so new children appeared at full opacity mid-resize. Both are now wrapped in a single `AnimatedContent(targetState = isAwaitingConfirmation)` with a pure fade `transitionSpec` from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`. (`RemoteControlScreen.kt`; RemEx-mfc1.)
- **The Connection screen's Discover and Connect buttons now cross-fade their spinner/label swaps instead of popping in one frame.** Both buttons rebuilt their entire content (icon/spinner + text) via a raw `if`, causing an abrupt label and width jump when the async operation started or finished. Both now wrap their content in `AnimatedContent` (`targetState = isDiscovering` / `isConnecting`) with a fade `transitionSpec` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-fbuq.)
- **The Task Manager sort-direction arrow now rotates instead of swapping icons, and its label is localized.** The icon swapped between `ArrowDownward`/`ArrowUpward` in a single frame, and its content description was a hardcoded English literal ("Descending"/"Ascending"). It's now a single `ArrowDownward` icon that rotates 180° via `animateFloatAsState` with `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`, and the description comes from two new string resources (`sort_descending`/`sort_ascending`) translated across all 9 locale files. (`TaskManagerScreen.kt`, `strings.xml` ×9; RemEx-2uvw.)
- **Task Manager's CPU/RAM usage bars now animate to their new value on refresh instead of jumping.** `UsageIndicator` fed raw progress straight into `LinearProgressIndicator`, so every process-list refresh snapped all bars to their new values in one frame. Progress is now wrapped in `animateFloatAsState` with `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`, matching the equivalent gauges already animated this way in `TelemetryViews.kt`. (`TaskManagerScreen.kt`; RemEx-ag7d.)
- **Task Manager's disconnected/loading/list states now cross-fade instead of hard-swapping.** The three full-height regions (disconnected banner, centered spinner, process `LazyColumn`) were driven by a raw `if`/`else if`/`else`, the odd one out on a screen whose list items already animate via `animateItem`. All three now render through a single `AnimatedContent` keyed on a new `TaskManagerListState` enum, with a fade `transitionSpec` from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`; the existing `animateItem` placement spec is untouched. (`TaskManagerScreen.kt`; RemEx-t19x.)
- **Task Manager's load-error banner now animates in/out like its sibling `NotConnectedBanner`, instead of popping via an early return.** The banner now renders inside `AnimatedVisibility` with the same `expandVertically`/`shrinkVertically` on `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()` pattern `NotConnectedBanner` already uses, and remembers its last non-blank message so the shrink-out exit animation still has text to display after the caller clears the error. (`TaskManagerScreen.kt`; RemEx-fqd8.)
- **The Connection screen's preferences-loading state now cross-fades into the connection form instead of popping in one frame.** The whole form (the entire body of the screen) previously replaced a loading spinner via a raw `if`/`else` the instant DataStore finished loading. Both branches are now wrapped in a single `AnimatedContent` keyed on whether preferences have loaded, with a fade `transitionSpec` from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`; the form's own content is completely untouched. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-x4gb.)
- **The five telemetry chart views' "collecting data" placeholder now has real motion and cross-fades into the chart instead of popping in.** `CollectingData` was a static `Text` swapped in and out by five separate bare guards (`if (history.size < 2) { CollectingData(); return }` in `LineChartView`/`AreaChartView`/`DualMetricOverlay`, plus an early `isEmpty()` check in `BarHistogramView` and an if/else in `ValueSparkView`), so cards flipped between dead text and a fully-drawn chart with nothing in between. `CollectingData` now pairs a small `RemexLoadingIndicator` with its text, and a new shared `ChartOrCollecting` helper wraps all five call sites in `AnimatedContent` with a `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` fade — the chart-drawing code itself is untouched. (`TelemetryViews.kt`; RemEx-6ok7.)
- **The file manager's transfer-queue panel now expands and fades into place instead of popping.** `FileManagerQueuePanel` bailed out with `if (transfers.isEmpty()) return`, so the entire elevated panel — header, divider, transfer rows — appeared and vanished in a single frame, shoving the file list below it with no motion. The early return is replaced by `AnimatedVisibility` with `expandVertically`/`shrinkVertically` on `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultSpatialSpec()` plus a fade on `fastEffectsSpec()`; the panel also remembers its last non-empty queue so the shrink-out exit animates over the real rows instead of recomposing to an empty "0 transfers" shell. Reviewed by Opus kotlin-reviewer (PASS after 1 fix round). (`FileManagerQueuePanel.kt`; RemEx-8uaq.)
- **Dashboard cards now fade their selection border and dim-out instead of popping in one frame.** Entering multi-select made the 2dp selection stroke appear instantly (`border = if (isSelected) BorderStroke(...) else null`) and flipped unselected cards' container alpha with no animation, so every card on the canvas popped simultaneously. Border width/color and container alpha now animate via `animateDpAsState`/`animateColorAsState`/`animateFloatAsState` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`; drag/pan/pick-up gesture and selection-toggle logic is untouched. (`DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-mk2n.)
- **Dashboard cards now ramp their shadow on pick-up instead of jumping 1dp→8dp in a single frame.** Elevation now animates via `animateDpAsState` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()` (a spatial spec, matching the elevation's z-axis nature, distinct from the effects specs used for the sibling border/alpha animations). Kept the existing literal `1.dp`/`8.dp` values as-is — a separate, still-open M3-token bead (RemEx-anij) owns replacing them with `CardDefaults.draggedElevation` tiers. (`DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-1tkv.)
- **The dashboard coach overlay's scrim now uses the theme's `scrim` role instead of a raw `Color.Black` literal.** The literal meant the dimming overlay didn't participate in the theme or respond to the contrast setting, unlike every other overlay surface in the app. (`DashboardCoachOverlay.kt`; RemEx-pcy7.)
- **The Connection screen's Help section chevron now rotates instead of swapping icons.** Matches the existing `FaqScreen.kt` pattern: a single `ExpandMore` icon rotates 180° via `animateFloatAsState` with `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`, replacing the instant `ExpandLess`/`ExpandMore` glyph swap. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-mkt5.)
- **The Help card's size-change animation now uses the app's motion tokens instead of the library-default spring.** `animateContentSize()` had no `animationSpec`, so it didn't match the app's expressive spatial motion. Now passes `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`, matching the existing `RemoteControlScreen.kt` precedent. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-pbal.)
- **The Connection screen's status text color and "Paired" badge now animate instead of snapping in.** The status text color switched instantly between `primary`/`onSurfaceVariant` via a raw ternary, and the Paired row popped in the instant the async `PinnedHostStore` lookup completed. The color now goes through `animateColorAsState` with `motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`, and the Paired row is wrapped in `AnimatedVisibility` with `motionScheme` expand/fade enter and shrink/fade exit specs. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-w9nf.)
- **Task Manager's search-field clear icon now scales and fades in/out instead of popping.** The trailing clear `IconButton` appeared/disappeared in a single frame on the first typed character. Now wrapped in `AnimatedVisibility` with `scaleIn`/`scaleOut` + `fadeIn`/`fadeOut` sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`. (`TaskManagerScreen.kt`; RemEx-z2y4.)
- **The pairing screen's auto-PIN-failed hint and Submit button now animate instead of popping.** The hint text appeared with a raw `if`, while the `pairingError` block right below it already used `AnimatedVisibility` with `motionScheme` specs — now both match. The Submit button's spinner/label swap now goes through `AnimatedContent` keyed on `state.isLoading`. Purely visual; no pairing logic touched. (`PairingScreen.kt`; RemEx-8rr9.)
- **The telemetry view-picker's sensor-candidate and view-catalog lists now animate row moves instead of re-indexing content in place.** Both `items()` calls lacked a stable key and `Modifier.animateItem`, so sensors appearing/disappearing from live telemetry swapped row content with no motion. Both now declare a stable key (`it.id` / `it.mode`) and carry `Modifier.animateItem(placementSpec = MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec())`; `ViewPickerCell` gained a `modifier` parameter to carry it through. (`TelemetryViews.kt`; RemEx-4eum.)
- **The remote-control command card grid now springs neighbouring cards into place when one expands, instead of them jumping.** `CommandCard` already animated its own confirm-state expansion via `animateContentSize`, but sibling cards below it snapped to their new positions with no motion. `CommandCard` gained a `modifier` parameter, now wired to `Modifier.animateItem(placementSpec = MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec())` at its grid call site (which already had a stable key). (`RemoteControlScreen.kt`; RemEx-lxvq.)
- **The settings category list now has placement motion, matching the pattern already used in `TaskManagerScreen`/`AppLauncherScreen`.** The list's `items()` call already had a stable key, but `ExpressiveSettingsRow` had no `Modifier.animateItem`. `ExpressiveSettingsRow` gained a `modifier` parameter, now wired to `Modifier.animateItem(placementSpec = MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec())`. (`SettingsScreen.kt`; RemEx-37t0.)
- **The settings row's icon badge color now animates alongside its container color instead of snapping.** `ExpressiveSettingsRow` already animated `containerColor` via `animateColorAsState`, but `badgeColor`/`onBadgeColor` were raw `if(selected)` ternaries directly below it — on selection the row background glided while the icon badge snapped. Both now use `animateColorAsState` with the same `defaultEffectsSpec()`. (`SettingsScreen.kt`; RemEx-prbw.)
- **Remote Desktop's overlay chrome now uses the app's motion tokens instead of library-default springs.** `PlainAnimatedVisibility`'s default `enter`/`exit` were bare `fadeIn()`/`fadeOut()`, and the PC-keys bar's slide-in used spec-less `slideInVertically`/`slideOutVertically`, so overlay motion physics diverged from the rest of the app. Both now source from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` (spatial spec for slides, effects spec for fades); two other call sites (Unlimited/Custom preset notices) already had no per-call override and automatically pick up the corrected default. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-u94d.)
- **Shared-folder rows in Settings now animate in and the list visibly shrinks on removal, instead of popping.** Adding a folder via the system picker or removing one made rows appear/disappear with no motion. Each row now has a stable `key` and fades/expands in via `AnimatedVisibility`; the enclosing `Column` gained `animateContentSize` so removals animate the list's overall height down smoothly, sourced from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`/`fastEffectsSpec()`. (`SettingsScreen.kt`; RemEx-6p3n.)
- **`Theme.RemEx` now has a Material 3 (`Theme.Material3.DayNight.NoActionBar`) parent instead of the Material 2 `Theme.AppCompat.Light.NoActionBar`.** Every Activity's window background, default text colors, and native dialog surfaces were resolving against M2 attributes. The DayNight parent also resolves light/dark from one declaration, so the separate `values-night/themes.xml` override (previously duplicating the same style with a different parent) is gone — light and dark can no longer drift out of sync. All five Activities using this theme are `ComponentActivity`, not `AppCompatActivity`, so the parent swap carries none of the usual AppCompat-hierarchy crash risk. (`values/themes.xml`, `values-night/themes.xml` removed; RemEx-qwcl.)
- **The fullscreen remote-desktop FPS chip's label color is now a theme role instead of a hardcoded `Color.White`.** Its container (`colorScheme.scrim` at 55% alpha) was already theme-derived, but the label wasn't — under this app's dynamic contrast/monochrome theming axes, `scrim` isn't guaranteed to stay near-black, so a fixed white label had no guaranteed contrast pairing. Both container and label now use the M3 inverse-surface pair (`inverseSurface`/`inverseOnSurface`), which is spec-guaranteed to stay legible against the app's current theme regardless of style or contrast setting. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-xj83.)
- **Four settings/telemetry switch rows are now toggleable and announce their state anywhere on the row, not just on the switch thumb.** The full-device-browse and auto-accept-pushes rows in Settings, the dashboard value-overlay toggle in the telemetry view picker, and Remote Desktop's Direct Touch toggle each put a `Switch` beside a label `Text` in a plain `Row` with no semantic role, so tapping the label did nothing and screen readers announced the row as an unlabeled group. Each `Row` now carries `Modifier.toggleable(value, role = Role.Switch, onValueChange)` with the inner `Switch`'s `onCheckedChange` set to `null`, merging the label into the switch's semantics and widening the touch target to the full row. Covered by a new Compose UI test (`DisplayModePickerSheetTest`) asserting the on/off semantic state and the click-to-toggle callback. (`SettingsScreen.kt`, `TelemetryViews.kt`, `RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-nzud.)
- **The dashboard multi-select action bar's four icon buttons (Pin, Reshape, Remove, Exit) now report the Material 48x48dp minimum touch target instead of 44dp.** Each explicitly pinned `Modifier.size(44.dp)`, the only such overrides on `FilledTonalIconButton` anywhere in the app — every other call site already relies on the component's own default sizing, which reserves a 48dp touch target around a 40dp visual container. Removing the override brings these four in line with that existing convention rather than introducing a new one. Covered by a new Compose UI test (`DashboardSelectionActionBarTest`) asserting all four touch targets. (`DashboardInteraction.kt`; RemEx-4xyu.)
- **Removed the five unused Android Studio Material 2 template colors (`purple_200`/`purple_500`/`purple_700`/`teal_200`/`teal_700`) from `colors.xml`.** Leftovers from project generation with no M3 role and zero references anywhere in the module (confirmed by a repo-wide search before deletion); they were a second, non-systematic source of color truth sitting next to the app's real M3 theming. (`values/colors.xml`; RemEx-souv.)
- **Deleted the unused `Modifier.hapticClickable` helper instead of leaving it as a ripple-suppressing trap.** It passed `indication = null` alongside a locally-`remember`ed `MutableInteractionSource` that was never hoisted to the caller, so any surface adopting it would have silently lost its ripple/hover/focus/press state layer with no way to restore one. A repo-wide search found it was never actually called anywhere — dead code sitting in the shared component library where a future author was likely to reach for it. The file's five other haptic helpers (`hapticCommandSent`, `hapticCommandAcknowledged`, etc.), which are genuinely used throughout the app, are untouched. (`HapticModifier.kt`; RemEx-plto.)
- **The dashboard coach-mark panel no longer stacks a hand-picked drop shadow on top of its M3 tonal elevation.** `CoachPanel`'s `Surface` set both `tonalElevation = 6.dp` and `shadowElevation = 8.dp`, reintroducing the Material 2 shadow model on a surface everywhere else in the app expresses depth through tonal color alone. The panel now specifies tonal elevation only. (`DashboardCoachOverlay.kt`; RemEx-62jl.)
- **The static-fallback dark/light color schemes no longer hand-transcribe the five `surfaceContainer*` roles as hex literals.** `DarkColorScheme`/`LightColorScheme` (reached only when dynamic color is unavailable/disabled and no custom seed is set) restated the Compose Material3 baseline's own default neutral tokens verbatim — a copy that had already drifted slightly out of sync with the library's current light-scheme values before this fix. Both factory calls now omit the five `surfaceContainer*` arguments entirely and let the library supply its own current baseline, so the fallback scheme can no longer silently diverge from a future Material3 bump. (`Theme.kt`; RemEx-x0er.)
- **Documented why `SplashCosmicZoom`'s four VFX literal colors stay hardcoded rather than deriving from the active theme.** `SplashBrand.kt`'s header already records the splash as a deliberate, theme-independent brand moment (predates this audit), but the gold second shockwave ring and the three-color chromatic-aberration bloom (a red/cyan/green RGB split that IS the visual effect) live outside that shared object with no pointer back to the rationale. Both sites now carry a one-line comment tying them to the documented brand-lock; no color values, timings, or logic changed. (`SplashCosmicZoom.kt`; RemEx-wxwz.)
- **The QR scanner's `@Preview` camera-feed placeholder no longer pairs raw `Color.Black`/`Color.White` or leaves an unexplained hardcoded English string.** The backdrop and text now use `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.surface`/`onSurface`, and the placeholder text is prefixed `[Preview]` with a comment explaining it is design-time-only scaffolding (`@Preview` composables are stripped from release builds), rather than adding a 9-file string resource for text no user will ever see. (`QrScannerScreen.kt`; RemEx-qtov.)
- **The Tutorial screen's final-page Next/Get Started button swap now animates with the same motion tokens as the page dots beside it.** `AnimatedContent(targetState = isLastPage)` had no `transitionSpec`, so it fell back to the library-default fixed-duration tween while the page-indicator dots two elements away already used `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`. Now sources a pure-fade `transitionSpec` from `motionScheme.defaultEffectsSpec()`, matching the existing `RemoteControlScreen.kt` `CommandCard` pattern. (`TutorialScreen.kt`; RemEx-1fu6.)
- **The Personalization screen's shape-morph slider now shares its spring physics with the Dashboard's equivalent orb morph instead of an ad-hoc `spring(stiffness = ...)` literal.** The only raw `spring()` call left in the file now reads `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.slowSpatialSpec()`, matching `DashboardScreen.kt`'s orb-morph animation. (`PersonalizationScreen.kt`; RemEx-prm6.)

## [2.4.0] — 2026-07-19

### Added

- **PC sensor-card graph types were curated to seven genuinely distinct views, and a value-accuracy bug in the gauges was fixed.** The picker previously offered twelve; five were redundant or weak once rendered side by side and are retired from the menu (enum members kept and remapped so saved layouts load unchanged): **Radial**→Ring (near-identical), **ValueSpark**→Area→Glow (just a line — the value already renders on every card's own plate, so a "value + sparkline" layer adds nothing on PC), **HuePulse**→Glow (flat wash superseded by value-reactive Glow), **Area**→Glow (a second filled-area chart), and **Big Value** (trend-direction only, no magnitude). The seven kept: Bar, Line, Gauge, Ring, LED Meter, Glow, Dual Metric. **Gauge/Ring/LED now fill against the metric's real scale** (percentages 0–100, temperatures 0–100 °C, others 0–peak) instead of the narrow observed min/max band — a steady 19 % CPU load now reads 19 %, not "nearly full with red top segments." **Glow** is value-reactive (accent → warm red as load climbs). **Dual Metric** overlays a real second series (chosen via a searchable picker) and shows an on-card colour **legend** mapping each line to its sensor. (`SparklineControl.cs`, `CanvasView.axaml`, `SensorViewModel.cs`, `SensorGaugeScaleTests.cs` (new, 4 tests); RemEx-km0i.11.)
- **PC cards can overlay a second metric (Dual Metric view) via a searchable picker.** Right-click → "Second Metric" opens a dialog listing every other live sensor with a search filter (a rig can expose hundreds), replacing an unreliable flat context-submenu. The choice persists via `CardState.SecondarySensorId` and resolves to a live view-model for the overlay series. (`SecondMetricDialog.axaml`(.cs) (new), `SensorViewModel.cs`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `CanvasView.axaml`; RemEx-km0i.11.)
- **PC "Auto" view now picks per metric meaning, not just unit string.** A card left on `Auto` resolves its view from the host-stamped `MetricKind` (loads → ring, memory/throughput → value+spark, temps/voltage → line, clock/power → area, fans → bar) and falls back to the old unit-string heuristic only when the kind is `Unknown` — the PC mirror of Android's `bestDisplayModeFor`. (`SensorViewModel.cs`; RemEx-km0i.11.)
- **PC canvas gains a floating multi-select action bar (Pin / Remove / Done).** Ctrl+click cards to select several at once and a bar appears at the bottom of the canvas with the selection count and one-tap group Pin, Remove, and Done — replacing the buried per-card removal path. Group pin and group remove are single undoable operations, routed through the existing `RemoveCardOperation`/undo stack. (`CanvasView.axaml`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `ICanvasOperation.cs`; RemEx-km0i.12.)
- **PC canvas now has a first-visit coach mark.** The first time you open the sensor canvas, a hint card with a curved arrow points at the "Sensors" (add-cards) button and explains dragging, right-click customization, and Ctrl+click multi-select; dismiss with "Got it." A "?" button replays it anytime, and the hint suppresses itself while cards are selected so it never fights the action bar. Seen-state persists per screen in `DashboardProfile.SeenCoachMarks`. (`CanvasView.axaml`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`; RemEx-km0i.13.)
- **Android Home Base now has its own motion-first coach marks — not a port of the PC's.** On first visit, a **six-step** overlay teaches the dashboard through animated demonstrations: (1) an **animated hold-to-lift** demo — a finger rests on a sample card, shrinks to "grab" it as a ripple blooms, then springs back to size as the card pops up and drags (the gesture with no visual affordance); (2) & (3) **hollow pulsing rings** pixel-anchored (via `onGloballyPositioned`) to the real ⋮ menu and + button so they highlight without covering; (4) an **animated view-picker pop** out of a card's ⊞ grid icon; (5) an **animated multi-select group drag** — a finger taps two cards (each springs to a selected highlight), then the group lifts and drags together; and (6) an **animated hold-to-select → action bar** demo (reshape / pin / remove) showing the select branch of the hold gesture. All motion uses the **Material 3 Expressive** `MotionScheme` spring tokens (bouncy `spatial` springs for movement/scale, gentler `effects` springs for ripple/scrim), driven by `Animatable`s in coroutine loops so each phase — and parallel phases like "finger returns as card pops" — is real spring physics. A **"?" button** in the header replays it anytime (disabled while a group is selected); the overlay auto-suppresses while a card is lifted or a group is selected, and its scrim blocks input so no sheet can open beneath a live hint. Per-step caption placement keeps the panel clear of each hint's target. Seen-state persists via `SettingsManager.dashboardCoachSeen`. Requires `material3` 1.5.0-alpha24 for the `MotionScheme` token API. (`DashboardCoachOverlay.kt` (new), `DashboardViewModel.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`, `SettingsManager.kt`, `strings.xml`; RemEx-km0i.10.)

> New user-facing strings for the above were added to the base (English) resources (`Strings.resx` on PC, `strings.xml` on Android); their translation into the other 8 cultures/locales is tracked by RemEx-km0i.16 (localization rollout). Resource fallback shows English for these keys until then.

### Changed

- **Bumped Jetpack Compose `material3` 1.5.0-alpha23 → 1.5.0-alpha24** so the Android coach marks can use the official M3 Expressive `MotionScheme` spring tokens (`fastSpatialSpec`/`slowEffectsSpec`/etc.), which were added in alpha24. No existing code changed; a full release build (incl. `lintVitalRelease`) passes on the new version. (`gradle/libs.versions.toml`; RemEx-km0i.10.)
- **PC sensor cards now bind to telemetry by stable identity, not raw name.** `CanvasDashboardViewModel.ProcessTelemetry` matches an incoming reading to its card by the host-stamped `SensorReading.Id` (falling back to the name for unstamped sensors), the PC mirror of the host's Id/Kind stamping. A sensor the host relabels mid-session keeps its card instead of spawning an orphan, and two same-named sensors (e.g. dual GPUs both "GPU Temperature") stay correctly distinct. Persisted `CardState.SensorId` intentionally stays the name (it also drives the card title and pin key), so this is a runtime-match refinement with no storage migration. (`CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`; RemEx-km0i.14.)

### Fixed

- **Sharing a file to RemEx ("Send to PC") from another app no longer crashes when RemEx isn't already running.** The Android share sheet can start RemEx's process cold, straight into the share screen, with no prior visit to the in-app File Manager and no running transfer job — so nothing had initialized the shared `FileTransferEngine` singleton. `ShareToPcViewModel.confirmSend()` then enqueued directly into it, dereferencing the uninitialized `queueStore` `lateinit` and throwing "lateinit property queueStore has not been initialized" the moment you tapped Send. The share screen now starts the engine in its `ViewModel` init (idempotent; mirrors `FileTransferViewModel`), so the cold and warm paths behave identically. The intermittency was purely process state: it only crashed when RemEx was cold. Confirmed with an on-device cold-start reproduction and crash-buffer trace. (`ShareToPcViewModel.kt`; RemEx-c2pg.)
- **The "RAM Total" telemetry card no longer sits on "Collecting Data".** Neither producer was emitting a total-memory reading: HWiNFO frequently exposes no "Physical Memory Total" sensor, and the Windows WMI path emitted only used/available/load. The host now always stamps a `RamTotalGb` reading — Windows from `GlobalMemoryStatusEx.ullTotalPhys`, Linux from `/proc/meminfo` `MemTotal` — injected only when no producer already supplied one, so a real HWiNFO total still wins. Verified on-device alongside the `MetricKind` round-trip (RAM/CPU/GPU cards now read real %/GB, not the old `1089.0ms`). (`WindowsTelemetryService.cs`, `LinuxTelemetryService.cs`; RemEx-km0i.3.)

### Removed

- **Retired the deprecated HTTP pairing-PIN endpoints `GET /pairing-pin` and `POST /start-pairing`.** These were the trust-all HTTP surface Google Play's ASI scanner flagged; RemEx-1t0b (2.3.0) moved PIN auto-fetch onto the `/ws` `pairing_pin_request` message — gated by the **identical** `TransportTrust.IsTrustedForPinAutoFetch` check — but kept the HTTP endpoints one release so shipped Android apps ≤2.2.4 (versionCode ≤32) kept working. The fleet is now past that (versionCode ≥33), so the HTTP endpoints are deleted entirely. Remote first-time pairing over Tailscale is unaffected — the `/ws` path carries exactly the same capability. The single Android reference was already a documentation comment (the Kotlin caller was deleted in RemEx-1t0b); no client change is required. (`HostBootstrapper.cs`, `PairingHandler.cs`, `PingPongHandler.cs`, `PairingPinRequestHandlerTests.cs`; RemEx-0xp0.)
- **Deleted the dead `PairingThrottle` class.** Its only call site was inside the now-removed `POST /start-pairing` handler, and it was never registered in DI — `GetService(typeof(PairingThrottle))` always returned `null`, so the per-IP throttle never actually ran. The active pairing brute-force defense is unchanged: `PairingService` still caps failed HMAC attempts at 5 per session with a ~120 s session lifetime. A genuine per-IP cross-session throttle on the `/ws` pairing path is filed as a follow-up. (`PairingThrottle.cs` (removed); RemEx-0xp0.)

### Security

- **The trust-all HTTP PIN surface is now fully gone from the host.** With `GET /pairing-pin` / `POST /start-pairing` removed, the only path that ever discloses an active pairing PIN is the `/ws` `pairing_pin_request` message, which fails closed on any non-loopback / non-Tailscale transport (`TransportTrust.IsTrustedForPinAutoFetch`) and can only *relay* an already-active PIN — it can never create a session. This eliminates the last endpoint an unauthenticated LAN/internet caller could probe for pairing state. (RemEx-0xp0.)
