## [2.3.2] — 2026-07-18

### Added

- **The PC Home screen now links to HWiNFO.** A "Get HWiNFO" card sits beside the GitHub and phone-app links and opens HWiNFO's download page. HWiNFO (with Shared Memory Support) is what powers RemEx's detailed PC hardware sensors, so a new user now has a one-click path to the dependency the hardware-monitoring dashboard depends on. Localized across all 9 cultures. (`HomeView.axaml`, `HomeViewModel.cs`, `Strings*.resx`; Dashboard 2.0.)
- **The Android "add card" picker now groups sensors by device.** With a full HWiNFO rig exposing hundreds of readings, the picker shows a section header per device — CPU, GPU, Memory, each drive (`Samsung 980 PRO 2TB [C:]`), Network, Motherboard, Performance — driven by the host's new per-sensor group, so you can find a card instead of scrolling one flat list. (`DashboardScreen.kt`, `DashboardViewModel.kt`; RemEx-km0i.22.)
- **Android telemetry cards now have 10 genuinely distinct views instead of 3 duplicated-looking ones, plus a picker to choose between them.** `TelemetryDisplayMode` grows from 6 values (2 of which rendered pixel-identically) to 11: big value + trend, value + sparkline, ring gauge, true arc gauge, line, area, bar histogram, and three ambient views (hue-pulse tile, LED meter, dual-metric overlay). Tapping a card's view icon now opens a thumbnail grid (`DisplayModePickerSheet`) instead of blindly cycling; each thumbnail renders the real view against the card's live data. `AUTO` is the new default and resolves per-`MetricKind` (`bestDisplayModeFor`), so a fresh or reset card always gets a sensible view for its metric. The curated starter set grows from 3 cards (CPU/GPU/RAM) to 8 (adds RAM total, CPU/GPU temp, and combined network throughput), all seeded `AUTO`. New files `TelemetryViews.kt` (the 10-view catalog + dispatcher + picker sheet) and `TelemetryFormat.kt` (`formatSensor`/`gaugeFraction`/`bestDisplayModeFor`, replacing the old inline formatting and the broken blind `/100` gauge math). The on-disk layout format gains a `{schemaVersion, cards}` envelope with backward-compatible migration from the old bare-array format and old display-mode names. (`DashboardViewModel.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`, `TelemetryViews.kt` (new), `TelemetryFormat.kt` (new); RemEx-km0i.4, RemEx-km0i.5, RemEx-km0i.6.)
- **Telemetry and PC-status cards now render a shape per metric category instead of one universal user-picked shape.** CPU cards default to Square, GPU to Slanted, RAM to Oval, temperature to Gem, network to Pill, Wake-on-LAN to Arch, and PC status to a new plain Rounded Rectangle (shape index 24) — each resolved by `DashboardShapes.resolveShapeIndex` with per-card override → legacy class slider → category default precedence, so an explicit user choice is never silently lost. The old universal Clover4Leaf default is retired via a one-time, idempotent, non-destructive migration (`SettingsManager.migrateShapeDefaultsV2`) that only rewrites an absent-or-legacy-clover stored value, never touches saved card layouts, and isn't undo-tracked. The Personalization shape sliders now show "Auto (per category)" instead of a misleading "Circle" when a bucket is unset, and gained a reset-to-default control plus the previously-missing Remote Desktop bucket. (`DashboardShapes.kt` (new), `Theme.kt`, `SettingsManager.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`, `DashboardViewModel.kt`, `PersonalizationScreen.kt`; RemEx-km0i.7.)
- **Dashboard cards now support renaming and an optional live-value overlay on chart views.** Real HWiNFO sensor names are often long even truncated (e.g. "AMD Ryzen 9 7950X3D CCD1 Temp…"), so the view picker sheet gained a title field — type a short name, or leave it blank to keep the original. It also gained a "Show live value" switch: Line/Area/Bar charts previously showed only a squiggle with no number at all, which read as broken on a low-variance reading; toggling it on adds a small corner value chip. Both are per-card and additive on the existing layout envelope. (`DashboardViewModel.kt`, `TelemetryViews.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-cgiv.)
- **Dashboard cards can now be rearranged without a drag on any card immediately moving it.** A quick drag anywhere on the canvas — including on a card — now pans the board; holding a card for ~0.5s (with haptic feedback) lifts it into a selection, and a floating action bar (Pin/Reshape/Remove/Done) appears for as long as one or more cards are selected. Tapping additional cards while a selection is active adds them to a group move/pin/remove. `DraggableDashboardCard` extracts the per-card gesture handling that used to move a card on any touch. (`DashboardInteraction.kt` (new), `DashboardViewModel.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`, `HapticModifier.kt`; RemEx-km0i.9.)
- **PC sensor cards were redesigned for consistency and given three new visualizations.** The title now sits in its own band at the top of the card, and the value+unit render in their *own* `Viewbox` at the bottom on a floating plate — decoupled from the title, so every card's number now renders at a consistent size instead of shrinking whenever the title was long (e.g. "Average Effective Clock"). The sparkline is more prominent (opacity 0.35→0.6, filling the body beneath the title) while the value stays crisp on its plate above it. Three new graph types join the right-click Graph Type menu, each honoring the card's accent color: **Ring** (a bold full ring gauge), **LED meter** (a segmented bar row whose top segments glow red when the reading runs hot), and **Glow area** (a gradient-filled area with a neon top edge). (`CanvasView.axaml`, `SparklineControl.cs`, `GraphType.cs`, `Strings*.resx` ×9; RemEx-7jjt.)
- **PC sensor-canvas cards gain the per-card customization the Android dashboard has — and their titles are readable again.** Each placed hardware sensor now sits under a semi-opaque backing plate so its name reads clearly over the full-card sparkline (previously the title was a dim purple almost the same hue as the line). Right-clicking a sensor now offers: **Color / Preset** (the 8 `SensorCardTheme` palettes, previously defined but wired to no UI so every card was frozen on one low-contrast scheme), **Rename** (inline edit — real HWiNFO names are long, e.g. "AMD Ryzen 9 7950X3D CCD1 Temp…"), and **Show Value Overlay** (hide the number for a clean ambient card, or keep it on the readable plate). New sensor cards also default to **200×150** — a clean multiple of the 50px snap grid — so they tile edge-to-edge without hand-resizing. All settings persist across restart and are localized across all 9 desktop cultures. (`CanvasView.axaml`(.cs), `SensorViewModel.cs`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `CardCustomizationConverters.cs`, `SensorCardTheme.cs`, `DashboardProfile.cs`, `RemexJsonSerializerContext.cs`, `Strings*.resx` ×9; RemEx-rg28.)

### Changed

- **`scripts/update-local-install.ps1` (the fast "update my installed copy without re-running Setup" loop) no longer references the retired `RemexHost` Windows Service.** RemEx runs as an elevated interactive-session app started by the `RemEx` Task Scheduler logon task, so the script now stops the running app (the task instance plus any stray `Remex.Agent` process, waiting for file locks to release), copies the fresh self-contained publish over the install directory, and restarts via the logon task (or launches `Remex.Agent.exe` directly if the task isn't registered). Added `-NoRestart`, a friendly non-Windows guard, and a note that machine-wide pairing/cert state lives outside the install directory and is never touched. (`scripts/update-local-install.ps1`.)

### Fixed

- **Importing a savefile now refreshes the canvas in place instead of only after an app restart.** `ImportAsync` wrote the imported layout to disk and re-applied theme/language live, but the already-open `CanvasDashboardViewModel` kept its own cards until the next launch, so a restored layout appeared to "not come back." Settings now calls a new `CanvasDashboardViewModel.ReloadFromPersistedLayout()` after a successful import, re-applying the on-disk profile live (non-sensor cards immediately; sensor cards as their telemetry arrives). (`CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `SettingsViewModel.cs`; RemEx-83c4.)
- **The PC canvas layout no longer silently erodes to "whatever sensors are live right now" — a data-loss bug that also truncated exported savefiles.** Sensor cards are restored into the canvas *lazily* (only as each sensor's telemetry arrives), but `TriggerSave()` overwrote the persisted layout's *entire* card list (and pinned-sensor list) with the current in-memory cards. So any save that fired before every sensor had reported — a card drag, snap toggle, resize, pin, or (new) colour/rename change — wrote a truncated layout and **permanently deleted every sensor card whose sensor hadn't reported yet**. Over a few sessions a rich layout collapsed to a handful of cards (observed auto-snapshots marching 3→3→3→0→2 cards, pinned IDs 11→0), and because Export reads the persisted layout, exported `.remexsave` files inherited the truncation. Saves are now non-destructive: persisted sensor cards (and pins) for sensors that haven't been materialized this session are preserved via `CanvasLayoutMerge`, while sensors that *have* been seen keep the live canvas authoritative so genuine deletions/unpins still stick. (`CanvasLayoutMerge.cs` (new), `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `CanvasLayoutMergeTests.cs` (new, 6 tests); RemEx-jwvg.)
- **PC sensor cards no longer forget their chosen graph type (and now their color/title/overlay) after a restart.** `CanvasCardViewModel.ToCardState()` snapshotted only position/size/z-order and silently dropped `DisplayMode`, so the graph type picked from the right-click menu was lost on every reload even though the persisted `CardState` already had a field for it. The save/restore round-trip now carries `DisplayMode` plus the new `CustomTitle`, `ShowValueOverlay`, and `CardTheme`, applied back onto the sensor on layout restore; changes are persisted the moment they happen (the shared per-sensor VM's customization properties trigger a save). All fields are additive and nullable, so existing saved layouts load unchanged. (`CanvasCardViewModel.cs`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `DashboardProfile.cs`; RemEx-rg28.)
- **The four new curated Dashboard cards (RAM total, CPU/GPU temp, network throughput) never showed a chart, even with a live connection.** Their history lookup was keyed by the card's declared sensor id, but those four kinds don't have a stable `cardSlug`, so the sensor that actually carries their readings resolves under a different id — the history map (keyed by the resolved sensor's real id) was always empty for them. Fixed by looking up history by the resolved sensor's own id everywhere, not the card's declared id. (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-km0i.5.)
- **Hardware-monitor cards can no longer show an impossible unit like `1089.0ms` for RAM.** The bug was sensor *matching*, not formatting: the Android app collapsed every sensor whose name contained "memory"/"ram" onto one `sensor:ram` slot and let whichever sorted last win it, so a millisecond-unit timing sensor could hijack the RAM card. The PC host now classifies each sensor by its real reading type and stamps a semantic `MetricKind` + a stable id + the metric's **canonical unit** onto the telemetry payload (Windows HWiNFO/WMI and Linux hwmon alike); the Android app parses that kind and binds each curated card **by meaning** — the RAM card accepts only `RamUsedGb`/`RamLoad`, structurally excluding the `Unknown` timing sensor — and the two divergent lookups that used to disagree (last-wins in the view, first-wins in the view-model) are unified into one `selectSensor` rule. Additive optional wire fields only, so **`protocolVersion` stays 2**: older hosts fall back to the legacy name match and older apps ignore the new fields. (`remex.core/Messages/MetricKind.cs` + `MetricUnits.cs` (new), `TelemetryPayload.cs`, `WindowsTelemetryService.cs`, `LinuxTelemetryService.cs`, `RemexJsonSerializerContext.cs`, Android `ui/telemetry/MetricKind.kt` (new), `DashboardViewModel.kt`, `DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-km0i.1–.4.)

- **PC hardware cards are now accurately categorized, named per-device, and the RAM card reads real data.** The host read only HWiNFO's flat reading list and categorized by each reading's generic label — so 166 of 439 sensors fell to "Other", six drives were indistinguishable (all just "Temperature"), and, because HWiNFO reports "Physical Memory Load/Used" as reading-type `OTHER` (not `USAGE`), the RAM card found no memory sensor and showed nothing. The producer now also reads HWiNFO's **parent-sensor section** and categorizes / groups / names each reading by the real device it belongs to — CPU & GPU by model, each drive with its letter (e.g. `Samsung 980 PRO 2TB [C:] · Drive Temperature`), motherboard, per-interface network, and PresentMon framerate as its own "Performance" group — and rescues the `OTHER`-typed memory readings so RAM load/used bind correctly. Adds an optional `SensorReading.Group` for the upcoming grouped card picker (additive; `protocolVersion` stays 2). (`WindowsTelemetryService.cs`, `TelemetryPayload.cs`; RemEx-km0i.21.)
- **Telemetry cards honor your card-shape customization again instead of being stuck as circles.** The card shape was selected by matching the card id against `"sensor:"`, but the units fix gave non-CPU/GPU/RAM cards their stable HWiNFO id (`hwi:…`), so they stopped matching and silently fell back to the circle default. Shape is now keyed on the card **type**, so every telemetry card follows the shape picker. (`DashboardScreen.kt`; RemEx-km0i.23.)
- **Reconnecting no longer gets stuck needing a "pairing handshake" when the phone reaches the PC on more than one network (e.g. LAN + Tailscale).** The reconnect proof-of-possession secret was stored *and retrieved keyed by the host's IP address*, but the host keeps a single secret per client (regenerated on each pairing). So after a re-pair, the copy the phone had cached under a *different* IP went stale, and reconnecting over that address made the phone answer the host's challenge with the wrong key → host logged "Reconnect proof verification failed" → **gated actions** (process list, remote commands, app launcher) were refused as unpaired. Remote desktop, telemetry and file transfer were unaffected — they authenticate by client-id / SPKI, not the reconnect proof, which is what pinpointed the wrong *key* rather than a wrong socket. The secret is now keyed by the **SPKI certificate hash** — identical across every address of the same host — so any transport uses the one correct secret. Existing pairings need a one-time re-pair to store the SPKI-keyed secret. Hardened alongside: the reconnect proof is sent only on the exact socket its challenge arrived on, so a dual-homed re-point can't cross a proof onto the wrong connection. (`RemexClientManager.kt`, `PairingScreen.kt`, `RemexNativeClient.cs`; RemEx-060g.)

## [2.3.1] — 2026-07-14

### Fixed

- **The phone now authenticates the moment it connects — previously nothing worked until you opened the Task Manager page.** The host issues its reconnect challenge lazily, on the first message that carries the phone's client id — and no screen sent one automatically, so every request was rejected with "Pairing required" until the Task Manager page (coincidentally the only screen that talks on open) happened to trigger the handshake. The app's native core now sends a gate-exempt kickoff `ping` right after the socket opens, which drives the existing challenge → proof handshake to completion within about a second of connecting, on every connect and reconnect — no user action needed, and a never-paired app is unaffected (no client id, no kickoff). The PC also shows it now: a 📱 "Phone connected" entry lands in the Home recent-activity feed whenever a phone pairs or re-authenticates, throttled to once a minute so a flapping network can't flood the feed. No protocol change — `ping` is an existing, pairing-exempt message. (`RemexNativeClient.cs`, `PingPongHandler.cs`, `ActivityService.cs`, `Strings.resx` ×9; RemEx-moqo.)
- **Remote desktop no longer starts black until you switch monitors away and back.** The stream's only guaranteed self-contained keyframe was emitted right at stream start — often before the phone's decoder surface existed — and the phone's one compensating keyframe request was swallowed by the host's 5-second anti-reinit-storm cooldown, so the picture stayed black until a monitor switch forced a full restart. Four layered fixes close the race: (1) the AMD and Intel encoder paths now repeat SPS/PPS on every periodic keyframe (`-header_insertion_mode idr` / `-repeat_pps 1`; `repeat-headers=1` made explicit and unit-tested on libx264, NVENC already repeats implicitly), so a late-joining or rebuilt decoder always configures from the next ~1 s GOP keyframe; (2) the host honors the **first** client keyframe request after stream start even inside the reinit cooldown (single-shot bypass — storm protection intact); (3) the phone's decoder retries its keyframe request every 2 s while waiting for SPS/PPS instead of asking exactly once; (4) the stall watchdog now tracks frames actually **decoded**, not merely arriving — if frames flow but nothing renders, it nudges for a keyframe at 4 s and at 9 s automatically does what the manual workaround did: restarts the stream (bounded to 2 attempts per episode). (`FFmpegH264Encoder.cs`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.cs`, `H264StreamDecoder.kt`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel.kt`, new encoder-args tests; RemEx-vj7b — extends RemEx-p7fz, RemEx-5t4, RemEx-bqc.)

## [2.3.0] — 2026-07-14

### Added

- **The pairing PIN now auto-fills over the pairing connection itself, so the Android app no longer makes a separate trust-all HTTPS call (Google Play ASI compliance).** When you pair over a trusted transport (loopback, or an active Tailscale/WireGuard tunnel), the app fetches the host's active PIN over the **already-open pairing WebSocket** via a new `pairing_pin_request` / `pairing_pin_response` exchange, instead of the old `HttpsURLConnection` to `GET /pairing-pin`. The trust of that socket is established in the app's native .NET core (`libRemexCore.so`) — code Google Play's App Security Improvement scanner does not inspect — so the fix removes the flagged Kotlin trust-all TLS entirely rather than hiding it. The host gate is the **identical** `TransportTrust.IsTrustedForPinAutoFetch(remote, local)` that already guarded `GET /pairing-pin`, and the new path can only *relay* an already-active PIN — it never gains the session-creating power of `POST /start-pairing`. Remote first-time pairing over Tailscale is preserved. The messages are an **optional addition — `protocolVersion` stays 2**; old apps and old hosts fall back to manual PIN entry (or, for shipped ≤2.2.4 apps, the retained HTTP auto-fetch). A new `supportsPinAutoFetch` flag on `pairing_response` lets a new app skip the request entirely against an older host. On a trusted transport that returns no PIN, the app now shows a localized "enter the PIN shown on your PC" notice (`pairing_auto_pin_failed`, 9 locales). (`RemexMessage.cs`, `PairingMessages.cs`, `PairingClient.cs`, `AndroidNativeExports.cs`, `PairingHandler.cs`, `PingPongHandler.cs`, `HostBootstrapper.cs`, `RemexCoreClient.kt`, `PairingScreen.kt`, `strings.xml` ×9; RemEx-1t0b.)
- **Home: an always-on CPU · memory · uptime strip.** The Home page now shows live CPU load, memory load, and system uptime in a strip beneath the welcome — **independent of which sensors you've pinned** (previously the only at-a-glance numbers on Home required pinning sensors on the Canvas first). It reuses the existing ~1 Hz telemetry the host already streams — no extra polling — with a thin load bar under the CPU and memory readouts, and everything blanks to "—" the moment the link drops. (`HomeViewModel.cs`, `HomeView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-fp4b.)
- **Home: a "Recent activity" feed.** Home lists your most recent actions newest-first — files uploaded/downloaded/sent, apps launched, power/remote commands run, **and now what your phone does to this PC** (files it pushes here, commands it runs on it) — with a friendly empty state and a **Clear** button. A lightweight static `ActivityService` records them across the PC app's two DI containers and persists the last 60 events to `%LocalAppData%\Remex\recent_activity.json`, so the feed survives restarts; descriptions are computed from the current language at display time. Phone-initiated events are recorded host-side and de-duplicated against the PC UI's own loopback traffic (via an `isLoopback` check) so nothing is counted twice. (new `Services/ActivityService.cs`, `HomeViewModel.cs`, `HomeView.axaml`, `AppLauncherViewModel.cs`, `RemoteViewModel.cs`, `FileTransferViewModel.cs`, `FileTransferQueue.cs`, `PingPongHandler.cs`, `TransferSessionManager.cs`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-gket, RemEx-godd.)
- **Personalization: a UI-size slider.** A new *UI Size* control (85%–130%) in the Typography section scales the whole interface — text and controls — for readability, applied live via a layout transform over the shell and persisted in the profile. (`MainWindow.axaml`, `App.axaml`, `ThemeService.cs`, `CustomizationViewModel.cs`, `PersonalizationPanelView.axaml`, `DashboardProfile.cs`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-befr.)

### Fixed

- **The font picker no longer freezes the app when a chosen font can't be loaded.** Selecting "JetBrains Mono" — which isn't embedded as a font file; its picker entry pointed at a non-font asset folder — applied an unresolvable font as the whole window's default, which threw at *render* time (outside the construction-time try/catch) and froze the UI thread. Font selection now force-loads the glyph typeface at apply-time and falls back to the default if it can't be materialized, so no font choice can freeze the app; the personalization panel also shows a plain-English "couldn't load" notice when a picked font isn't available. (`SystemFontService.cs`, `ThemeService.cs`, `CustomizationViewModel.cs`, `PersonalizationPanelView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-1h6y.)

### Removed

- **Removed the trust-all `HttpsURLConnection` PIN auto-fetch from the Android app.** `PairingScreen.kt`'s `httpFetchPin` / `tryFetchPinFromHost` opened a `HttpsURLConnection` with an empty-body `X509TrustManager` and a `{ _, _ -> true }` `HostnameVerifier` to read the PIN — the exact shapes Google Play flagged (Unsafe TrustManager, Unsafe HostnameVerifier). Both functions, and every `javax.net.ssl` reference in the file, are deleted in favour of the WebSocket relay above. The dead `POST /start-pairing` phone fallback (never loopback from a phone, so always 404'd) is not replicated. (`PairingScreen.kt`; RemEx-1t0b.)

### Security

- **Eliminated the two ASI-flagged unsafe-TLS shapes and tightened the file-transfer channel's hostname verifier.** Beyond the PIN-fetch removal above, `FileTransferChannelClient.kt`'s `/ws/files` hostname verifier — previously `{ _, _ -> true }`, which ASI would flag next — now verifies the presented leaf certificate against the **same SPKI pin** the TLS handshake already enforced (identity on this self-signed channel is the pinned SPKI, not a DNS name), returning false on any mismatch. Behaviourally a no-op for legitimate connections, but conditional and ASI-compliant. No re-pair is required: no certificate, ACL, or `paired_clients.json` change. (`FileTransferChannelClient.kt`; RemEx-1t0b.)

## [2.2.0] — 2026-07-13

### Added

- **Automatic update check — the desktop app tells you when a newer version is out.** On startup RemEx makes a single anonymous request to the public GitHub Releases API (`api.github.com/repos/clindsay94/remex/releases/latest`), compares the latest release to the running build, and surfaces the result in **About → Software Update**: "You're on the latest version", "Update available: X" with a **Download** button that opens the release page, or a friendly "couldn't check" on failure. A **Check for updates** button re-runs it on demand. The check is **on by default with a Settings toggle** ("Check for updates automatically") to disable it — no telemetry or personal data is ever sent, and a missing network never delays launch. PC-only; the Android client is unaffected. (new `Services/UpdateCheckService.cs`, `AboutViewModel.cs`/`AboutView.axaml`, `SettingsViewModel.cs`/`SettingsView.axaml`, `App.axaml.cs`, `DashboardProfile.cs`, `Strings.resx` ×9.)

### Changed

- **Android file transfer moved off the `dataSync` foreground service onto a User-Initiated Data Transfer (UIDT) job — cleaner Google Play compliance.** Google recommends UIDT jobs over the `dataSync` foreground-service type for network transfers made in response to an explicit user action (Share-to-PC, downloads), and `dataSync` now carries a 6-hour/24-hour runtime cap (Android 15+) plus Play Console justification scrutiny. `FileTransferForegroundService` is replaced by a **`FileTransferJobService`** (`JobScheduler` + `setUserInitiated(true)`) that holds the transfer lifetime and the ongoing progress notification via `setNotification()`; `FileTransferEngine` and all transfer logic are unchanged. UIDT requires Android 14 (API 34) and the entire user base is on Android 16, so this goes **UIDT-only and raises `minSdk` 26 → 34**, letting the `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_DATA_SYNC` permission and the `dataSync` `<service>` declaration be removed entirely (`RUN_USER_INITIATED_JOBS` added). The separate `connectedDevice` foreground service for the main PC connection is untouched. (new `FileTransferJobService.kt`, removed `FileTransferForegroundService.kt`, `AndroidManifest.xml`, `build.gradle.kts`, `ShareToPcViewModel.kt`, `FileTransferViewModel.kt`; RemEx-gvbq.)
- **PC UI redesign — fixed the wide/narrow empty-space layout, deduplicated the Connections card, and merged FAQ back into About.** The non-feature screens placed fixed-width columns (`MinWidth`/`MaxWidth`) inside a centered, non-stretching `WrapPanel`, so wide windows left large side gutters and the columns ended at uneven heights ("pockets of empty space"). A new `ResponsiveColumnWidthConverter` plus a `.page-content` style now make the columns *fill* the available width — two balanced columns when wide, collapsing to a single full-width column below ~900px — and cap+centre page content on ultrawide displays. The duplicated **Connections card is removed from About** and now lives only in Settings (reversing the 2.1.1 fold-into-About). **FAQ is merged back into About** as an `Expander` accordion and removed from the nav rail; the standalone `FaqView`/`FaqViewModel` and the long-dead standalone Connection route are deleted. (`App.axaml`, `AboutView.axaml`, `AboutViewModel.cs`, `SettingsView.axaml`, `ShellView.axaml`, `ShellViewModel.cs`, new `Converters/ResponsiveColumnWidthConverter.cs`; RemEx-12ug.)
- **Diagnostic logs page rebuilt — live non-destructive filtering, subsystem presets, colour-coded entries, and scoped export.** The page was a single flattened string in a read-only `TextBox`, and its level filter was *destructive*: raising Trace→Info dropped the lower entries at the sink, so you could never switch back and see them. Capture and display are now decoupled — the sink retains down to a **Debug** floor (buffer 1000→3000; a *Capture* selector can drop to Trace for deep dives), and the on-screen **display level, search, and preset filters re-project the retained buffer live without discarding anything**. Entries render in a virtualized, colour-by-severity list (time · level · category · message, with expandable exceptions) instead of one text blob. New **preset filters** (Connection & Pairing, Remote Desktop, File Transfer, Input & Commands, Startup & Elevation, Errors & Warnings) scope common diagnoses, and **Export** writes TXT or JSON at a chosen scope — current view, everything captured, a verbosity floor, or any preset — prompting for a filename, independent of what is on screen. (`InMemoryLogSink.cs`, `DiagnosticLogsViewModel.cs`, `DiagnosticLogsView.axaml`/`.cs`, new `LogLevelToBrushConverter.cs`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-12ug.)
- **Personalization: a system + bundled font picker for page-title headers.** A new *Typography* section in the personalization panel sets the page-title font from the app's bundled display fonts (Orbitron — the default — Bungee Shade, Sixtyfour, Nabla, JetBrains Mono, Victor Mono) **plus any font installed on the system**, so a newly-installed Windows/Linux font appears automatically (`FontManager.Current.SystemFonts`). The choice persists in `CustomizationSettings` and applies live via a `PageTitleFontFamily` `DynamicResource` — default defined in `App.axaml`, overridden by `ThemeService` — mirroring how accent colours theme live. Card-header and body font tiers are scaffolded (persisted fields reserved) for a follow-up. (new `Services/SystemFontService.cs`, `DashboardProfile.cs`, `CustomizationViewModel.cs`, `ThemeService.cs`, `App.axaml`, `PersonalizationPanelView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-12ug.)
- **File Transfer: switchable details/icon views with a resizable icon grid.** The remote file browser now toggles between the details list and an **icon grid** whose folder/file glyphs auto-scale (via `Viewbox`), with a size slider (64–176px) on the sort bar driving the tile size. Both views share selection and double-click navigation. (`FileTransferViewModel.cs`, `FileTransferView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-12ug.)
- **App Launcher: drop a shortcut or program onto it to add it.** Dragging `.lnk` / `.exe` files onto the launcher grid now adds them as launcher tiles (name taken from the filename), following the same connected-vs-local save path as the "Add program" dialog. This is a new OS drag-drop handler, separate from the existing pointer-capture tile reorder. (`AppLauncherView.axaml` / `.cs`, `AppLauncherViewModel.cs`; RemEx-12ug.)
- **Home page made welcoming.** The Home "HUD" now opens with a proper welcome — logo, a plain-English intro to what RemEx does, a clickable **command-palette bar (Ctrl + K)** to search screens/actions, and a keyboard-shortcuts strip — above the existing telemetry and quick-action cards. (`HomeView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **Brand mark enlarged to fill its frame.** The `BrandMark` control shrank the terminal-window logo to 80% and centred it, leaving a grey padding ring. It now fits the window geometry's bounds to the control (border-to-edge, proportions preserved), so the `>R|` mark reads clearly — and the sidebar logo is enlarged from 30px to 44px. The tray, taskbar, and installer icons (`icon.png`, `icon.ico` for both desktop and agent, and every MSIX tile) are regenerated with the same fit so the enlarged mark shows everywhere; the splash mark is unchanged (a `groupScale` override keeps its padding). (`Controls/BrandMark.cs`, `ShellView.axaml`, `remex.branding/SplashBrand.cs`, `tools/BrandAssetGen`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **PC Remote screen redesigned.** Power actions are now bigger square **card buttons** with the label on top and a large glyph beneath. The old "send a Wake-on-LAN packet" form is replaced with a **beginner-friendly Wake-on-LAN info card**: it explains what Wake-on-LAN is in plain English, shows **this PC's MAC address** (with a Copy button) for the user to enter into the phone app, and tells them exactly where to put it. (`RemoteView.axaml` / `.cs`, `RemoteViewModel.cs`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **Content (body) font picker — and the sensor workspace now follows it.** The Typography section gained a second picker, **Content Font**, applied as the app's default font (default Inter) — so body text, labels, and the **Sensor Workspace** follow it, while the monospace data readouts keep their fixed-width font. Also fixed the Canvas page title, which hardcoded Orbitron and so ignored the title-font picker. (`App.axaml`, `MainWindow.axaml`, `ThemeService.cs`, `CustomizationViewModel.cs`, `PersonalizationPanelView.axaml`, `DashboardProfile.cs`, `SystemFontService.cs`, `CanvasView.axaml`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **Nav keyboard shortcuts renumbered to match the rail; Files & Logs finally have shortcuts.** `Ctrl+1-5` (Home/Sensors/Commands/Launcher/Processes) unchanged; **`Ctrl+6` → Files**, **`Ctrl+7` → Logs**. The old `Ctrl+6` opened a dead pre-Kotlin "connect to another host" remote-desktop screen (there's no PC client anymore) — that shortcut is removed, so the dead screen is no longer reachable. Also corrected the stale `Ctrl+Shift+P` command-palette hint to **`Ctrl+K`**. (`MainWindow.axaml`, `ShellView.axaml`, `AboutView.axaml`, `HomeView.axaml`.)
- **Removed the Remote Desktop quality/FPS section from Settings.** Those host-side sliders did nothing — the Android client controls stream quality and frame rate. (`SettingsView.axaml`.)
- **About → What's New now reads the latest release notes from the bundled CHANGELOG.** Instead of a fixed hand-written list, it parses the most recent CHANGELOG section (title + summary, developer attribution stripped) so it stays current automatically. (`AboutViewModel.cs`, `remex.desktop.csproj`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **Home page: quick-launch grid + GitHub / Google Play cards.** The four action cards became a full 8-tile **quick-launch grid** (Sensors, Commands, Apps, Processes, Files, Logs, Personalize, About & Help) for one-click access to every screen, plus a link row for **RemEx on GitHub** and **the Android app on Google Play**. (`HomeView.axaml`, `HomeViewModel.cs`, `Strings.resx`; RemEx-aoln.)

### Fixed

- **File transfer was completely broken in both directions — every upload and download failed with "Peer did not respond," even though the PC side looked perfectly healthy.** The Android client receives every file-transfer control reply through the NativeAOT core (`libRemexCore.so`): the native inbound router (`AndroidNativeExports.OnNativeMessageReceived`) classifies each incoming `/ws` message and hands file messages to the Kotlin layer via the `onFileTransferMessage` JNI callback. That router used a **hand-maintained allowlist that only listed the 2.0-era message types**, so when the 2.1 File Sharing Overhaul (protocolVersion 3) introduced `file_transfer_ready` / `_complete` / `_result` / `_control`, the richer browse responses (`file_volumes` / `_search` / `_metadata` / `_thumbnail_response`), and consent/push, the router **silently dropped every one of them**. The phone would send its `file_transfer_offer`, the host would genuinely accept it and send `file_transfer_ready` back (hence "it works on the PC side"), but the reply never reached the phone's negotiation waiter — which timed out with "Peer did not respond" for 100% of transfers, both upload and download. The router now forwards the **entire `file_*` family** and lets each Kotlin collector filter for the types it handles, so this single choke point can no longer go stale as the protocol grows. This also un-bricks v3 browse (volumes / search / metadata / thumbnails) and the phone-as-file-host path, which were dark for the same reason. (The earlier RemEx-ix8d "zombie socket" work targeted the wrong layer — the binary `/ws/files` channel — and did not fix this.) (`remex.core/Native/AndroidNativeExports.cs`; RemEx-y6x6.)
- **A file pushed from the phone to the PC saved one folder too deep — at `root/name/name` instead of `root/name` (every uploaded file became a folder named after itself).** By protocol an upload's `destRelativePath` is the destination *directory* — the host appends the file name itself via `CombineHostRelative(DestRelativePath, FileName)` — but the Android client was sending the *full* `directory/filename` path (Share-to-PC via `resolveDestRelativePath`, the file-manager via `combinePath`), so the host doubled it into `directory/filename/filename` (confirmed in host logs as `photo.jpg/photo.jpg`). The client now sends the destination **directory only**, matching what the host expects — no host change required. (`remex.android/.../share/ShareToPcViewModel.kt`, `remex.android/.../ui/screens/FileTransferViewModel.kt`; RemEx-y6x6.)
- **A pushed file failed with "Transfer incomplete" even though every byte was sent.** The bulk data (on the `/ws/files` socket) and the `file_transfer_complete` signal (on the `/ws` control socket) are independent connections, so the tiny completion message raced ahead of the still-in-flight data. The host would finalize the transfer against a partial file — host logs showed `file_transfer_complete` processed *before* the first data frame even arrived — tear down the receive session, then reject every subsequent data frame with "No active inbound transfer." The Android uploader now **drains all acknowledgements** before announcing completion: it waits until the host has acked every byte (the host always acks the `Final` data frame, advancing the committed offset to the full size) and only then sends `file_transfer_complete`, so the completion can no longer overtake the data. (`remex.android/app/src/main/java/com/clindsay94/remex/service/FileTransferEngine.kt`; RemEx-y6x6.)
- **The page-title font picker had no visible effect.** `PageTitleFontFamily` was defined as an *own key* in `App.axaml`, and an Avalonia resource dictionary's own keys take precedence over its merged dictionaries — so the live value `ThemeService` wrote into the merged override dictionary was permanently shadowed by the App.axaml default. `ThemeService` now writes the chosen font directly onto the application's root resource (the winning own key), which still raises `ResourcesChanged`, so page titles re-font live when you pick a font. (`ThemeService.cs`; RemEx-aoln.)
- **Remote desktop went black (cursor only) + briefly zoomed when the resolution scale changed mid-session, recovering only after a monitor switch.** The Android H.264 `SurfaceView`/decoder was keyed on the encoded resolution, so the instant the host reported new dimensions (a capture-scale change rebuilds the encoder) Compose tore the live decoder down and built an empty one — which came up mid-GOP and dropped every P-frame until the host's next periodic keyframe or a monitor-switch re-bootstrap. The decoder already adopts a new resolution *in place* via its own SPS-change reconfigure (`maybeReconfigureForNewSps`), feeding the fresh keyframe with no gap, so the resolution is no longer part of the `SurfaceView` key; only the video-box size still triggers a rebuild (an unrelated frozen-buffer-scale guard). This is the underlying fragility that 2.1.1 only *mitigated* by disabling adaptive capture scale — deliberate scale/preset changes now reconfigure seamlessly too. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-x3eb.)
- **A freshly-created H.264 decoder that came up mid-GOP stayed black until the host's next *periodic* keyframe.** When the decoder is (re)built while the stream is mid-GOP — e.g. the video-box (`imageSize`) settle that still legitimately rebuilds the `SurfaceView` — its pre-configure wait dropped the undecodable P-frames without asking for anything, so it rendered nothing until the host happened to emit its next scheduled IDR (up to a full GOP later). It now requests an on-demand keyframe **once** the moment it sees it joined mid-GOP, so it configures from the very next frame; the one-shot guard avoids flooding the host. Removes the one-time black/zoom on the first scale change after connecting. (`H264StreamDecoder.kt`; RemEx-p7fz.)
- **Changing the resolution preset while the desktop was static froze the H.264 stream on the host — the encoder starved in a reinit loop until something on screen changed.** The WGC and DXGI capture backends cache the last converted frame and replay it whenever the desktop hasn't changed, but the cache didn't remember what *scale* produced it: after a preset switch the replay was the *old* scale's buffer size, the stream loop rejected it against the rebuilt encoder's fixed input size and reinitialized the encoder — every frame, each reinit costing ~1s — so on a static desktop the stream shipped almost nothing (measured: 1 access unit in 5 s) until the desktop happened to repaint. Both backends now tag the cached frame with its scale and, when a different scale is requested with no new frame available, re-derive a correctly-sized frame from the retained staging texture — so a preset change on a perfectly still desktop re-streams immediately (measured: 83 access units in the same 5 s window). The lock-contended replay path likewise reports a wrong-scale cache as stale instead of live so it is never fed to the encoder. (`WgcDesktopCapture.cs`, `DxgiDesktopCapture.cs`; RemEx-wmm8.)
- **Remote desktop rendered zoomed-in — magnification tracking exactly 1/scale (Smooth+Sharp 0.5 = most zoomed, Balanced 0.75 = least) — after a preset change, until an unrelated surface rebuild "refreshed" it.** The `SurfaceView`'s producer buffer geometry is pinned once in `surfaceCreated` via `holder.setFixedSize(streamRes)`; since RemEx-x3eb the surface deliberately *survives* a mid-session resolution change (the decoder adopts the new SPS in place), so nothing ever re-pinned it — and on the S24 Ultra's Qualcomm `c2` decoder a stale fixed size is **not** overridden by the codec's decoded geometry, leaving every frame composited at the creation-time scale (old ÷ new resolution ≈ 1/scale zoom). The `AndroidView` now re-pins `setFixedSize` to the current stream resolution on recomposition (a no-op when unchanged), so preset changes render at correct geometry immediately. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`; RemEx-oenj.)
- **Adding or removing a File Transfer shared folder in Settings had no effect on what the phone could actually browse.** The Settings writer (`FileTransferRootSettingsService`) built its config path straight from per-user `%LocalAppData%\Remex`, while the live host (`FileTransferService`) reads the machine-wide `%ProgramData%\RemEx` location via `RemexDataPaths` (where the host runs as the elevated session app and the TLS cert already lives). After the one-time migration the two paths decoupled on Windows, so edits in Settings silently never reached the host. The Settings writer now resolves its path through the **same** `RemexDataPaths.ResolveDirectory` / `TryMigrateWindowsFile` helpers the host uses, so both read and write one file; behaviour off Windows is unchanged. (`FileTransferRootSettingsService.cs`; RemEx-y8xy.)
- **Android file-transfer notification now shows live progress — required for the Play `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_DATA_SYNC` declaration.** The `dataSync` foreground service posted a static "Syncing files…" notification with no visible activity, which does not meet Google Play's "noticeable to the user" bar for a data-sync foreground service. `FileTransferForegroundService` now observes the `FileTransferEngine.queue` `StateFlow` and re-posts an **ongoing determinate notification** — direction glyph + filename (`↑`/`↓`), a moving progress bar, and `percent · file N/M` — quantized to whole-percent and `sample()`-throttled so per-chunk frames never exceed the OS notification rate limit; Negotiating/Verifying show an indeterminate bar. The in-app File Transfer screen already renders the M3-Expressive `LinearWavyProgressIndicator` for the same progress. One locale-neutral format string added (`file_transfer_service_progress`, `translatable="false"`). (`FileTransferForegroundService.kt`, `strings.xml`.)
- **Android file-transfer polish — upload confirmations, a proper properties sheet, and per-item actions in grid view.** Five gaps in the phone file manager: (1) a Share-to-PC **upload/push now posts a completion or failure notification** the same way a download does — previously it finished silently; (2) the **properties sheet shows a loading spinner** while the metadata round-trip is in flight (it used to stay invisible until data arrived) **and a thumbnail preview** for image files; (3) the **icon-grid view gained a per-item overflow menu** (download · properties · rename · delete · pin) — those actions were reachable only in list view; (4) the **copy/move destination picker surfaces browse errors** instead of showing a silent empty folder; and (5) a hardcoded English `"Error: …"` status string is now localized across all 8 languages. (`FileTransferEngine.kt`, `FileTransferViewModel.kt`, `FileManagerSheets.kt`, `FileManagerFileItem.kt`, `FileTransferScreen.kt`, `FileManagerLogic.kt`, `strings.xml` ×9; RemEx-bi5z.)
- **Dashboard "Wake PC" now reports whether the packet actually sent.** The dashboard's Wake-on-LAN action optimistically showed "sent" regardless of the native result; it now parses the `WakePc` JNI response's success flag (mirroring the Remote Control screen) so a failed wake surfaces as a failure snackbar instead of a false confirmation. (`DashboardViewModel.kt`; RemEx-nbfb.)
- **The "PC found" snackbar no longer re-fires when you rotate the phone.** The discovery event was consumed only *after* its snackbar finished displaying (a few seconds), so a rotation in that window replayed it. The event is now consumed immediately on autofill and the snackbar runs on the screen's own scope, making it a true one-shot. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`; RemEx-b0lv.)
- **Minor Android polish.** Turkish and Ukrainian now translate the upload-complete notification (was English "Uploaded %1$s"); the copy/move destination picker shows a spinner while a folder loads; and the move-selection icon uses the RTL-aware `AutoMirrored` variant (clearing a deprecation warning). (`values-tr`/`values-uk/strings.xml`, `FileManagerSheets.kt`, `FileManagerToolbar.kt`; RemEx-g3q6.)

## [2.1.1] — 2026-07-12

### Fixed

- **Recovered the splash / brand / navigation revamp that 2.1.0 shipped without.** The 2.1.0 changes (file-sharing overhaul, savefile import/export, launcher rework, Orbitron typography) were committed onto the commit *before* the `feature/android-splash-revamp` branch diverged, so the published 2.1.0 build was missing: the **third splash style (Signal Pong)** and the Skia/Compose splash rework on both PC and Android; the **slate-and-amber brand mark** (replacing the old neon icons); the **navigation-rail glyphs** and the settings-family cluster (Connection · About · FAQ) beneath Settings; and the **FAB → live Personalization popup** (replacing the full-screen Customization page). 2.1.1 merges that branch back in on top of everything in 2.1.0 — nothing from 2.1.0 was dropped. (Merge `0bac425`.)
- **About page referenced a removed `FaqItems` binding.** FAQ is now its own `FaqView` destination; the orphaned inline FAQ section in `AboutView` (bound to the since-removed `AboutViewModel.FaqItems`) is removed. (`AboutView.axaml`.)
- **PC app crashed on launch after the brand refresh.** Two views still referenced the deleted old-neon `New-REMEX.png` (`ShellView.axaml`, `AboutView.axaml`), so Avalonia threw `FileNotFoundException` when showing the window. Both now render the new native-vector `BrandMark` control, matching Home — so the sidebar, Home, and About all show the slate/amber mark and the app launches. (`ShellView.axaml`, `AboutView.axaml`.)
- **Default host address used plaintext `ws://`, which never connected.** The host serves TLS only on `/ws`, so a fresh install's default `ws://localhost:5005/ws` was refused until hand-edited. The default is now `wss://localhost:5005/ws`. (`DashboardProfile.cs`.)
- **Remote desktop stuttered/black-flashed periodically (adaptive capture scale).** The adaptive capture-scale controller (added this cycle) was on by default and changed the host capture scale mid-stream; each change resizes the encoded frame, which forces the Android client to rebuild its `SurfaceView` + H.264 decoder — so as the controller oscillated the stream hitched and briefly blacked out every few seconds. Adaptive scale is now **off by default** on both sides (the feature is retained, dormant, and can be explicitly opted into); presets use their stable fixed scale. (`RemoteDesktopHandler.cs`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel.kt`.)
- **File transfer: dragging a batch of files in while a transfer was running could fail the transfer and drop the connection.** Each file chunk and every folder browse / ping / launcher-sync / layout-sync went out over the shared WebSocket without serialization; when the queue advanced to the next file at the same moment a browse fired, two concurrent `SendAsync` calls violated the socket's single-writer contract — faulting the connection and orphaning the partial. All outbound sends now serialize through one per-connection lock. (`ConnectionViewModel.cs`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `AppLauncherViewModel.cs`.)
- **File transfer: unified the host path-security validator.** Browse, transfer, delete, rename, and hash now resolve paths through the same shared validator as copy / move / search / metadata / thumbnail, enforcing the full restricted-system-path denylist (`/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/run`, `/boot/efi`) uniformly instead of a narrower local copy. (`FileTransferService.cs`.)

### Changed

- **Connection screen folded into About RemEx.** The single-card Connection screen (status, host address, discover-hosts, QR, pairing PIN) now lives as a section on the About page and is removed from the left nav rail; the settings-family cluster is now Settings · About · FAQ. The controls bind to the same shared `ConnectionViewModel`, so all behavior is unchanged. (`AboutView.axaml`, `AboutViewModel.cs`, `ShellView.axaml`.)

## [2.1.0] — 2026-07-11

### Added

- **File sharing overhaul — fast, resumable transfers over a dedicated binary channel.** RemEx 2.1 moves file transfer onto a new `protocolVersion 3` binary WebSocket channel (`/ws/files`) that streams raw file bytes instead of base64 JSON. Transfers now **resume after an interruption** — drop Wi-Fi mid-transfer, reconnect, and it continues from where it stopped, re-verifying the partial with SHA-256 — and run through a **FIFO transfer queue** that survives the app closing, the phone sleeping, and the PC restarting (one active transfer per direction; an Android `dataSync` foreground service keeps them alive). Every completed file is checked end-to-end with a full-file SHA-256; a mismatch or cancel discards the partial and leaves nothing behind. (`FileFrameEnvelope`, `FileFrameCodec`, `TransferSessionManager`, `TransferQueueService`, `FileTransferEngine`, `FileTransferChannelClient`.)
- **File sharing overhaul — full file managers on both the PC and the phone.** The old browse/upload/download list is rebuilt into a proper file manager on both sides: tappable breadcrumbs, sort by name/size/date, search, multi-select copy/cut/move, new folder, a properties pane with image thumbnails, a list⇄grid toggle (Android), a live transfer-queue panel, pull-to-refresh (Android), and drag-and-drop upload from the OS file manager (PC). New file operations — **copy, move, make folder, recursive search, file properties (metadata), and image thumbnails** — are served by the PC host and the Android host alike, each resolved through the shared path-security helper. (`FileTransferView`, `FileTransferScreen`, `FileManagerLogic`, `FileTransferService`, `ThumbnailService`, `FileHostHandler`.)
- **File sharing overhaul — opt-in full-device browse.** With your explicit permission, one device can browse the *other's* whole file system — all Windows drives, all Linux mounts, or (on Android) a folder tree you pick — instead of only the shared folders you chose. It is **off until you turn it on**, and a drives/volumes quick-access row appears only once the grant is held. (`file_volumes_request`, `VolumeEnumerator`, `FilePathValidation`.)
- **File sharing overhaul — Android is now a share target with open-after-download.** Share files to RemEx from any Android app (`ACTION_SEND` / `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE`) to push them straight to a folder on your PC, kept running by the `dataSync` foreground service so the transfer survives the share screen closing. A finished download posts a notification with an **Open** button that opens the file in the right app (MIME inferred from the file name, served via a `FileProvider`). (`ShareToPcActivity`, `ShareDestinationResolver`, `ShareMimeInference`, `FileOpener`, `FileTransferNotificationManager`.)
- **Full program savefile export/import (`.remexsave`).** Manual Export/Import in Settings plus a silent rolling auto-snapshot (every 30 s of state change, 5 kept, in `%LocalAppData%\Remex\backups` on Windows / `~/.local/share/Remex/backups` on Linux) let a reinstall or a brand-new install restore your telemetry canvas layout, launcher apps, settings, and theme customization in one step. A first-run restore prompt offers to reapply the most recent snapshot when RemEx starts with no existing local state, and import is applied section-by-section so one corrupt section can't block the rest. **Secrets are never included** (see Security below), so you always re-pair after a restore. (`RemexSavefileService`, `RemexSavefile`, `RestorePromptWindow`.)
- **App launcher: edit, sort, and drag-to-rearrange.** Existing launcher entries can now be edited in place (name, target, color, icon) instead of only added or removed, and a drag gesture — reusing the app's existing pointer-capture pattern (long-press threshold, move threshold, scale/opacity feedback) — replaces the old ←/→ arrow-shuffle reorder with direct drag-to-position. The add flow and automatic icon extraction are unchanged. (`AppLauncherViewModel`, `AppLauncherView`, `LauncherLayoutMath`.)

- **Desktop UI: dedicated Connection and FAQ screens in the left nav rail (RemEx-uqpd).** Connection (`ConnectionView`) is a proper destination bound to the shared `ConnectionViewModel` — live status, host address, discover-hosts, connect/disconnect, host-runtime summary + latency, and the QR-code / pairing-PIN panels. FAQ (`FaqView` + new `FaqViewModel`) is extracted from the About page into its own destination; its Q/A list rebuilds live on language change. Both sit in a new "settings-family" cluster in the nav rail beneath Settings (Settings · Connection · About · FAQ), each with its own glyph. (`ConnectionView`, `FaqView`, `FaqViewModel`, `ShellViewModel.NavigateToConnection`/`NavigateToFaq`, `ShellView.axaml`.)
- **Desktop UI: live Personalization popup on the FAB (RemEx-uqpd).** The bottom-right floating button now opens a right-side slide-in Personalization panel (`PersonalizationPanelView`) holding **every** visual/customizable option — theme presets, accent color, corner-radius / glass-opacity / glow sliders, atmosphere (background / window-opacity / scheme / splash / hardware-sync), **and the language selector** — as glass-cards, live-applied through the existing `CustomizationViewModel` so changes preview against whatever screen is open (no more navigate-tweak-return). The FAB icon changed from the gear to a palette. New localization keys (`Nav_Commands`, `Nav_Processes`, `Nav_Connection`, `Nav_Faq`, `Personalize_*`, `Conn_*`) across all 8 languages. (`PersonalizationPanelView`, `ShellView.axaml`, `ShellViewModel.ToggleSettingsPanel`/`NavigateToCustomization`.)
- **Desktop UI: the GlowStrength slider now previews live across every screen.** `ThemeService` drives a shared `CardShadow` resource — a base drop shadow plus an accent-tinted neon glow whose blur/spread/alpha scale with the slider (0 = no glow) — that every `Border.glass-card` consumes, so glow changes preview against whatever screen is open instead of only taking effect after a restart. (`ThemeService`.)
- **Android: "Preview Animation" button in Personalization.** The splash-style picker gains a Preview button that plays the selected splash animation full-screen in a dialog (with a Close affordance), so users can see Original Scan / Cosmic Zoom / Signal Pong before committing. (`PersonalizationScreen`, `personalization_splash_preview` / `personalization_splash_preview_close`.)

### Changed

- **Orbitron typography and sidebar branding.** The mismatched Bungee Shade / Sixtyfour page headers across Remote, App Launcher, Task Manager, Canvas, Diagnostic Logs, File Transfer, About, and Settings are unified into a single Orbitron-based header system (Black-weight titles, Medium-weight subtitles), and the sidebar's 3×3-square glyph + hard-coded "REMEX" text is replaced with the RemEx logo plus an Orbitron "RemEx" wordmark. Verified across all four themes (CyberNOC, Monolith, SolarFlare, BaseDarkGlass).
- **Adaptive page widths.** About, Settings, and Diagnostic Logs no longer clamp to a narrow fixed-width column on wide/4K displays — Settings and About center and widen their two-column layouts, Diagnostic Logs drops its width cap, and Home's sensor panel widens and centers instead of hugging the left edge. Pages that were already full-stretch (Canvas, Remote, App Launcher, Task Manager, File Transfer, Customization, Remote Desktop) are unchanged.
- **File-transfer protocol bumped to `protocolVersion 3` — additive and backward-compatible.** The minimum supported version **stays 2**: every new `RemexMessage` field is optional, and a v2 phone or host keeps the untouched legacy base64 file-transfer path and is never sent v3-only messages. Every v3 feature is gated on capabilities the host advertises, so nothing v3 ever reaches a v2 peer, and the v2 legacy path is retained for this release. The launcher, savefile, typography, and page-width changes are all wire-unchanged; `LauncherSync` / `LauncherAdd` / `LauncherRemove` message shapes are unchanged. (`ProtocolVersionPolicy`, `RemexMessage`, `RemexJsonSerializerContext`.)

- **Desktop UI: nav rail relabel + bold gradient treatment (RemEx-uqpd).** "Remote" → **Commands** (the remote power/control panel — shutdown/restart/reboot-to-UEFI) and "Tasks" → **Processes** (running-process list), both with clearer glyphs, disambiguating them from unrelated concepts. The rail gains a theme-accent vertical gradient, larger (22px) accent-tinted glyphs, and a stronger active-item pill (Direction C "bold"). Language moved out of Settings → General into the Personalization popup. (`ShellView.axaml`, `App.axaml`, `SettingsView.axaml`.)
- **Desktop UI: nav-rail glyph column widened 48 → 64 px** so the accent-tinted icons sit centered in the collapsed rail instead of hugging the left edge. (`ShellView.axaml`.)
- **Android: About and Tutorial screens use the vector launcher mark** (`ic_launcher_foreground_vector`) instead of the rasterized foreground, matching the new brand mark at any size. (`AboutScreen`, `TutorialScreen`.)


### Fixed

- **Host-side launcher reorder was not saved.** A drag-reorder performed while an Android client was connected updated the in-memory order and echoed it back over the socket, but was never written to `launchers.json` — the host's inbound message handler had no `LauncherSync` case (only `LauncherAdd` / `LauncherRemove`) — so the reordering was silently lost on the next host restart. The host now persists an inbound `LauncherSync` the same way it persists add and remove. No wire-format change. (`PingPongHandler`.)

- **Remote desktop, Android: opening the PC-keys bar or the soft keyboard no longer blacks out the stream (RemEx-2y31).** Both used to shrink the video box — the bar was a layout sibling *below* the video (stealing height whenever it appeared, plus the IME's height via its `imePadding`), and on pre-Android-15 devices the screen additionally flipped the window out of edge-to-edge so the IME resized the whole window. Since the video box's size (`imageSize`) keys the H.264 `SurfaceView`, every open/close tore down the decoder and showed black until the next keyframe. The bar is now a **floating, draggable PC-keys pill** overlaid *on top of* the stream (grip handle to move it anywhere; position persists and re-clamps across rotation; still rides above the IME and auto-appears with it), and the screen stays edge-to-edge always — matching `MainActivity.enableEdgeToEdge()` — so neither the bar nor the IME ever resizes the video surface. Also fixes a latent bug where leaving the remote-desktop screen force-disabled edge-to-edge for the whole app (`onDispose` restored decor-fits to `true`). New `cd_move_pc_keys` accessibility string in EN + all 8 locales. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`: `PcKeysBar`, `clampedPcKeysOffset`, overlay lane in the video box, `DisposableEffect` decor-fits handling.)

### Security

- **Consent-gated file access.** Full-device browse and incoming pushes always require explicit consent. The PC raises an Allow/Deny dialog with an optional "remember this device"; Android shows both a high-priority consent notification (Allow/Deny actions) and an in-app dialog with a live countdown. A dismissal or a **60-second timeout resolves as a clean deny**, and a push prompt shows the offered file names and total size before you agree. (`IFileTrustService`, `FileConsentDialog`, `FileConsentCoordinator`, `FileConsentManager`.)
- **Per-device trust grants, revocable.** "Allow full browse" and "auto-accept incoming" are remembered per paired device and surfaced in Settings on both platforms; **unpairing a device revokes all of its file-sharing grants.** (`FileTrustService`, `SettingsManager`.)
- **Path-escape protection on every operation.** Browse, transfer, copy, move, make-folder, search, metadata, and thumbnail all resolve their paths through one shared validator that blocks directory traversal (`..`), sanitizes filenames, and enforces an unconditional block on sensitive Linux system paths (`/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/run`, `/boot/efi`) that can never be disabled. Root-escape attempts are rejected rather than served. (`FilePathValidation`.)
- **Secrets never leave in savefiles.** `.remexsave` exports and auto-snapshots exclude `cert.pfx`, `paired_clients.json`, `pinned_hosts.json`, and the keep-session-unlocked flag, so a backup can never carry the pairing credentials that authorize a device — the user always re-pairs after a restore. (`RemexSavefileService`.)

### Removed

- **Stale "Windows Service" / "Linux Service" onboarding tutorial pages.** These two steps of the first-run tutorial told new users to install a background service — obsolete since the RemEx-aep single-process migration removed that architecture entirely. The remaining tutorial and glossary pages were renumbered to close the gap; 39 now-orphaned localization keys were removed from all 9 PC locale files. (`ShellViewModel`.)

- **Desktop UI: full-screen Customization page (RemEx-uqpd).** Its controls now live in the live Personalization popup; `CustomizationViewModel` is retained and re-hosted there, so no functionality was lost. (`CustomizationView.axaml` deleted.)
- **Desktop UI: the "Remote card shape" corner-radius slider** — it targeted a control that no longer has a dedicated card surface — plus the four dead `Main_*` localization accessors (`Main_HostWatermark` / `Main_Latency` / `Main_Status` / `Main_Title`) left over from a removed view. (`PersonalizationPanelView.axaml`, `Strings.Designer.cs`.)
---

## [2.0.1] — 2026-07-11

### Added
- Remote desktop: **`encodedWidth`/`encodedHeight` on `desktop_meta` and `desktop_stream_descriptor`** — the true post-scale, even-aligned encoder output size, distinct from the existing `pixelWidth`/`pixelHeight` (unscaled screen size). The Android H.264 `SurfaceView` now sizes itself from these instead, and the host re-advertises them whenever the encoded size changes mid-stream (preset switch, adaptive-scale step). Additive, no `protocolVersion` bump; hosts predating the fields fall back to `pixelWidth`/`pixelHeight`. (`DesktopMeta`, `DesktopStreamDescriptor`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.GetEncodedFrameSize`.)
- Remote desktop: **adaptive H.264 capture scale** (RemEx-eo0f). A new pure, clock-injected state machine (`AdaptiveScaleController`) holds the sharpest capture scale the host can sustain at the target FPS instead of a fixed preset value — starting at the preset's configured scale and stepping up a coarse ladder (0.4/0.5/0.65/0.75/0.85/1.0) when the encoder proves it can keep up (≥98% of target FPS for 5 consecutive ~2s windows with no output drops), stepping straight back down on any encoded-output overflow or two consecutive windows under 90% of target FPS. 5s minimum between changes; a step-up that fails within its own hold period is barred from retrying for 60s (anti-oscillation). Opt-in via the new `DesktopConfig.AdaptiveScale` field (explicit client value wins; default is on for H.264 at `targetFps >= 90`). Android's SMOOTH_SHARP and UNLIMITED presets opt in explicitly; BALANCED/DATA_SAVER/CUSTOM defer to the host's default rule. (`AdaptiveScaleController`, `IH264Encoder.AcceptedInputFrameCount`/`DroppedAccessUnitCount`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel.buildConfigJson`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **always advertise cursor-state/shape/frame-envelope capabilities**, independent of display-target selection — previously the client only sent `clientCapabilities` at all once a display option was resolved, so a fresh connection with no display picker interaction fell back to the legacy fixed-arrow cursor path. `supportsDisplaySelection` (and the paired `desktopProtocolVersion`/`captureMode`) stay gated on a resolved catalog option, since the host rejects an unpaired `SupportsDisplaySelection` advertisement. (`RemoteDesktopViewModel.buildConfigJson`.)

### Fixed
- Build: **`dotnet build`/`dotnet test Remex.sln` failed with XA5300 ("Android SDK directory could not be found") on any machine without the Android SDK configured**, because `remex.core.csproj` unconditionally multi-targeted `net10.0-android` alongside `net10.0`. `net10.0-android` is now only included in `TargetFrameworks` when `ANDROID_HOME`, `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT`, or an explicit `AndroidSdkDirectory` MSBuild property is set (mirroring how `build-remex.ps1` already resolves the SDK dir) — plain solution builds/tests now succeed without Android tooling installed, while `build-remex.ps1`'s Android build path (which always has one of those set) is unaffected. (`remex.core.csproj`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **doubled cursor when WGC can't disable its own cursor compositing.** The WGC capture session's `IsCursorCaptureEnabled = false` projection setter was wrapped in a Debug-only try/catch — on failure, WGC silently baked the OS cursor into every captured frame on top of the client's own rendered cursor (shape + position streamed separately), producing a "trail of cursors" smear under constant-QP encoding. Added a COM fallback (`IGraphicsCaptureSession2::put_IsCursorCaptureEnabled`) for projection versions where the setter throws, and a Warning-level log (not Debug) when both paths fail so the doubled cursor is diagnosable from the host log. (`WgcDesktopCapture.TrySuppressCursorCapture`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **stream stayed cropped until a fullscreen toggle (or other unrelated re-layout) forced a rebuild.** The H.264 `SurfaceView`'s buffer→view content scale freezes at surface creation; if the surface was first created before real desktop metadata arrived (at the 1920×1080 placeholder) or before the encoded size was known, it stayed mis-scaled until something else happened to force a `key()` rebuild. Surface creation is now gated on `desktopMetaReady`, and the surface sizes itself from the new `encodedWidth`/`encodedHeight` fields instead of the unscaled `pixelWidth`/`pixelHeight`. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **macroblocking/blur on high-motion frames at high FPS.** The H.264 encoders (NVENC/QSV/AMF/x264) ran pure constant-QP with no `-maxrate`/`-bufsize` anywhere, so bitrate scaled unbounded with FPS×motion; when it overran the bounded encoded-output channel, `DropOldest` silently evicted access units mid-GOP, corrupting the reference chain until the next keyframe. Rate control is now quality-anchored VBR/QVBR with a QP-derived `-maxrate`/`-bufsize` ceiling (`FFmpegH264Encoder.ComputeMaxRateBps`/`ComputeBufSizeBps`), GOP now scales with FPS (`ComputeGop`, clamped 30–120) instead of a fixed 60, and output-channel overflow is now detectable (`DropWrite`) and recovers cleanly by dropping until the next IDR and requesting one, instead of forwarding a broken reference chain. VA-API (Linux) keeps its existing CQP rate control pending separate driver validation. (`FFmpegH264Encoder`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **cursor shape change (e.g. arrow → I-beam) took up to ~111ms to reach the client.** Shape re-sync previously only ran on the 10Hz "slow tick" alongside position streaming. The host now cheaply polls the current cursor handle (`WindowsCursorShapeCapture.TryGetCursorHandle`) on every 90Hz position tick and re-syncs immediately on a handle change, cutting worst-case shape-change latency to ~11ms; the 10Hz tick still runs unconditionally to catch animated cursors that mutate pixels without changing their handle.

### Changed
- Remote desktop, Windows: encoded-output overflow (`FFmpegH264Encoder`'s bounded output channel) no longer silently corrupts the stream on eviction — see Fixed above. This is a behavior change worth calling out separately: a sustained overflow now produces a brief freeze (bounded by one GOP) followed by a clean keyframe recovery, instead of macroblocking that could persist until the next natural GOP boundary.

## [2.0.0] — 2026-07-04

### Added
- Remote desktop: **an "Unlimited/uncapped" frame-rate option** across the Android client and the desktop UI, raising the shared target-FPS ceiling from 120 to 360 (RemEx-z377). The Android custom-preset FPS slider (`RemoteDesktopScreen`) and the pre-connect slider (`ConnectionScreen`) now range 1–360; any value above 240 (`DesktopConfig.PacedMaxFps`) displays as "Unlimited" instead of a number — 240 already exceeds every consumer display's refresh and the H.264 encoder is throughput-bound well below it at real resolutions, so it is effectively uncapped while the host frame pacer stays safe (1000/360 ≈ 2.78 ms per tick, so a static screen can never busy-spin the capture loop). The authoritative wire clamp (`DesktopConfig.TargetFps`) and the host cap (`RemoteDesktopHandler.MaxTargetFps`) both move to the shared `DesktopConfig.MaxTargetFps = 360`; hosts predating the bump clamp an incoming 360 straight back to 120, so the change is backward-compatible with **no `protocolVersion` bump**. The Android "Unlimited" preset now requests the real 360 ceiling (was pinned at 120). On the desktop UI (`remex.desktop`), the two former FPS ComboBoxes (in-session toolbar + Settings) are replaced by 1–360 sliders via a new `FpsDisplayConverter`, and capture **Quality and Scale now default to 100%** (`DashboardProfile.StreamQuality`/`StreamScale`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `SettingsViewModel`). The "Unlimited" label is localized across all 8 languages on both platforms. (`DesktopConfig.MaxTargetFps`/`PacedMaxFps`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`/`RemoteDesktopScreen`/`ConnectionScreen`, `FpsDisplayConverter`, `RemoteDesktopView.axaml`, `SettingsView.axaml`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **translated remote-desktop preset labels and warning messages to 8 languages** (RemEx-c93). The remote-desktop preset chip labels, warning messages, and resolution scale strings are now translated into Spanish (es), French (fr), Hindi (hi), Indonesian (in), Polish (pl), Portuguese - Brazil (pt-rBR), Turkish (tr), and Ukrainian (uk) to fulfill ship-quality localization. (values-es, values-fr, values-hi, values-in, values-pl, values-pt-rBR, values-tr, and values-uk strings.xml.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **the "PC keys" modifier bar is now independent of the soft keyboard and gained Ctrl** (RemEx-yi8o). Previously the bar only showed alongside the IME (which could cover it) and had no Ctrl button. It now toggles on its own via the toolbar (`pcKeysBarVisible`), stays visible while explicitly opened even after the IME is dismissed, and renders above the IME via `imePadding()`. Shift/Ctrl/Alt/Win are now full latching chips (`ModifierState`: OFF → ARMED (applies to the next keypress only) → LOCKED (held down on the host until cycled back to OFF), `cycleModifier`). Typing a single alphanumeric character while a modifier is armed/locked now sends it as a real chord (Ctrl+C/V/A/Z, etc.) via `sendKeyPress` instead of as literal text; every other edit (multi-character, punctuation, autocomplete) keeps the existing text path unchanged. All 4 new content-description strings are translated across all 8 supported locales. **Known gaps, filed separately:** AltGr is not implemented (RemEx-9krr); an ARMED modifier can silently persist onto an unrelated later keystroke if the very next input isn't a plain single-character insert, e.g. punctuation or autocomplete (RemEx-rimh). (`ModifierState`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel.cycleModifier`/`sendKeyPress`/`releaseAllModifiers`, `RemoteDesktopScreenContent`, `singleCharacterInsertion`, `asciiAlphanumericVirtualKeyCode`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **quality presets rebuilt to spec — Unlimited / Smooth & Sharp (default) / Balanced / Data Saver / Custom** (RemEx-vj31). The old 3-chip set (Performance/Balanced/Crisp) had labels and semantics that didn't match the product plan and offered no way to hand-tune capture scale. Each preset is now a named `{quality, targetFps, scale}` bundle (`DesktopPreset`, `DESKTOP_PRESET_BUNDLES`): Unlimited (100%/120fps/100% scale — the uncapped technical ceiling, which this capture pipeline can't always sustain, so it carries a persistent "may vary" info caption plus a one-time Snackbar overflow warning shown only the first time it's selected); Smooth & Sharp (95%/120fps/50% scale, new default — the scale that reliably sustains steady 120fps); Balanced (85%/60fps/75% scale); Data Saver (60%/30fps/65% scale). Selecting **Custom** reveals raw quality/fps/scale sliders (a scale slider is new — previously scale was only reachable via presets); selecting a named preset hides them. The preset id persists in DataStore (`desktop_preset` key) alongside quality/fps/scale, so the sheet reopens showing the correct selection instead of re-deriving it from raw numbers (which couldn't distinguish Custom from a coincidental match). Installs upgrading from the old 3-preset system — which have leftover quality/fps/scale but no persisted preset id — resolve to Custom rather than falsely claiming a named preset that doesn't match their actual settings. (`DesktopPreset`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel.applyDesktopPreset`/`selectCustomPreset`, `SettingsManager.RemoteDesktopPreferences`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **hardware H.264 (NVENC) is now the real steady-state codec on KDE Wayland — ~68 FPS per-monitor streaming, up from 12.5 FPS CPU MJPEG** (RemEx-nadp, RemEx-lq6h). Four pieces landed together: (1) the PipeWire stream now requests an explicit `maxFramerate` range (1–120), without which KWin delivers on a slow damage-driven cadence (~12 FPS) no matter how fast the encoder is; (2) the captured full-virtual-desktop frame is **cropped to the selected monitor** before encoding, so a single 2560×1440 monitor stays under the 4096-px H.264 hardware limit and needs no CPU downscale (the "both screens" 5120×1440 surface still auto-downscales to fit); (3) per-monitor display enumeration on KDE Wayland via `kscreen-doctor` (true geometry + primary), feeding the display selector, with a fresh session defaulting to the primary monitor; (4) monitor selection works mid-session and on any display server because the crop is applied post-capture. Verified end-to-end on CachyOS/KDE: 67–71 FPS H.264 on either monitor, 49–57 FPS on the full dual-monitor desktop. (`pipewire_capture.c`, `LinuxScreenCaptureService`, `LinuxJpegEncoder`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **drift-free absolute mouse injection through the unified RemoteDesktop+ScreenCast portal session** (RemEx-lq6h). Absolute pointer targets now go through `NotifyPointerMotionAbsolute` on the same portal session that owns the capture stream (stream-relative coordinates, compositor-clamped), replacing the relative-delta emulation whose unclamped virtual tracker desynced permanently on every screen-edge overshoot — the "mouse doesn't clamp" bug. The host additionally clamps every absolute input target to the active capture region (all platforms), so the pointer can never be driven onto a monitor the remote user isn't viewing. The relative-delta path remains as an automatic fallback when no capture session is active. Verified live: exact landings, right/bottom-edge clamp, and left-edge confinement to the streamed monitor. (`LinuxPortalRemoteDesktopSessionService.TryNotifyPointerMotionAbsolute`, `LinuxCaptureSessionLifetime.TryInjectPointerMotionAbsolute`, `LinuxInputSimulationService`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.ClampToActiveBounds`.)
- Linux: **RemEx now auto-starts at login** — the Linux equivalent of the Windows Task Scheduler logon task (RemEx-aep.7). `client-install.sh` writes an XDG autostart entry (`~/.config/autostart/remex-client.desktop`, `Exec=… --minimized`, `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=true`) so the agent comes up minimized to the tray at sign-in, ready for the phone to connect; uninstall removes it, and it is the **same file** the in-app Settings > "Launch at login" toggle manages, so the two stay in sync. `StartupRegistrationService` now mirrors the installed launcher (prefers the per-user `~/.local/share/applications/remex-client.desktop`, falls back to the system copy, then to a generated entry branded `Name=RemEx` / `Icon=remex` to match). The `.desktop` generation strips CRLF (the templates live on a shared Windows/Linux drive), so entries pass `desktop-file-validate`. The headless SYSTEM service (`remex-host.service`, for logged-out power commands) is intentionally kept — it serves a different purpose than the interactive user-session autostart. (`installer/linux/client-install.sh`, `StartupRegistrationService`.)
- Linux: **runtime-validated the P0/P1 security file permissions on CachyOS** (RemEx-lr9) and added Linux regression tests asserting `cert.pfx` and `paired_clients.json` (which holds reconnect secrets) are written `0600`, and that mDNS advertises only real interfaces — excluding Tailscale/CGNAT (`100.64.0.0/10`), loopback, and link-local. Confirmed the former named-pipe IPC ACL surface (IPC-1/6) is gone (`System.IO.Pipes` imported nowhere), so that check is obsolete. (`CertificateServiceTests`, `PairedClientRegistryTests`, `MdnsInterfaceFilterTests`.)
- Remote desktop: **binary cursor-position protocol** (opt-in capability `supportsBinaryCursor`). High-frequency cursor position (60–90×/s) now travels as a fixed 32-byte binary packet (`DesktopCursorBinaryEnvelope`, magic `"RDXC"`) over the existing `/ws/desktop` binary channel — demultiplexed from H.264 frames by magic (distinct from a NAL start code and the `"RDXF"` frame envelope) — instead of a JSON `desktop_cursor_state` message. This removes per-message JSON string allocation on the .NET host and, on Android, the JNI re-serialization to a JSON string *plus* the `JSONObject` parse: the packet crosses JNI as a `byte[]` and is parsed with `ByteBuffer`, cutting GC churn / micro-stutter on both sides. X/Y are signed (negative virtual-desktop monitors are representable). The cursor *shape* still travels as JSON (rare, large). Additive and capability-gated — **no `protocolVersion` bump**; older hosts/clients stay on the JSON path. NativeAOT-safe (`BinaryPrimitives`/spans). Requires an Android rebuild (the `Remex.Core`/`libRemexCore.so` JNI layer changes). (`DesktopCursorBinaryEnvelope`, `DesktopClientCapabilities.SupportsBinaryCursor`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.SendCursorBinaryAsync`, `RemexDesktopClient`, `AndroidNativeExports`, `RemexClientManager`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`.) (RD-E)
- Remote desktop: **opt-in "keep session unlocked while connected"** (off by default, Windows).
