When enabled, RemEx keeps the signed-in session usable for the life of a remote-desktop connection — reconnecting a disconnected/locked session to the console (via `tscon`, run by the Session-0 service) so input works even after a Microsoft "Windows App" (RDP) client disconnects and locks it, holding off idle-lock, and re-locking (disconnecting) the session when the last client disconnects. It is security-sensitive — while engaged it unlocks the PC without a password — so it stays disabled unless explicitly turned on, engages only for an authenticated remote-desktop stream, and audit-logs every unlock/re-lock. Currently enabled via a `ProgramData\RemEx\keep-session-unlocked.flag` file containing `1`; an in-app toggle with a security warning is a follow-up. Pure decision logic (`SessionGuardPolicy`) is unit-tested. An **in-app Settings toggle** (Windows-only, off by default) now turns it on/off, with a prominent, fully-localized (8-language) security warning shown while enabled; the toggle writes the `ProgramData` flag via a new `ISessionKeepUnlockedService`. Live unlock/re-lock still requires on-PC verification. (`IInteractiveSessionGuard`, `WindowsInteractiveSessionGuard`, `SessionGuardSettings`, `ISessionKeepUnlockedService`, `SettingsViewModel`, `SettingsView`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RemEx-l6o)
- Remote desktop now **confines the host cursor to the streamed display** while a single monitor is being streamed, so the pointer can't wander onto another monitor the remote user can't see (where it would just disappear). Implemented host-side via Win32 `ClipCursor`, re-applied on each cursor tick (Windows releases the clip on display/desktop/foreground changes) and released when streaming stops, cancels, or the client disconnects. No-op when streaming the full virtual desktop, and on Linux. (`IInputSimulationService`, `WindowsInputSimulationService`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **fullscreen overlay now includes Start and Stop stream controls** (RemEx-klq). The fullscreen mode previously exposed Settings and Exit Fullscreen but no way to start or stop the stream — forcing users out of immersive mode just to restart a dropped connection. A **Play** button (disabled while a connection is in progress) and a **Stop** button (styled with `errorContainer` colors for quick recognition) are now in the fullscreen action row. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop now **pans to follow the cursor while zoomed in**: when you zoom past 1× and the host cursor nears the edge of the view, the picture glides to keep it on screen (edge-deadzone tracking that mirrors the Microsoft Windows App), instead of letting the cursor disappear off the edge. With this in place, the remote-desktop screen **no longer forces landscape** — it rotates with the device like the rest of the app. (`PanFollowCalculator`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop now defaults to **120 FPS** streaming on a fresh install across both the Windows/Linux host (`DesktopConfig`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`) and the Android client (`RemoteDesktopConfigState`, `SettingsManager` DataStore defaults, connection-screen slider). High-refresh phones get the full frame rate with no manual settings change.
- Remote desktop, Android: **PC-keys bar gained AltGr and an expandable F-key/nav-key grid** (RemEx-9krr, RemEx-bct). AltGr is a fifth latching modifier chip (`VK_ALTGR = 165`/`VK_RMENU`) alongside Ctrl/Alt/Shift/Win, following the same ARMED/LOCKED cycle and chord-wrap as the existing four. A new expand chevron on the compact bar reveals a `FlowRow` grid of F1-F12 plus Home/End/PgUp/PgDn/Insert, routed through the same plain keypress path the existing utility keys use — an armed/locked modifier wraps these for free (e.g. Ctrl+Home) with no new chord logic. This closes out the remaining scope of the deferred RemEx-bct special-keys-toolbar ask; the toggle buttons, latching modifiers, and chord routing it also called for had already shipped via RemEx-yi8o. F1-F12 labels are literal (never localized by any keyboard vendor); the 5 nav keys + AltGr + the chevron got real content-description strings across all 8 supported locales. The modifier-wrap decision was extracted from `RemoteDesktopViewModel.sendKeyPress` into a pure `computeChordApplication` (new `RemoteDesktopChordApplication.kt`), covered by `RemoteDesktopChordTest`. (`RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`, `RemoteDesktopChordApplication.kt`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: a **Windows.Graphics.Capture (WGC) screen-capture backend**, now the preferred per-monitor capture path ahead of DXGI Desktop Duplication. The host tries capture backends in a **WGC → DXGI → GDI** ladder, so the modern, driver-robust WGC API replaces hand-rolled Desktop Duplication for monitor capture — targeting the recurring DXGI bug class (driver-wedge / black screen `RemEx-crk`, `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST`, cursor-only freeze `RemEx-ltd`) **without any change to the wire protocol, H.264 encoder, Android client, or pairing**. WGC captures cursor-less (`IsCursorCaptureEnabled = false`), so the existing GDI cursor-shape streaming continues to drive the remote cursor, and the yellow capture border is suppressed (`IsBorderRequired = false`, via `IGraphicsCaptureSession3` so it no-ops cleanly on older Windows). A machine-wide HKLM toggle — `HKLM\SOFTWARE\RemEx\Capture\Backend` = `Auto` (default) \| `Wgc` \| `Dxgi` \| `Gdi` — lets an operator pin a problematic GPU to DXGI/GDI, or A/B WGC vs DXGI, with no redeploy; it fails open to `Auto` so a bad value can never disable capture. The WinRT-touching code is isolated in a new Windows-only `remex.agent.windows` project (TFM `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0`) referenced **only on Windows** behind the `WGC_CAPTURE` constant, so the Linux build is completely unaffected. WGC, like DXGI, requires the interactive session (not Session 0) and a GPU, so it isn't CI-testable; coverage stays at the `IScreenCaptureService` seam plus the new HKLM-preference parse tests, with the real validation a manual interactive matrix. The shared encoder-dimension helper `CaptureScaling` moved from `remex.agent` to `Remex.Core` so the new project shares the one formula. (`IWgcCaptureSource`, `WgcDesktopCapture`, `CaptureBackendPreference`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`, `HostBootstrapper`.) (RemEx-g6r)

### Changed
- **Brand-icon refresh to Material 3 Expressive — new launcher, adaptive/themed icon, and unified two-tone action icons (Android).** Replaced the old neon "R + lightning bolt on a blue cyber-grid" launcher and the unstyled Material action-icon grab-bag with a single stylised terminal-window monogram — a shell-prompt chevron, the letter **R**, and a cursor (`>R▮`) — on an **amber-on-slate** palette (slate gradient `#20242F`→`#39404F`, window `#0C0E13`, amber accent `#FFB63D`, ink `#F4F5F9`). The adaptive icon (`mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` + `_round`) now composes three **vector `@drawable` layers** — `ic_launcher_background` (gradient), `ic_launcher_foreground_vector` (window + `>R` + cursor, scaled 0.8 into the safe zone), and `ic_launcher_monochrome` (themed-icon layer for Android 13+) — replacing the former raster foreground PNG and the per-dpi mipmap background/foreground/monochrome webp layers, which are now deleted as dead weight. The nine action icons (`ic_shutdown`, `ic_restart`, `ic_restart_uefi`, `ic_sleep`, `ic_hibernate`, `ic_lock`, `ic_wol`, `ic_refresh`, `ic_monitor_off`) are rebuilt in one two-tone amber language (full-alpha glyph over a 16%-alpha amber tile, so the two-tone survives an `android:tint`). Per-dpi `ic_launcher.webp`/`ic_launcher_round.webp` legacy rasters and the 512×512 `ic_launcher-playstore.png` were refreshed to match. Verified via `:app:mergeDebugResources` (BUILD SUCCESSFUL, no duplicate-resource errors). The 512×512 Play Store icon and the 1024×500 feature graphic must be uploaded to the Play Console manually — they are not app resources.
- **On-brand wordmark + notification icon to match the new brand system (Android).** Regenerated the in-app wordmark webps (`remex_wordmark_light.webp`, `remex_wordmark_dark.webp`, both 1024×512, transparent) from the new lockup — **Sora ExtraBold (800)**, "Rem" in ink (`#F4F5F9` on the light asset / `#1A1C24` on the dark asset), "Ex" in amber `#FFB63D`. Replaced the stock Android-Studio face/mask notification small icon (`ic_notification.xml`, used by `RemexConnectionService` and `FileTransferNotificationManager`) with a flat-white, minimal `>R▮` monogram derived from `ic_launcher_monochrome` (glyph-only, no window frame, so it stays legible at status-bar size). Deleted the unreferenced default `ic_stat_name.png` set across all five `drawable-*dpi` folders (dead weight, zero references) — leaving those folders empty, so they were removed. Verified via `:app:mergeDebugResources` (BUILD SUCCESSFUL).
- **Documentation currency + non-technical onboarding pass (RemEx-4cwk).** Purged stale "Windows Service / Session 0 / LocalSystem / named pipe / desktop client" references — obsolete since the RemEx-aep interactive-session migration — from `README.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (§5 "Local IPC" rewritten as *removed in 2.0*), `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` (deleted the phantom `dotnet run --project remex.desktop` "Run Client" step and the duplicate project row), and `docs/ANDROID_SETUP.md` (corrected `EncryptedSharedPreferences` → Tink AES-256-GCM + Android Keystore + Jetpack DataStore). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-HOST.md` rewritten to lead with the current single-process elevated-session architecture, preserving the two-plane history and its rationale below. README "Getting Started" now leads with the Windows installer (`RemEx-v<version>-Setup.exe`) instead of a developer `dotnet run` command, and the pairing walkthrough is more hand-holdy. `docs/KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md` replaced its inaccurate "no known limitations" line with an honest, source-verified list.
- **New — `docs/SECURITY_EXPLAINED.md`.** A three-tier (plain-English → intermediate → full-technical) explanation of every security feature: TLS 1.3/WSS; self-signed RSA-2048 (SHA-256, 5-year) cert + SHA-256 SPKI pinning; ECDH P-256 → HKDF-SHA256 session key (salt = cert SPKI); 6-digit PIN HMAC binding with 120 s TTL and `PairingThrottle` back-off; HMAC-over-nonce reconnect secrets; default-deny 8338; at-rest ACLs / Tink-Keystore storage; the elevated single-session model — plus an explicit in-scope / out-of-scope threat model. Cross-linked from `README.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md`. (RemEx-4cwk)
- **User-facing text, 2.0 ship-day pass (RemEx-87vl).** Android: the 11 display-picker and home-screen-widget strings that were missing from every locale are now translated in all 8 (Turkish and Ukrainian additionally had 6 connection-flow strings left in English — now translated); the most-visible connection-flow strings now say "PC" instead of "host" ("PC ONLINE", "Searching for your PC…", "PC found…", "No RemEx PC found on the network…") in English and all 8 locales; top-bar Back/More accessibility labels are localized; "Wake On LAN" → "Wake-on-LAN". Desktop UI: the discovery status no longer ships a "(Selectable discovery not yet implemented)" admission; "LINUX DAEMON (SYSTEMD)" → "LINUX BACKGROUND SERVICE"; canvas dashboard card titles and the "PC address is required" validation message are localized instead of hardcoded English; "Remex" → "RemEx" in the tray tooltip and window title; "colour" → "color". (`strings.xml` ×9, `Strings*.resx` ×9, `RemexFlexibleTopBar.kt`, `CanvasDashboardViewModel.cs`, `ConnectionViewModel.cs`, `MainWindow.axaml`.)
- **Linux install is now a single `remex-agent` package — full parity with the Windows single-app model (RemEx-u0oc).** `installer/build-linux.sh` produces one `remex-agent-vX.Y.Z-linux-x64.tar.gz` (the old `remex-client`/`remex-host` pair wrapped the same consolidated `Remex.Agent` binary twice); `client-install.sh` was renamed to `agent-install.sh` (shipped in the package as `install.sh`) and installs to `~/.local/share/remex-agent` with a `remex-agent` launcher symlink and a `remex-agent.desktop` menu + XDG autostart entry. The installer is now also the **upgrade/repair path**: it removes the legacy `remex-host` root systemd service and `remex-client` user install, and migrates a root-owned `/var/lib/remex/cert.pfx` (left behind by the old root service — it bricked every non-root run because the brick canary correctly refused to regenerate) into the per-user store **unchanged**, so SPKI-pinned pairings survive. On the code side, the Linux cert path is now per-user (`~/.local/share/Remex/cert.pfx`, beside `paired_clients.json`) with a one-time, SPKI-preserving auto-migration from `/var/lib/remex` (`CertificateService.ResolveNonWindowsCertificatePath`, unit-tested incl. the unreadable-legacy canary case); the read-side brick-canary message is platform-aware (the old text told Linux users to "restart elevated", which would have created *more* root-owned state); and `StartupRegistrationService` manages `remex-agent.desktop` with a one-time startup migration of legacy `remex-client.desktop` autostart entries (`Program.cs`), preventing double-launch or a stale pre-rename binary starting at login. `build-installer.ps1` dropped the obsolete Linux `-SkipClient`/`-SkipHost` switches; `build-linux.sh` warns-and-ignores the old flags. `docs/LINUX_INSTALL.md` was rewritten as a plain-English beginner guide matching the real install (incl. the cert-repair and double-autostart troubleshooting entries); `docs/ARCHITECTURE-HOST.md` is marked superseded. This supersedes the RemEx-aep.7 note above that the systemd service was "intentionally kept". (`installer/build-linux.sh`, `installer/linux/agent-install.sh`, `installer/linux/remex-agent.desktop`, `CertificateService`, `StartupRegistrationService`, `Program.cs`.)
- **Renamed `scripts/install-service.ps1` → `scripts/autostart-remex.ps1` (RemEx-67x).** The script has not created a Windows Service since RemEx-aep — it registers the elevated Task Scheduler logon task and removes any leftover `RemexHost` service. The old name implied a service still existed and was misleading; all references were updated (`installer/RemEx.iss`, `StartupRegistrationService`, `fix-host-install.ps1`, `BUILDING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`). No behavior change — same actions (`Install`/`Uninstall`/`Status`/`Start`/`Stop`).
- **RemEx is now a single elevated interactive-session app — the Windows Service is gone (RemEx-aep).** The PC side previously ran as a LocalSystem **Session-0 Windows Service** that spawned a separate interactive GUI host and bridged commands/input across the session boundary. A Session-0 service structurally cannot capture the interactive desktop or inject input into it, and a directly-launched host ran at *medium* integrity, so Windows UIPI silently dropped its input into elevated windows — the two root causes of "the host won't stream" and "the cursor is unusable in an elevated window". `remex.agent` now runs as **one process inside the signed-in user's session, always elevated** (`requestedExecutionLevel=requireAdministrator` in `app.manifest`), **auto-started by a Task Scheduler logon task** (`scripts/autostart-remex.ps1` registers task `RemEx`, `LogonType=InteractiveToken`, `RunLevel=Highest`) so it starts elevated at sign-in with **no UAC prompt**. Elevation is load-bearing: an elevated token keeps FullControl over the machine-wide `cert.pfx` / `paired_clients.json` (ACL = LocalSystem + Administrators, inheritance disabled), so **existing pairings survive with no re-pair** — a non-elevated start would get Administrators as deny-only, fail to read the cert, and brick every SPKI-pinned pairing. `CertificateService` gained a brick canary (logs Critical and refuses to regenerate when an existing `cert.pfx` is unreadable). The in-app "Launch at login" toggle is now backed by the scheduled task; the installer drops the old HKCU Run-key (which would start a competing medium-integrity instance). Pre-login remote control is intentionally dropped (the task fires after interactive logon). Linux gets a follow-up (systemd user service). (`app.manifest`, `autostart-remex.ps1`, `installer/RemEx.iss`, `StartupRegistrationService`, `CertificateService`.)
- **Local IPC collapsed to in-process DI (RemEx-aep).** With the UI and host in one process, the `RemExLocalIPC` named pipe and `LocalIpcServerService` were pure cross-process indirection. The desktop adapters (`IpcClientCommandService`, `IpcWakeOnLanService`, `IpcPairingPinQueryService`, and the app-launcher) now resolve the real host services straight from the embedded host's DI container via a new `EmbeddedHostServiceLocator`; the obsolete two-process "server vs client" mode in `CommandModeContext` (a `Global\RemExServiceMutex` arbiter + a duplicate network listener) is removed. The "Windows Service Management" section of Settings is gone (the launch-at-login toggle remains). 8338 ingress is untouched.
- **Session guard reduced to keep-awake (RemEx-aep).** The interactive session guard no longer runs the `tscon`/`WTSDisconnectSession` reconnect-and-re-lock dance (which only made sense for a Session-0 service resuming an orphaned RDP session, and would lock the very session RemEx now lives in). It is now ref-counted `SetThreadExecutionState` keep-awake only; the "keep session unlocked" toggle keeps the screen awake while a client is connected.
- Remote desktop, Windows: the per-frame capture→encode conversion **no longer routes through `System.Drawing`/GDI+ on the hot path** — the real cause of a ~30 FPS ceiling that hardware NVENC was wrongly blamed for. Every capture backend (WGC/DXGI/GDI) previously wrapped each frame in `System.Drawing.Bitmap` objects and ran a GDI+ `Graphics.DrawImage` software blit, then a separate `LockBits`+`Marshal.Copy` into a fresh multi-MB `byte[]` — every frame. When no resample is needed (capture `scale` 1.0), the new `BgraFrameConverter` reads the mapped staging texture straight into a tightly-packed buffer with a single row-wise `Marshal.Copy` (honoring the GPU row stride) — no Bitmap, no blit. The downscale path (`scale < 1`) keeps GDI+'s native bilinear resampler (a scalar managed bilinear would be slower); GPU-side scaling is a measured follow-up. **For maximum FPS on a capable GPU, capture at full resolution (`scale 1.0`) and let the phone downscale for display** (NVENC handles full res easily; the new fit-to-height framing downscales on the phone). Output byte-count is asserted to equal the encoder's `-s WxH` input (a mismatch makes NVENC emit 0 frames) and unit-tested. (`BgraFrameConverter`, `WgcDesktopCapture`, `DxgiDesktopCapture`.) (RD-C)
- Remote desktop: the **cursor-position stream now paces at a true ~90 Hz**. It used `Task.Delay(11)`, which rounds up to the ~15.6 ms Windows OS timer floor (~64 Hz, missing the target). The video-frame loop and the cursor loop now share one `PrecisionPacer` (the hybrid coarse-sleep + `Thread.SpinWait` pacer), so cursor cadence and frame pacing have a single, regression-guarded source of truth. (`PrecisionPacer`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RD-A1)
- Remote desktop, Android: the remote screen now applies **orientation-aware initial framing** on connect and on monitor switch (RemEx-2i5). In **landscape** phone orientation the view opens at 1× zoom, showing the whole desktop (pinch to zoom in); in **portrait** orientation the view fits to the phone's height so the stream fills vertically and the user pans left/right to traverse a wide host display. A `lastFitKey` (encoding the active display token and phone orientation) ensures the fit fires once per `(display, orientation)` pair and re-fires automatically on monitor switch or phone rotation. Manual pinch/pan after the initial fit is preserved. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.) (RemEx-2i5)
- Remote desktop, Android: the cursor overlay's animated position is now **read in the Compose draw phase, not composition**, so the ~90 Hz cursor spring redraws the overlay without recomposing the screen subtree every animation frame (lower CPU/battery/thermal). The pan-follow deadzone epsilon is now density-scaled (was a hardcoded `0.5f` → sub-pixel jitter on high-DPI panels at high zoom); the accelerated pan-follow glide is unchanged. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.) (RD-B, RD-A2)
- **Project rename (RemEx-bcf):** all project folders, `.csproj` files, and solution entries are now lowercase — `remex.core`, `remex.agent` (was `RemEx.Host`), `remex.agent.windows` (was `RemEx.Host.Windows.Capture`), `remex.desktop` (was `Remex.Client`), `remex.agent.tests`, `remex.desktop.tests`, `remex.core.tests`, `remex.agent.native.linux`, and `remex.android`. C# namespaces and output assemblies move with them: `Remex.Host` → `Remex.Agent` and `Remex.Client` → `Remex.Desktop` (the host binary is now `Remex.Agent.exe`), while `Remex.Core` and the Android JNI library `libRemexCore.so` are unchanged. Runtime/install identities are deliberately preserved so existing installs keep working: the Windows service name `RemexHost`, the systemd unit `remex-host`, the IPC pipe `RemExLocalIPC`, the mDNS `_remex._tcp` type, the `RemEx` product/installer brand, and the `UserSecretsId`.
- Project `README.md` rewritten from a one-line stub into a comprehensive, visually polished landing page — hero/badges, "What's New in 2.0" matrix, feature tour, a color-styled Mermaid architecture diagram, protocol/security/theme tables, system-requirements + getting-started + build instructions, a Documentation hub linking the `docs/` set, project layout, and roadmap. It is the entry point new users reach from the RemEx app. Logo stored at the stable `docs/assets/remex-logo.png` path (decoupled from the legacy `remex.desktop/` asset folder).
- Remote desktop cursor now moves **smoothly instead of stepping**: the host streams the cursor position at ~60 Hz (up from 10 Hz; the cursor shape sync and the `ClipCursor` confinement stay throttled to ~10 Hz to avoid hammering the OS), and the Android client animates the cursor overlay toward each received position with a critically-damped spring, snapping on (re)appearance or display switch so it never slides across the screen. (`RemoteDesktopHandler`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop host frame pacing rewritten to a **hybrid precision wait**: it coarse-sleeps via `Task.Delay` for the bulk of each frame interval, then busy-spins with `Thread.SpinWait` for the final few milliseconds. A bare `Task.Delay` rounds up to the OS timer resolution (~15.6 ms on Windows), which oversleeps an 8.33 ms (120 FPS) interval to ~15.6 ms and capped the achievable rate near 60 FPS. The new pacing is fully localized — no global timer changes (`timeBeginPeriod`) — so it also benefits Linux pacing.
- The two divergent local-IPC stacks are **collapsed into one**: `LocalIpcServerService` over a single `RemExLocalIPC` pipe (one framing, one ACL, one shared pipe-name constant). The redundant `IpcHostServer` / `RemexIPC` newline-protocol server and its duplicate hosted-service registration are removed, with `LaunchApp` folded into the unified command dispatch (cross-session via `IAppLauncherService`). (IPC-4 RemEx-irl; IPC-5 RemEx-qg2 absorbed.)

- Remote desktop, Android: **fullscreen overlay row polish — shared spacing constants, deduped toggle colors, action order reconciled with the windowed toolbar** (RemEx-klq). The fullscreen top-end row (Settings/FPS/Keyboard/PC-keys/Exit/Stop) previously repeated `16.dp`/`8.dp` padding literals and three near-identical `filledTonalIconButtonColors` blocks inline; these are now `FullscreenOverlayEdgePadding`/`FullscreenOverlayIconSpacing` and a shared `toggleIconButtonColors(active)` helper. The row's action order now matches the windowed `TopAppBar` for the actions both share (Keyboard, PC-keys, Settings, fullscreen-exit, Stop/Play); the fullscreen-only FPS toggle is slotted in without disturbing that shared order. Also fixed the fullscreen Settings button's hardcoded `"Settings"` content description to use the existing localized `R.string.cd_settings` (was never translated). **Not done, left as an open finding:** the two rows still have different action *sets* (Reset/zoom is windowed-only, FPS is fullscreen-only) — deciding whether either belongs on both surfaces is a rendering/UX call, not resolved here. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)

### Removed
- **The Linux root systemd service (RemEx-u0oc).** Deleted `installer/linux/host-install.sh` and `installer/linux/remex-host.service` — pre-login power commands are a non-goal on Linux exactly as on Windows (RemEx-aep), and the root-run service was the source of the root-owned `/var/lib/remex/cert.pfx` that bricked user-session starts. The PC side on Linux is the single per-user `remex-agent` app, auto-started at login; `agent-install.sh` uninstalls the legacy service from existing machines.
- **The Windows Service and its second process (RemEx-aep).** Deleted `AgentCoordinator`, `HostControlServer`/`HostControlClient` (the `RemExHostControl` port-handoff pipe), `InteractiveDesktopHostLauncher`, `LocalIpcServerService` + the `RemExLocalIPC` pipe (`Remex.Core/Services/RemExLocalIPC.cs`), `SessionBridgingCommandService`, `WindowsActiveSession` (the `WTSQueryUserToken` + `CreateProcessAsUser` bridge), and `SessionGuardPolicy`. Removed the `--agent`/`RunAgent`/`UseWindowsService` and `--session-task`/`RunSessionTask` entry points, the `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting.WindowsServices` package reference, and the Session-0 detection/warnings across `HostBootstrapper`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`, `AppLauncherService`, and `HostCapabilitiesProvider`. A legacy `RemexHost` service, if present, is uninstalled by the new logon-task installer.

### Fixed
- Android FAQ screen: **rewrote all FAQ answers to match the current architecture** — the FAQ referenced two products that no longer exist (`Remex.Client.Desktop`, `Remex.PC`); the only PC-side app is `remex.agent`. Also fixed the capture-backend description (was "DXGI on Windows, X11/Wayland on Linux"; now the real WGC → DXGI → GDI ladder on Windows and PipeWire-via-portal on Linux), the default-port fallback behavior, and stale "overflow menu" navigation wording (the app uses a bottom "More" tab). Removed a false claim that the Linux installer sets up Tailscale automatically. Added two new FAQ entries verified against the codebase: file transfer (shared folders configured independently on each side) and why RemEx sometimes refuses to reconnect and asks to re-pair (certificate-pinning behavior, by design). (`FaqScreen.kt`, `values*/strings.xml` faq_q1–faq_q13.)
- Build: **`./build-remex.ps1 -t android`/`-t all` no longer fails with a spurious "SDK license not accepted" error when switching between the Windows and CachyOS build machines that share this repo's drive.** `remex.android/local.properties` is a flat, OS-agnostic properties file, so a `sdk.dir` written on one OS is a nonexistent path on the other; combined with a stray `ANDROID_HOME` pointing at a `build-tools\<ver>` subfolder instead of the SDK root, Gradle resolved SDK components at an inconsistent location and reported `platforms;android-37.0` as license-unaccepted even though the real SDK's license was fine. The Android compile step now auto-syncs `local.properties`' `sdk.dir` from `ANDROID_HOME`/`ANDROID_SDK_ROOT` for the current OS before every Gradle invocation, so switching platforms never requires a manual edit. (`build-remex.ps1`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **an ARMED PC-key modifier no longer silently latches onto a later, unrelated keystroke** (RemEx-rimh). Typing anything that isn't a single plain character while a modifier chip was armed (punctuation, autocomplete, multi-character edits) routed through the text-injection path, which bypasses key events entirely — the armed modifier stayed latched and fired on the next keypress. `sendText` now spends ARMED modifiers after every text-injection send (LOCKED chips are untouched, and real chords like Ctrl+Backspace still work). (`RemoteDesktopViewModel.spendArmedModifiers`.)
- Android splash screens, **2.0 ship-day polish pass** (RemEx-87vl): the brand "R" logo and its wordmark offsets are now density-scaled — the logo path was drawn in raw pixels and rendered up to ~3× too small next to its dp-scaled text on real phones (it only looked right in 1× previews); the wordmark and titles switched from hardcoded white to theme tokens so light theme no longer shows invisible text; the CosmicZoom subtitle picks a theme-safe accent; the lightning haptic no longer buzzes on the RemexCommand style (which has no lightning strike); the RemexCommand "zoom into the monitor" finale is visible now (the fade-out used to cover it before it landed); the CosmicZoom title enters as three staggered fade+rise lines instead of one flat pop; navigation is buffered 100 ms past the exit fade so a dropped frame can't cause a visible pop; splash-style picker labels ellipsize instead of clipping in longer locales. (`SplashScreen.kt`, `PersonalizationScreen.kt`.)
- Pairing, Android: **the PIN screen can no longer dead-end on a forever-spinner with Cancel disabled** when the PC goes offline mid-pairing (RemEx-87vl). Both native pairing calls now time out after 15 s with a plain-English localized error (all 8 locales), and Cancel stays enabled throughout. (`PairingScreen.kt`.)
- File transfer, Android: **folder listing and browsing no longer spin forever** if the connection drops before the PC replies — both now use the same 30 s timeout pattern the delete/rename operations already had. (`FileTransferViewModel.kt`.) (RemEx-87vl)
- Connection, Android (RemEx-87vl): a failed connect no longer ends silently — the failure reason from the PC is surfaced instead of discarded; a stale error card is cleared when the background heartbeat auto-reconnects; the "Connected" status highlight now works in all 8 languages (it compared the localized status text against the English literal); the "PC found" discovery snackbar is localized; QR-scanner setup failures show a localized message instead of a raw exception; auto-pairing failures no longer show a hardcoded English "Pairing failed:" prefix around raw protocol text. (`ConnectionScreen.kt`, `ConnectionViewModel.kt`, `QrScannerScreen.kt`, `RemexClientManager.kt`.)
- Material 3 consistency, Android (RemEx-87vl): pairing and QR-scanner error banners now use the app's expressive motion tokens like every other error banner in the app (they snapped in with framework-default fades); FAQ, Remote Control, and Personalization expand/collapse animations use `MaterialTheme.motionScheme` tokens instead of hardcoded springs; the dashboard connection-orb glow animation no longer runs while disconnected (idle battery drain on the most-viewed screen); the pairing error icon and task-manager clear-search button gained localized accessibility descriptions; settings and remote-control lists use stable item keys.
- Remote desktop, Windows: **synthetic AltGr/Home/End/PgUp/PgDn/Insert now set `KEYEVENTF_EXTENDEDKEY`** (RemEx-9krr). `WindowsInputSimulationService.KeyDown`/`KeyUp` sent every key with `dwFlags = 0` (down) / `KEYEVENTF_KEYUP` (up) only — no extended-key handling existed anywhere in the file. Per Win32 docs, `VK_RMENU` (0xA5, AltGr) needs this flag or Windows can't reliably tell it apart from left-Alt. Scoped to only the VKs newly introduced by the AltGr chip and F-key/nav grid above; arrows/Delete/RCONTROL already ship without the flag and already work, so they are intentionally left untouched. **Caveat, not yet verified on real hardware:** the extended flag fixes which physical key Windows thinks fired, but real AltGr hardware emits left-Ctrl + right-Alt together, and character-layer translation (€, @, {, etc.) may key off that combination — sending right-Alt alone, even extended-flagged, might not unlock the AltGr character layer. Needs confirmation on a real Windows target; if insufficient, file a follow-up to synthesize Ctrl+RAlt. Linux required **no change** — `LinuxInputEventTranslator` already maps AltGr/F-keys/nav-keys correctly. (`WindowsInputSimulationService.IsExtendedVirtualKey`.)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **H.264 no longer permanently falls back to 12.5 FPS MJPEG on monitor switch or disconnect/reconnect** (RemEx-lq6h). The fallback was a set of one-way ratchets, all removed: a single failed encoder rebuild permanently demoted the session to MJPEG with no retry (`RemoteDesktopHandler` now keeps the negotiated codec, serves MJPEG-*tagged* frames during the outage — safe because envelope-capable clients route each frame by its own codec tag — and retries H.264 on a 3 s cooldown; envelope-less legacy clients keep the old permanent fallback); a failed ffmpeg capability probe was cached negatively **forever** per geometry, so one transient 5 s timeout during display churn pinned that geometry to MJPEG until restart (`FFmpegH264Encoder` now expires failed verdicts after 30 s; positives stay cached); and the capture service replayed a cached raw frame from the *previous* capture target on any 80 ms PipeWire timeout — marked "live" — which desynced the encoder's fixed input size into an endless ~1 s reinit storm (`LinuxScreenCaptureService` now signature-guards both the raw and JPEG caches by target offset+size+scale, implements `CaptureRawScreenLiveAsync` properly, and never replays across a target switch).
- Remote desktop, Linux: **reconnecting or switching monitors no longer lands on a dead capture stream** (RemEx-lq6h). Closing a KDE ScreenCast session and restoring it shortly afterwards — exactly what the Android client's reconnect and monitor-switch flows do — reliably yields a stream that KWin reports as valid (`pw_stream_connect` succeeds) but that never produces a single buffer, for minutes after the close; only restores after process death worked. The portal capture session is therefore now opened once and kept **warm for the process lifetime** (`LinuxCaptureSessionLifetime`): reconnects adopt the live session instantly instead of re-opening it, and cold starts verify first-frame production (with one automatic session recreation) before wiring the stream in. Side effect: KDE's screen-sharing indicator stays on while the agent runs — honest, since the machine remains remotely controllable. Also, `SetCaptureCoordinator` no longer resets an explicitly chosen monitor target back to the primary on session (re)open, and raw frame dimensions are even-aligned with `CaptureScaling.ScaledEven` (including at scale 1.0) so the BGRA buffer always matches the H.264 encoder's fixed input size.
- Remote desktop, Linux: **the cursor is no longer duplicated in the stream** (RemEx-lq6h). The portal ScreenCast session requested `cursor_mode=Embedded`, baking the host's cursor into every frame, while the Android client (which sends `drawCursor=false`) renders its own cursor from the streamed cursor state — showing two cursors at once. The session now requests `cursor_mode=Hidden` by default (configurable per session for legacy host-composited clients), so only the client-rendered cursor is visible. (`LinuxPortalRemoteDesktopSessionService`.)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **silent MJPEG degradation (low FPS) on NVIDIA — the H.264 codec fallback never fired because ffmpeg opens its encoder lazily (RemEx-h038).** The Linux codec ladder tried `h264_vaapi` first, but NVIDIA ships no VAAPI *encode* entrypoints (its VAAPI driver is decode-only), so the encoder can never open on an NVIDIA-only box. The failure was invisible to the 900 ms early-exit watch in `TryStartFFmpeg`: a rawvideo-pipe ffmpeg idles waiting on stdin and only opens the encoder **when the first frame arrives** — after "successfully initialized using codec: h264_vaapi" was already logged — then died on that first real frame (`Error while opening encoder`, `-38 Function not implemented`), silently dropping the session to MJPEG. `Initialize` now runs a cached **one-shot capability probe** per codec before starting the real pipe: it encodes a single black frame with the *exact* real-run arguments and input geometry (`BuildEncoderArgs` is shared; only the output tail differs — `-frames:v 1 -f null -`), so the lazy encoder-open happens up front and exit-code 0/nonzero makes codec fallback deterministic. Verdicts are cached per codec+geometry (`ProbeCache`) because the handler reinitializes the encoder on every on-demand keyframe. Verified on CachyOS/RTX 5080: the vaapi probe exits 218, nvenc probes exit 0 at 4096×1152@120. (`FFmpegH264Encoder`.) (RemEx-h038)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **Windows-parity NVENC BGRA fast path — the Linux codec ladder now tries `h264_nvenc_bgra` first** (Linux half of RemEx-whfp). Plain `h264_nvenc` on Linux still paid the per-frame CPU `swscale` (BGRA→YUV420) that caps full-res FPS; NVENC ingests `bgra` natively (verified against the CachyOS ffmpeg build), doing the color convert in fixed-function hardware exactly as on Windows (RemEx-dptu). Ladder is now `h264_nvenc_bgra → h264_nvenc → h264_vaapi → libx264`; the capability probe (above) makes the NVENC attempts free on AMD/Intel boxes, where VAAPI still serves. (`FFmpegH264Encoder`.)
- Remote desktop, Linux: **prompt-free reconnect on KDE Plasma — persistence now goes through RemoteDesktop portal v2 `SelectDevices`, not ScreenCast `SelectSources` (RemEx-mswt).** KDE's `xdg-desktop-portal-kde` rejects ScreenCast-level persistence on remote-desktop sessions by design ("Remote desktop sessions cannot persist", RemEx-82fk) — the fallback kept capture alive but re-prompted the "Share your screen?" dialog on **every** connect. The RemoteDesktop portal **version 2** (xdg-desktop-portal ≥ 1.16; Plasma 6 implements it — verified v2 live on Plasma 6.7.2) moved session persistence to `SelectDevices` (`persist_mode=PersistUntilRevoked` + replayed `restore_token`), with the refreshed token returned in the `Start` results (the existing token save/replay path handles it unchanged, `~/.local/share/Remex/portal_restore_token`, `0600`). The host now reads the interface `version` property (new `GetPortalInterfaceVersionAsync`, plain `Properties.Get`) and requests persistence in `SelectDevices` when ≥ 2, skipping `SelectSources` persistence in that case; v1 portals and ScreenCast-only fallback sessions keep the legacy `SelectSources` path, and **both** calls retry once without persistence if a compositor hard-rejects it, so a failed persist request can never take down capture. First connect after this change still prompts once (grant + token mint); every reconnect after that is prompt-free. (`LinuxPortalRemoteDesktopSessionService`.) (RemEx-mswt)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **0-fps black screen on the H.264 GPU codec path (RemEx-dptu).** The `h264_nvenc_cuda` codec ran `-vf hwupload_cuda,scale_cuda=format=nv12`, but `scale_cuda` cannot convert RGB/BGRA→NV12 in any prebuilt Windows ffmpeg — both Gyan and BtbN ship `--enable-cuda-llvm`, whose Clang-compiled `scale_cuda` lacks the RGB→semiplanar conversion kernel. FFmpeg passed init then died at **runtime** (`cuModuleGetFunction … CUDA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND: "named symbol not found"` / `Unsupported conversion: rgb0 -> semiplanar8`), ending the encoder pipe so no frames were produced; the init-time codec fallback never fired because the failure was at runtime. The GPU path now feeds NVENC the raw **BGRA frames directly** (`-c:v h264_nvenc`, no `-vf`, no `-pix_fmt yuv420p`) — NVENC ingests `bgra`/`bgr0` natively and does RGB→YUV in fixed-function hardware, so there is no CUDA filter to fail and no CPU swscale. Codec renamed `h264_nvenc_cuda` → `h264_nvenc_bgra`. Verified via the exact agent encode args at 2560×1440 and 4096×1152 (RTX 5080). (`FFmpegH264Encoder`.) (RemEx-dptu)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **WGC never actually served — two latent WinRT-interop bugs made it silently fall back to DXGI/GDI on every connect (RemEx-hvqv).** (1) `IGraphicsCaptureItemInterop::CreateForMonitor` was passed the **GraphicsCaptureItem runtimeclass GUID** instead of the **`IGraphicsCaptureItem` interface IID** (`79c3f95b-…`), returning `E_NOINTERFACE` (`0x80004002`). (2) The `IDirect3DDevice` was projected with `Marshal.GetObjectForIUnknown(...) as IDirect3DDevice`, yielding a legacy `__ComObject` CsWinRT can't re-marshal — so `Direct3D11CaptureFramePool.CreateFreeThreaded` threw *"Failed to create a CCW … IDirect3DDevice: the specified cast is not valid"*; it is now projected via `MarshalInspectable<IDirect3DDevice>.FromAbi` (matching the working `GraphicsCaptureItem.FromAbi` pattern). Both were invisible because the only failure log was `Debug` while the in-memory `/debug/logs` sink floors at `Information`; the WGC monitor-selection **outcome** is now logged at Information/Warning, de-duplicated so the per-frame capture path never floods, reaching both the in-memory sink and the Windows Event Log. (`WgcDesktopCapture`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`.) (RemEx-hvqv)
- Remote desktop: **the entire intended monitor is now visible and the mouse is pixel-accurate on the *first* connect — no more "fullscreen → tap both monitors → zoom out → back to primary" workaround (RemEx-4k4).** This supersedes the `WarmUpCapture` timing patch (RemEx-6my): priming DXGI earlier still lost the race because `GetScreenSize()` — the single source of the dimensions Android uses to *both* letterbox the video and map the cursor — was decoupled from the backend that actually draws frames. On a single-monitor target the real backend is **WGC** (`WgcDesktopCapture`, per-monitor), but `GetScreenSize()` only consulted DXGI-or-`GetActiveBounds()` and never read WGC's size, so the bootstrap reported probe dimensions (DXGI uninitialised → virtual-desktop/GDI bounds) that disagreed with the WGC frame in value (and, on scaled displays, units). The fix makes the contract **frame-derived and single-source**: `WarmUpCapture()` now also selects the WGC monitor (its `GraphicsCaptureItem.Size` is populated the instant the monitor is selected, before the first frame), and `GetScreenSize()` reports the dimensions of whichever backend actually serves the target (WGC → DXGI → GDI), with a new `ActiveMonitorOrigin()` supplying the monitor's virtual-desktop origin for absolute cursor mapping. On Android, the initial fit no longer computes against the 1920×1080 placeholder: a new `desktopMetaReady` signal makes the fit wait for the host's real metadata, and the fit key now encodes the real stream dimensions so authoritative metadata that arrives or changes mid-stream (resolution/monitor/DPI) re-fits instead of being ignored. A host bootstrap log line (`RD bootstrap: streaming WxH @ (L,T) via {backend}`) makes a mis-framed connect diagnosable from the host log alone. (`WindowsScreenCaptureService.GetScreenSize`/`WarmUpCapture`/`ActiveMonitorOrigin`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.) (RemEx-4k4)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **Android no longer frames the initial stream at wrong dimensions on first connect (RemEx-6my).** `DxgiDesktopCapture` defers `DuplicateOutput` until the first capture frame — by design, to avoid holding an idle Desktop Duplication consumer slot when no client is connected (RemEx-hmj). `SendCurrentStreamBootstrapAsync` fires *before* `StreamFramesAsync` starts the capture loop, so on first connect `GetScreenSize()` found DXGI uninitialised and fell back to GDI bounds, which could be stale or mismatched. Android computed its initial zoom/pan/coordinate mapping from those wrong dimensions; the user had to switch monitors (which re-emits the bootstrap after DXGI is live) to get correct framing. A new `IScreenCaptureService.WarmUpCapture()` (default no-op; Windows calls `_dxgi.TryRecover()`) is invoked once per client connection immediately before the bootstrap, ensuring DXGI is always initialised and `GetScreenSize()` always returns real hardware dimensions at connect time. (`IScreenCaptureService`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RemEx-6my)
- Remote desktop, Android: **keyboard can now be re-summoned after dismissal with the back gesture (RemEx-46q).** The soft keyboard state was driven by the hidden `BasicTextField`'s focus (`onFocusChanged { isRemoteKeyboardOpen = it.isFocused }`); the back gesture hides the IME without clearing focus, so the next `focusRequester.requestFocus()` was a no-op and the keyboard never returned until leaving and re-entering the remote desktop screen. `isRemoteKeyboardOpen` is now derived from live IME insets (`WindowInsets.ime.getBottom(LocalDensity.current) > 0`) so it tracks the actual on-screen keyboard state. The keyboard button now calls `LocalSoftwareKeyboardController.show()` (or `.hide()`) alongside `requestFocus()`, guaranteeing the IME opens/closes even when the field was already focused. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **L/M/R click buttons in the fullscreen control bar now click at the cursor's current position** instead of the top-left corner or a nearby wrong position (RemEx-gd7). The three `FilledTonalButton` click handlers were passing `-1, -1` as coordinates; the host's `CoordinateValidation.ClampToRange` clamped `-1` to `0`, sending every menu-bar click to screen coordinate `(0, 0)`. A first fix used `mapLocalToHost(Offset(cursorX, cursorY))`, but this re-applies the current zoom/pan transform to the *old* local touch coords — if the user panned or zoomed the view since their last cursor move, the same local coords map to a different host pixel, causing the cursor to jump slightly before clicking. The buttons now use `lastSentAbsHost` — the exact `Offset` last passed to `onSendMouseAbsolute` — which is immune to zoom/pan drift because it is already in host-pixel coordinates. Falls back to `mapLocalToHost` only if no absolute cursor move has been sent yet (e.g. in trackpad-relative mode). (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop, Android: **scaling the stream up (e.g. 0.5 → 1.0) no longer goes black or garbled (RemEx-aep).** `H264StreamDecoder` configured `MediaCodec` exactly once and sized `KEY_MAX_INPUT_SIZE` from the *initial* resolution hint, so when the host rebuilt its encoder at a larger capture scale, the bigger IDR no longer fit the input buffer and the new SPS-declared resolution was never re-read. The decoder now detects a mid-stream SPS change (comparing the raw SPS NAL bytes against the configured `csd-0`, with a cheap `containsNalType` pre-check so P-frames stay on the fast path) and reconfigures (`stop`/`configure`/`start`) for the new resolution, feeding the IDR into the fresh codec. `KEY_MAX_INPUT_SIZE` is now sized for the host's full-screen frame (4K-bounded, 8 MiB-capped) and `KEY_MAX_WIDTH`/`KEY_MAX_HEIGHT` are set so adaptive-playback decoders absorb most scale changes without the reconfigure hitch (the explicit reconfigure is the fallback). The host already emits a fresh SPS/PPS+IDR on rebuild, so no new dimension protocol is needed. (`H264StreamDecoder`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **the `SendInput` partial-injection canary now reports the right diagnosis for the elevated model (RemEx-aep).** When `SendInput` delivers fewer events than requested it now logs `sent/total` and explains the UIPI/integrity-level failure mode (Secure Desktop active, or RemEx unexpectedly at medium integrity), replacing the obsolete "Remex may not be running in the active user session" hint. (`WindowsInputSimulationService`.)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **S-Pen hover (and absolute pointer input) now reaches a second monitor positioned to the left of / above the primary** — one with negative virtual-desktop coordinates. The host clamped incoming absolute input coordinates to `[0, width-1]` against the streamed display's size while discarding its origin, so any negative coordinate floored to 0 and the cursor stuck at the edge, unable to move onto that monitor. A new range-aware `CoordinateValidation.ClampToRange` clamps to `[origin, origin+size)` using the streamed display's actual (possibly negative) `desktopLeft`/`desktopTop`, preserving the anti-spoofing bound (NaN/∞/out-of-range still rejected — RD-8/RemEx-q6u) while allowing legitimate negative coordinates. (`CoordinateValidation.ClampToRange`, `RemoteDesktopHandler.EnqueuePointerSampleAsInputEvent`.) (RD-D)
- Remote desktop, Windows: added **UIPI diagnosis logging** for the "the cursor won't move while an elevated/admin window is in the foreground" symptom. The Session-0 service launches its interactive input-injector host at HIGH integrity via the signed-in user's linked admin token; when no linked token is available it now logs the `TokenElevationType` (1=Default/UAC-off or non-admin, 2=Full/already-elevated, 3=Limited/split-admin) so the logs reveal whether UIPI is blocking input because the host fell back to medium integrity, and why. The durable fix (a `uiAccess`-manifested signed helper, a SYSTEM-integrity injector, or removing a competing medium-integrity host instance) is chosen from this diagnosis. (`WindowsActiveSession.TryGetLinkedElevatedToken`.) (RD-F)
- Remote desktop, Windows: the H.264 stream **no longer thrashes the encoder into a black screen** when a client's decoder desyncs. Honoring an on-demand keyframe request rebuilds the FFmpeg/nvenc encoder (a rawvideo stdin pipe has no per-frame IDR API), which is a ~1s operation. A desynced Android decoder floods keyframe requests (its input backlog keeps refilling), so once the routing fix above let those requests reach the host, every capture-loop iteration rebuilt the encoder — pinning the stream at ~2 FPS with no stable GOP, i.e. a black screen with only the (separately-streamed) cursor visible. The on-demand reinit is now a **rare backstop (one per 5 s cooldown) rather than the primary resync path**: because the encoder already emits a keyframe every 60 frames (`-g 60`) — every 0.5–1 s at a healthy 60–120 FPS — a desynced decoder resyncs on the next *natural* GOP keyframe without any reinit, and once delivery is smooth the request flood stops on its own. Honoring every request instead reinitialized the encoder continuously, which was the very stall that kept the stream from ever smoothing out. Requests arriving inside the cooldown are swallowed; any legitimate rebuild (target switch, quality/fps/scale change) is unaffected and also satisfies the cooldown. The per-window Stream Metrics log reports `Throttled keyframe reinits: N`, and each rebuild now logs its trigger (`reason=keyframe|target-switch|config-change`) so a storm is diagnosable at a glance. (`RemoteDesktopHandler` capture loop.) (#2b)
- Remote desktop, Windows: with **"keep session unlocked" enabled while RemEx runs interactively**, connecting/disconnecting (and especially **switching monitors**) no longer **locks the screen and black-screens the stream**. The interactive session guard set its `_heldSessionId` from the policy decision *unconditionally*, then on the last client disconnect ran `WTSDisconnectSession(_heldSessionId)` — even when the session was already `AlreadyUsable` (Active/Connected) and the guard had never unlocked it. When RemEx runs **inside** the interactive session (launched from the desktop, `SessionId != 0`) rather than as the Session-0 service, that disconnect locked the very session RemEx lives in, after which GDI screen capture failed with `Win32 6: The handle is invalid` (black screen) and input injection failed with `Win32 5: Access is denied`. Because the Android display switch restarts the stream (`StopDesktopStream`→`StartDesktopStream`), every monitor switch tripped this disconnect/re-lock cycle. The guard now (1) only remembers — and later re-locks — a session it **actually unlocked** via a console reconnect, leaving an already-usable session untouched on both engage and disengage, and (2) **never reconnects/disconnects its own session** (`Process.GetCurrentProcess().SessionId`). The Session-0 service path for resuming an orphaned/locked RDP session unlocked, then re-locking it on the last disconnect, is unchanged. Pairing is untouched. (`WindowsInteractiveSessionGuard.EngageForRemoteControl`/`Disengage`.) (#2b/#2c)
- Remote desktop: an **on-demand keyframe request (`desktop_keyframe_request`) now reaches the host encoder** instead of being logged as `Unknown message type` by the control-channel `PingPongHandler`. The Android client and the host's `RemoteDesktopHandler` both already supported the message, but the native client's `HandleDesktopMessage` router (which demultiplexes outbound messages onto the `/ws` control socket vs the `/ws/desktop` stream socket) had **no case for it**, so it fell through to the control socket where no desktop handler exists. Added the missing routing case plus `RemexDesktopClient.RequestKeyframeAsync`, so a desynced H.264 decoder's keyframe request now lands on the stream socket and triggers a real IDR. Additive and backward-compatible — no `protocolVersion` bump, no Kotlin change. Requires an Android rebuild (the fix is in the `Remex.Core`/`libRemexCore.so` JNI layer). (`AndroidNativeExports.HandleDesktopMessage`, `RemexDesktopClient`.) (#2a)
- Android build: **`./gradlew remexFreshAssembleDebug` / `remexFreshAssembleRelease` build reliably again**, including on repeat runs. Three issues were fixed in `remex.android/app/build.gradle.kts`: (1) the native-lib *copy destination* (`remexGeneratedDebug/ReleaseArm64So`) had been repointed from the required `<jniLibsRoot>/arm64-v8a/libRemexCore.so` to a deep `artifacts/bin/...` path, so Android's `mergeNativeLibs` never packaged the `.so` and `verifyRemexCoreInApk` failed — reverted to the ABI-folder convention (the relocated *source* `.so` is already resolved by `arm64PublishedSoCandidates()`, so no source change was needed); (2) `VerifyRemexCoreInApkTask.generatedArm64So` / `mergedArm64So` were `@InputFile`, so with the configuration cache enabled Gradle failed input-existence validation on a fresh `clean` build *before* the producing task ran — changed to `@Internal` (matching the existing `strippedArm64So`/`apkDirectory` pattern; the task has no outputs and verifies existence + content hashes at execution); (3) `clean` ran concurrently with the AGP build tasks (only a few top-level tasks were ordered after it), intermittently deleting `navigation.json`, R8 inputs, and the synced `libRemexCore.so` mid-build — replaced the scattered `mustRunAfter("clean")` declarations with a single rule ordering every non-`clean` task after `clean`. (RemEx-3um)
- Pairing, Tailscale: the **6-digit pairing PIN auto-populates again when the phone reaches the host over Tailscale**, instead of forcing manual entry. The Android client has always been wired to auto-fetch the PIN over a trusted transport (`TransportTrust.canAutoFetchPin`, added in `079dcae`), but a later release-gate hardening pass (`fe71162`, reinforced by `53ccc78`) locked the host's `/pairing-pin` endpoint to **loopback only** — so over Tailscale the phone connects from a `100.64.x.x` CGNAT address, the host returned **404**, and the fetched PIN came back empty. The host now mirrors the client's trust model: a new host-side `TransportTrust` classifier serves the PIN when the caller is on loopback **or** when both the caller and the host-side address the caller reached are Tailscale addresses (IPv4 `100.64.0.0/10` or IPv6 `fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48`), i.e. the connection genuinely traversed the tunnel. Requiring the host-side (local) address to also be Tailscale defeats a LAN attacker who spoofs a `100.64.x.x` source while connecting to the host's LAN IP. Plain LAN/internet stay closed (still a scanner-quiet 404), and `/start-pairing` deliberately remains loopback-only because it *creates* a session and discloses its PIN with no host-side consent. PIN session lifetime (120 s) and the brute-force throttle are unchanged. Pure classifier logic is unit-tested. (`TransportTrust`, `HostBootstrapper` `/pairing-pin`.) (RemEx-i9e)
- Pairing, Windows: the **pairing PIN now displays (and pairing succeeds) when remex.agent runs interactively** as the signed-in user, not only when it runs as the LocalSystem service. The local-IPC privileged-command gate (`GETPAIRINGPIN`, `GENERATEPAIRINGPIN`, `STARTPAIRING`, shutdown/restart, …) resolves "the interactive console user" via `WTSQueryUserToken`, which requires the `SE_TCB_NAME` privilege that only LocalSystem holds. When the merged Avalonia host was launched directly by the user (e.g. with the Session-0 service stopped), that lookup failed with error 1314 (`PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD`), the gate could not identify the console user, and it rejected **every** privileged command — so the host UI's 2-second `GETPAIRINGPIN` poll was denied, no PIN was ever shown, and Android pairing failed with "invalid PIN". The gate now falls back to the host process's own identity **only when that process is itself running in the active console session as a real (non-system) logon** — secure because such a host *is* the signed-in console user — while the LocalSystem-service path is unchanged. A host in a non-console session (RDP, fast-user-switch, Session 0) still cannot authorize another session's identity. Regression introduced by the IPC-1 pipe-hardening (`fe71162`). (`LocalIpcServerService.IsConnectedClientInteractiveUser`, `TryGetSelfAsActiveConsoleUserSid`.) (RemEx-dqj)
- Pairing, Windows: **completing a pairing while remex.agent runs interactively no longer throws and fails to persist**. Hardening the paired-clients store (`paired_clients.json`, which holds per-client reconnect secrets) set the file *owner* to LocalSystem; assigning an owner other than yourself requires `SeRestorePrivilege`, which the host lacks when launched by the signed-in user rather than the LocalSystem service — so `SetAccessControl` threw `InvalidOperationException` ("The security identifier is not allowed to be the owner of this object"). That exception type was outside the method's "best-effort" catch, so it escaped `PersistToDisk` → `RegisterClient` and aborted pairing persistence (and failed 11 host tests). The store now takes an *assignable* owner (the current user when not running as LocalSystem, LocalSystem when it is) and grants full control to LocalSystem + Administrators + (interactively) the signed-in user; the best-effort catch now also swallows `InvalidOperationException` so permission-hardening can never block pairing. (`PairedClientRegistry.RestrictStorePermissionsWindows`, `RestrictStorePermissions`.) (RemEx-sgj)
- Android, discovery: the app **no longer crashes ("RemEx has stopped") during mDNS service discovery**, which manifested as the connection "session closed" / crash-restart loop on every connect attempt. `NsdDiscoveryManager.resolveServiceModern` (the Android 14+ `ServiceInfoCallback` path) handed `NsdManager` a single-thread `Executor` and shut it down in `cleanup()` immediately after `unregisterServiceInfoCallback()`. Because unregister is asynchronous, `NsdManager` still posts a final callback (e.g. `onServiceInfoCallbackUnregistered`) to that executor afterward; the executor's default `AbortPolicy` then threw `RejectedExecutionException` on the system `ConnectivityThread` — an uncatchable FATAL that killed the whole process. The executor is now a single-thread `ThreadPoolExecutor` configured with `DiscardPolicy`, so a task posted after shutdown is silently dropped instead of crashing the app. (`NsdDiscoveryManager`.) (RemEx-0ov)
- Android, pairing: **reconnect now authenticates** instead of every request being rejected as unpaired ("pairing handshake required" / host "awaiting proof-of-possession"). The 2.0 host hardening (PAIR-1) requires proof-of-possession on reconnect — the host challenges a returning client with a nonce and expects `HMAC-SHA256(reconnectSecret, nonce)` back — and the host + C# native client implement it, but the **Kotlin layer never persisted the reconnect secret** that pairing returns (`OK:hostId|spki|reconnectSecret`) nor supplied it on connect. So the native client had no secret, `RespondToReconnectChallenge` early-returned, no `ReconnectProof` was sent, and the host rejected every `process_list_request`. (Pre-2.0 a bare clientId authenticated, which is why opening Task Manager — sending the first command — appeared to "do the handshake".) `RemexClientManager` now persists the secret per-host (Tink-encrypted via `PinnedHostStore`) on pairing and includes it as `reconnectSecret` in the native init request on connect. Clients paired before this fix must re-pair once to store the secret. (`RemexClientManager`, `PinnedHostStore`.) (RemEx-xuo)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **the idle RemEx service no longer holds a Desktop Duplication consumer slot** while no client is connected, eliminating the "something watching" contention that blocks Windows Remote Desktop from opening its own duplication session. `DxgiDesktopCapture` previously called `InitializeDevice` + `DuplicateOutput` in its constructor, and the bootstrapper resolved `IHostCapabilitiesProvider` (which depends on `IScreenCaptureService`) eagerly at startup — so the Windows service held a live duplication slot from boot even with zero active clients. DXGI Desktop Duplication allows only one consumer per output; the idle RemEx slot forced Windows RDP and other DXGI consumers to fall back to a lower-quality path or fail. Initialization is now deferred: `DxgiDesktopCapture` opens the D3D device and calls `DuplicateOutput` on the first actual capture call via a lock-guarded `EnsureInitialized()` (called by `TryCapture`, `TryCaptureRaw`, and `TryRecover`). When no remote client is streaming, the slot is never opened. (`DxgiDesktopCapture`.) (RemEx-hmj)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **DXGI Desktop Duplication no longer hard-locks the host to a black screen** (black screen with only the mouse cursor visible; Ctrl+Alt+Del and the Win+Ctrl+Shift+B GPU-reset hotkey both unable to recover). When a display powered off on idle, a fullscreen app flipped, or a secure desktop appeared, `AcquireNextFrame` returned `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST` on every frame, and `TryReinitializeDuplication` re-created the duplication on each loss while only backing off when re-init *threw*. Because the capture methods return the last cached frame on access-loss, the streaming loop counted each as a successful capture and never engaged its own failure-backoff — so `DuplicateOutput` was called at the full stream frame rate (up to 120 Hz) against a display mid power-state transition, which on some NVIDIA drivers wedged DWM (`dwmredir.dll`) and the kernel display driver. Re-initialization is now rate-limited by a new `DuplicationReinitThrottle`: one attempt per backoff window, escalating 1 s → 8 s on consecutive losses and reset by any confirmed-healthy frame (a real frame or a no-change timeout), so a powering-off display is poked a handful of times instead of thousands. Pure throttle logic is unit-tested (8 tests). (`DxgiDesktopCapture`, `DuplicationReinitThrottle`.) (RemEx-crk)
- Remote desktop, Windows: a **frozen capture output now surfaces a clear error instead of silently replaying the last frame** — fixing the "cursor moves but the screen is stuck" failure where the desktop image froze while the separately-streamed cursor kept moving. `DxgiDesktopCapture` returns its cached `_lastFrame` / `_lastRawFrame` on `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST` (display power-off, secure desktop, session disconnect), and `RemoteDesktopHandler` counted *any* non-empty buffer as a successful capture — so a genuinely dead output replayed its last good frame indefinitely and the 5-consecutive-failure coded-error path never tripped. Capture now carries a **liveness signal** (`ScreenCaptureResult.IsLive`, via the new additive `IScreenCaptureService.CaptureRawScreenLiveAsync` / `CaptureScreenLiveAsync`): `true` for a fresh frame *or* a cached frame on a confirmed-healthy output (a static, unchanged screen — so the bandwidth-saving frame reuse is preserved), `false` only when a cached frame is a stale replay after the output was lost or errored. The handler skips stale replays rather than re-sending them, so a sustained freeze advances `consecutiveFailures` and emits the localized `desktop_error` (`capture_unavailable` / `capture_stopped`) the client already handles. The signal is host-internal data flow — **no wire-envelope or `protocolVersion` change** — and the other backends (Linux portal/PipeWire, GDI fallback, test fakes) inherit an always-live default, leaving their behavior unchanged. Seam-level test asserts a frozen output trips the coded error. (`IScreenCaptureService`, `ScreenCaptureResult`, `DxgiDesktopCapture`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RemEx-ltd)
- Remote desktop, Windows: **capture now pauses entirely while the monitor is asleep**, instead of repeatedly poking a powered-off Desktop Duplication output — defense-in-depth on top of the RemEx-crk re-init throttle. A new `WindowsDisplayPowerMonitor` hosts a message-only window on a dedicated pump thread, registers for the `GUID_CONSOLE_DISPLAY_STATE` power-setting notification, and tracks whether the console display is off. `WindowsScreenCaptureService` surfaces this as `IScreenCaptureService.IsDisplayPoweredOff`, and `RemoteDesktopHandler` checks it at the top of the capture loop: while the display is off it idles (500 ms ticks) without calling capture, and clears any in-flight failure counter so a sleeping monitor never raises a misleading "capture stopped" error — the stream stays connected and resumes automatically on power-on. Where the throttle bounded re-init to a few attempts per backoff window, this drives it to zero while the display sleeps. Windows-only; the monitor fails open (display treated as on) if it can't register, and Linux/other backends inherit an always-on default. A seam test asserts a powered-off display pauses without surfacing the capture error a stale replay would otherwise trigger. (`WindowsDisplayPowerMonitor`, `WindowsScreenCaptureService`, `IScreenCaptureService`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RemEx-960)
- Tests, Windows: **running the full test suite no longer black-screens the host** (DWM wedge, cursor-only lock requiring a hard restart). The real `WindowsScreenCaptureService` opens a live DXGI Desktop Duplication session in its constructor, and the host bootstrapper resolves `IHostCapabilitiesProvider` — which takes `IScreenCaptureService` as a constructor dependency — eagerly at startup. So every integration-test fixture that boots a host (`IClassFixture<RemexHostFactory>`, of which only `RemoteDesktopHandlerTests` previously mocked capture) opened and tore down a *real* duplication session; back-to-back across the suite that storm of `DuplicateOutput` calls wedged the NVIDIA driver and DWM (same failure family as RemEx-crk). `RemexHostFactory` now default-registers safe doubles for the OS-disrupting host services on every host it builds, composed so a test's own `WithServices(...)` override still wins (last-wins): a headless `FakeScreenCaptureService` (pure managed code, no DXGI/D3D/GDI), a `NoOpInteractiveSessionGuard`, and a `NoOpSystemCommandService`. The session guard mattered as much as capture: with the "keep session unlocked" feature enabled (the `ProgramData\RemEx\keep-session-unlocked.flag` file present), a desktop-stream test engaged the **real** `WindowsInteractiveSessionGuard`, which runs `tscon.exe <session> /dest:console` and physically locked the developer's Windows session — a screen flash to the login/PIN screen (the same desktop transition that, layered on the DXGI storm, produced the permanent black screen before). No integration test now touches the GPU, locks/disconnects the session, or runs a power command; a guard test asserts all three resolve to the safe doubles. Test-harness only — no production code changed. (`FakeScreenCaptureService`, `NoOpInteractiveSessionGuard`, `NoOpSystemCommandService`, `RemexHostFactory`.) (RemEx-21g)
- Remote desktop input: a **finger drag that maps to the host's top-left (0,0)** no longer silently fails to move the cursor. The host's pointer bridge inferred "absolute vs relative" from `LogicalX != 0 || LogicalY != 0`, so an absolute move to the legitimate `(0,0)` corner was treated as "no coordinate" and dropped. It now prefers an explicit relative delta when present and otherwise uses the absolute logical coordinates directly (including `0,0`); contact-start presses are likewise anchored at the logical position instead of falling back to the current cursor location. (`RemoteDesktopHandler.EnqueuePointerSampleAsInputEvent`.) (RemEx-ubm)
- Remote desktop: an **H.264 decoder that fails to initialize** no longer leaves a permanently-black stream with no feedback. The Android `H264StreamDecoder` previously only logged the failure; it now signals the ViewModel, which marks streaming stopped and triggers a backoff reconnect (surfacing a clear "connection lost" message after retries). (`H264StreamDecoder`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.) (RemEx-x0b)
- Remote desktop: a **display/target switch where the host is slow to produce frames** (StartDesktopStream returns but no frames arrive and no error is emitted) now self-recovers. The Android client arms a frame-arrival watchdog on stream start, reset by every decoded frame, that triggers a reconnect after ~7 s of silence — covering the previously-unrecoverable "silent black screen, no error" case. (`RemoteDesktopViewModel`.) (RemEx-5t4)
- Remote desktop over a VPN: the **local-network discovery permission prompt no longer reappears on a loop over an active connection**. The reconnect self-healing ran mDNS discovery (which triggers Android's "RemEx wants to connect to a device on your local network" prompt) even on Tailscale/CGNAT and public addresses where local multicast can't reach the host; it now runs only for private-LAN/link-local/hostname targets, and manual discovery is suppressed while already connected. (`RemexClientManager`, `ConnectionViewModel`.) (RemEx-fkz)
- Remote desktop: a **stale frame from a previous stream target can no longer ship after a target switch**. The host send loop now drops any buffered frame whose `StreamSerial` no longer matches the session's current serial (host-authoritative), closing the window where the capture thread could swap an old-serial frame in just after the buffer was cleared. (`RemoteDesktopHandler`.) (RemEx-gim)
- Windows autostart: the legacy per-user **HKCU `Run` "RemEx" launch-at-login entry is no longer created and is proactively removed**. The Session-0 service now owns elevated autostart (it spawns the HIGH-integrity GUI host); a lingering Run key started a competing MEDIUM-integrity instance that won the single-instance guard and reintroduced the UIPI input block. `StartupRegistrationService` no longer reports launch-at-login as user-managed on Windows (the GUI toggle hides), and the interactive host removes any legacy entry on startup. Linux XDG autostart is unchanged. (`StartupRegistrationService`, `Program`.) (RemEx-hmk)
- Remote desktop: a **codec switch (H.264 ↔ MJPEG) no longer briefly misroutes frames**. The client now opts into the host's per-frame envelope (`supportsFrameEnvelope`) — a small `RDXF` header tagging each frame with its codec and stream serial — and routes every frame to the decoder that produced it, instead of by a separately-updated "active codec" flag that could lag a frame behind. A JPEG fed to the H.264 decoder (or vice-versa) during the switch window previously produced a silent black gap. Legacy hosts that send untagged frames still work (the client falls back to negotiated-codec routing). Target switch stays disabled, so the stream serial is never reset mid-flight. (`RemoteDesktopFrameEnvelope`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `DesktopFrameEnvelope`.) (RemEx-w5v)
- Remote desktop: host-generated stream errors (capture unavailable/stopped, target unavailable, display-switch unsupported, runtime unavailable) are now **shown in the user's language** instead of English-only. The host tags `errorText` with a stable `DesktopErrorCodes` code (backward-compatible — the English text remains the fallback and the native client forwards the field unchanged), and the Android client maps the code to a localized string (8 languages), substituting the frame count where applicable. Rich Windows capture diagnostics remain untranslated by design. (`DesktopErrorCodes`, `RemoteDesktopHandler`, `RemoteDesktopViewModel`, Android `strings.xml`.) (RemEx-728)
- Remote desktop: the interactive input host is now relaunched into the **currently active console session** rather than being assumed present whenever a host exists in *any* session. Previously a GUI host left behind in a disconnected session — e.g. after a Microsoft "Windows App" (RDP) client disconnected — counted as "already running", so RemEx never spawned a working host in the session that actually owns the input desktop, and remote input stayed dead. The launcher now checks the host is in `WTSGetActiveConsoleSessionId()`'s session. (Partial fix for the RDP-disconnect input failure; the *locked*-session case is addressed by the opt-in keep-session-unlocked feature.) (`InteractiveDesktopHostLauncher`, `WindowsActiveSession`.)
- Remote desktop: the H.264 video no longer **stretches/squishes to fill the screen** — it now letterboxes to preserve the source aspect ratio, so a landscape desktop viewed in portrait looks correct instead of horizontally compressed. A `TextureView` always scales its content to its bounds, so the previous `fillMaxSize` distorted the picture; it is now sized via `Modifier.aspectRatio` to the stream's dimensions (the H.264 equivalent of MJPEG's `ContentScale.Fit`), and `contentRect()` letterboxes for both codecs so input, cursor overlay, and zoom/pan stay aligned. (`RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote desktop: the cursor now renders on a **secondary display positioned left of or above the primary monitor**, where it previously never appeared at all (not even the fallback arrow). The Android client overloaded a negative coordinate as a "cursor hidden" sentinel (`_hostCursorX = -1f`) and `mapHostToLocal` rejected any negative coordinate — but a monitor at a negative virtual-desktop origin has legitimately negative cursor coordinates, so a real cursor there collided with the sentinel and was dropped. Visibility is now carried as its own `hostCursorVisible` flag (the host already reports it via `IsCursorInRegion`), negative coordinates are treated as valid, and both the cursor overlay and the zoom pan-follow gate on the flag. As a result the cursor also correctly shows **only while it is on the streamed display** and hides when it moves onto another monitor. (`RemoteDesktopViewModel`, `RemoteDesktopScreen`.)
- Remote keyboard: typing **emoji and other non-BMP characters** (some CJK-extension glyphs, etc.) through the host now works instead of being garbled or dropped. `WindowsInputSimulationService.TypeText` iterated `foreach (char c in text)` and sent each UTF-16 code unit as its own `KEYEVENTF_UNICODE` down+up, so a surrogate pair went out as high-down, high-up, low-down, low-up. Windows only composes a surrogate pair when the two key-downs arrive consecutively, and the intervening key-up broke it. A surrogate pair is now emitted as both key-downs followed by both key-ups, sent in a single `SendInput` batch (extracted into a platform-neutral, unit-tested `UnicodeTextInput.BuildKeyEventGroups` helper). Ordinary BMP text — virtually all typing — is unchanged.
- Remote desktop input: a stylus/S Pen tap landing exactly on the host's **top-left pixel (0,0)** — the hot corner, Start button, or a maximized window's system menu — is no longer silently dropped, and absolute finger/mouse actions no longer fire a **phantom (0,0) corner click** when the surface isn't ready yet. The Android `mapLocalToHost` previously returned `Offset.Zero` for *both* its three not-ready/degenerate error cases *and* the legitimate host origin, so callers couldn't tell a real corner mapping from a failure. It now returns a nullable `Offset?` (null only on the error cases), and all seven call sites (stylus contact, stylus hover, absolute drag-down, absolute move, absolute tap-click, absolute long-press right-click) skip their action on null instead of sending bogus coordinates. (`RemoteDesktopScreen.kt`)
- Remote mouse/keyboard input now works against **elevated (admin) windows** such as Windows Terminal "Run as administrator". Previously the interactive GUI host — which injects input via `SendInput` — was started by the per-user HKCU Run key at **medium integrity**, and Windows UIPI silently drops input from a lower-integrity process against a higher-integrity foreground window (so control "stopped working" whenever an elevated window was focused, with `SendInput` even reporting success). On Windows, the Session-0 service now launches the GUI host with the signed-in user's **linked full-admin token** (HIGH integrity) via a new `InteractiveDesktopHostLauncher`, using `GetTokenInformation(TokenLinkedToken)` → `DuplicateTokenEx` → `CreateProcessAsUser`. Falls back to the user's default token for standard users / when no linked token exists. (Linux is unaffected — UIPI is Windows-specific.)
- Remote desktop input on secondary monitors: absolute pointer input (S Pen hover, direct-touch taps/clicks) now lands correctly on non-primary displays. The Android client already maps touch/stylus points into **absolute** virtual-desktop coordinates (`mapLocalToHost` adds the host-reported `desktopLeft`/`desktopTop`), but the host's `DispatchInput` re-added the same offset — double-applying it. On a monitor with a non-zero virtual origin this drove the cursor off-screen, so Windows clamped it to the desktop edge (the reported "hover sticks to the far-left of the primary screen on Display 2"). The host no longer re-adds the offset; the now-dead `_desktopLeft`/`_desktopTop` fields were removed. The primary monitor (origin 0,0) was unaffected, which is why the bug only showed on secondary displays.
- Remote desktop cursor now renders the **true native Windows cursor shape** on the Android client, moving smoothly even over a static desktop. The client advertises `supportsCursorState`/`supportsCursorShape` and the host streams the real cursor bitmap (`desktop_cursor_shape`, BGRA pixels, sent on change) plus lightweight live position/visibility (`desktop_cursor_state`); the client decodes the bitmap and draws it at the hotspot. Host-side cursor compositing is now gated off whenever the client supports shapes (`_drawCursor = config.DrawCursor && !SupportsCursorShape`). This fixes two regressions from the earlier host-composite-into-frame approach: the cursor intermittently disappearing, and the cursor appearing frozen while dragging over a static screen — both caused by the host only re-encoding frames when desktop pixels change, so a mouse-only move never refreshed the composited cursor. The end-to-end path required new JNI callbacks (`onDesktopCursorState`/`onDesktopCursorShape`) across the NativeAOT boundary. Legacy hosts/clients fall back to the generic-arrow overlay.
- Android build: the Gradle native-library sync and verify tasks (`syncRemexCore{Debug,Release}So`, `verifyRemexCoreIn{Debug,Release}Apk`) now locate `libRemexCore.so` under the repo-wide `artifacts/` output layout in addition to the legacy per-project `bin/` layout. Since `Directory.Build.props` enables `UseArtifactsOutput`, the NativeAOT output moved to `artifacts/bin/remex.core/<config>_net10.0-android_android-arm64/native/`, which the hardcoded `bin/` paths could no longer find — causing `Published Release libRemexCore.so not found` and a failed Android build even though the `.so` built successfully.
- Android build: `verifyRemexCoreIn{Debug,Release}Apk` no longer fails task-property validation on a fresh/clean build.
